# RFC-BPR-0053–0061 execution ledger

## Mandate, assignment and baselines

User mandate, 2026-09-29: "identify available work and implement it, work
unattended, if there are attended reviews at the end of a milestone do
everything up to that and then park the reviews until I'm ready for a
session", amended with "remote stations are available for windows/linux work,
push and pull as needed". Run `bpr-cycles-20260929`, mandate revision `1`,
strategy `coordinator-implementer` recipe revision 2. The macOS arm64
`rfc-implementer` owns the `intake` milestone (BPR-1143) and continues into
`domain` on local `main`.

- Execution base: `22d1841661b15a160e694b557c506cdbae66bb2a` (local `main`,
  equal to `origin/main` at intake). The only dirty path was the unrelated
  `.mask.lock.json`, which this work never stages.
- Historical import: pre-import `main`
  `8466534f96f254d88fed412a587188b7b45f2580`, import commit
  `fc4b39e8c3a54ecf931d74071d7793e5e4112496`, recorded in the
  [original import receipt](../rfcs/assets/bpr-review-cycles-v1.LIVE_IMPORT.md).
  That remains the base for the complete imported-pack diff at closeout.
- [Plan](../rfcs/plans/bpr-review-cycles-v1/milestone-plan.json) binding for
  `intake`: definition `sha256:a78d30d7…71f6af`, obligation `BPR-1143:*`,
  exits `intake:exit:1` and `intake:exit:2`; structural valid, references
  resolved, semantic coverage an engineering judgment.

No live GitHub mutation, model invocation, skill installation or remote push
beyond landing `main` is authorized by this intake. All 90 product criteria
remain **Not run**.

## Intake reconciliation

The 2026-09-25 import already allocated and adopted the pack; this milestone
re-checks it against the execution base and records live ownership. No IDs are
allocated for the pack and the counter is not rewound.

| Check | Result at the execution base |
|---|---|
| Archive | `python3 -B scripts/verify_pack.py` in the retained archive passes: 114 manifest files, 9 RFCs, 52 cards, 11 milestones, 90 requirements, 246 local links. `PACK-MANIFEST.json` SHA-256 `8c390cae…536d4f` and source `milestone-plan.json` `8640765f…59db` equal the import record. No archive or plan file changed after fc4b39e8c. |
| RFC identities | RFC-BPR-0053–0061 exist once each at canonical paths, all Proposed, each with its index row. |
| Card identities | [id-map.json](../rfcs/plans/bpr-review-cycles-v1/id-map.json) maps 52 aliases to 52 distinct IDs equal to the [reservation](../issues/_reservations/BPR-1143--BPR-1194--082f740c9e73.json) BPR-1143–1194. Every live card exists exactly once and carries its matching `pack_alias`. Each RFC has exactly one closeout card (BPR-1186–1194). `NEXT_ID` is past the block. |
| Plan | Native `milestone_plan.py check` finds the adopted plan structurally valid: 11 milestones, 52 work items, the authored groups and prerequisites intact. [source.json](../rfcs/plans/bpr-review-cycles-v1/source.json) is the adoption receipt for [import-proposal.json](../rfcs/plans/bpr-review-cycles-v1/import-proposal.json) (`e739c787…19b3`). |
| Source drift | [Intake audit](RFC-BPR-0053-0061.intake-audit.json): of the 30 baseline files, 29 matched at the pre-import head (the RFC-0052 working-tree header differed) and 11 match at the execution base; 19 differ, none missing. |

The 19 drifted baseline files are the architecture, security and review
documents, RFC-BPR-0023 and RFC-BPR-0052, the board README and project config,
both build files, `review_round_trip.hpp`, `workbench_interaction.hpp`,
`review_ledger_port.hpp` and `workbench_application_host.hpp`. They moved with
the review-tour, pr-tour CLI, RFC-0022/0023 and RFC-0052 work landed since
2026-09-25. None removes a seam this pack names; each card reads the live file,
not the snapshot.

## Earlier proposals that have landed

- **RFC-BPR-0023** (private review threads) is now Auto-Verified. The import
  record called its scope deferred; it is no longer. Review Cycles' private
  notes and provenance must extend the landed `private_review` core model and
  its store, not add a second thread model.
- **RFC-BPR-0039–0043** (review tours) are Auto-Verified.
- **RFC-BPR-0044–0046** (headless tour CLI, shared drafts, thin skill) are
  Proposed with 8, 9 and 4 cards Done. The `bpr` executable, the non-GUI tour
  runtime and the profile-owner channel exist; the local CLI/owner seam is
  therefore real and reusable, not only proposed.
- **RFC-BPR-0047–0051** (Conventional Comments) are Proposed with no card
  Done. No conventions runtime exists; cycles must not assume one.
- **RFC-BPR-0052** (workbench scaffolding) is Proposed with 17 cards Done and
  its chrome and tab semantics in the tree. RFC-BPR-0036 (native Markdown) is
  Proposed.

## Domain milestone

Bound with intake accepted: definition `sha256:10fd28db…07324c`, obligations
BPR-1144–1147, exits `domain:exit:1` and `domain:exit:2`.

### BPR-1144: cycle identities and transitions

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle.hpp`,
`src/core/src/review_cycle.cpp`, `tests/core/review_cycle_tests.cpp`, their
two CMake registrations, and the
[domain model](../architecture/domain-and-state-model.md#review-cycle-identities-rfc-bpr-0053)
section. No existing type changed; `pull_request_authority` is held whole as
one alternative of `review_subject`.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle$"` passes (macOS arm64,
Homebrew LLVM dev preset), and the same test passes under the `core-only`
preset with Apple clang. It covers: a cycle created with no pull request,
remote, commit, account or agent; uncaptured versus an empty complete R0;
zero profile/incarnation, foreign profile, foreign incarnation, stale
generation and foreign-cycle references refused; cycle and revision counter
exhaustion; stale expected revisions; archive/unarchive; identical recapture
as a no-op versus a deliberate handoff observation with the same bytes;
partial capture, narrowed scope and unavailable paths classified unknown
rather than deleted; canonical scope paths; frozen rounds reused only for the
same draft revision and digest; recipient claims kept apart from reviewer
decisions. `architecture_boundaries` and `architecture_boundary_contract`
pass with the new core files, and clang-tidy passes on them.

Limits: RC53-01's "reopen" and RC53-02's "persist" need the store; BPR-1145
supplies that evidence. RC53-03's application ports are BPR-1146's.

The `core-only` preset does not build at this base for an unrelated reason:
`tests/core/agent_profile_tests.cpp:138` asserts a layout size that Apple
libc++ does not produce (152 against 88). Only the review-cycle target was
built there.

### BPR-1147: local scope and disclosure policy primitives

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_policy.hpp`,
`src/core/src/review_cycle_policy.cpp`, `tests/core/review_cycle_policy_tests.cpp`,
their CMake registrations, and the Review Cycles section of the
[threat model](../architecture/security-threat-model.md#review-cycles).

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle_policy$"` passes. Its
31-row policy table covers an unknown-origin folder (local capture and OS
destinations allowed, external retrieval and adapters unconfigured), known
enterprise data (one configured allowance opens only that channel; a denial
closes it and does not leak to other classifications), denied file, terminal
and external-read channels, the response-only grant (read and append allowed;
verify, export, publish, capture and implementation instruction reviewer-only),
imported content refused for every capability even with a grant attached, and
publication refused until its plan is confirmed. Further checks bind a grant
to one subject, round and digest, honor revocation, refuse a consumer-chosen
export channel, keep classification from downgrading on reopen, and read
"verified", "approved" and command-bearing report text as no claim.
`architecture_boundaries`, `architecture_boundary_contract` and tidy pass.

Limits: these are core decisions. Wiring them into the owner channel, the CLI,
export and adapters belongs to the later milestones that add those surfaces;
RC60-04 and RC60-05 stay open for those surfaces until then.

### BPR-1145: cycle history, drafts, pins and operation receipts

Owned paths: `src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_store.hpp`,
`src/adapters/sqlite/include/bpr/adapters/sqlite/review_cycle_store.hpp`,
`src/adapters/sqlite/src/review_cycle_store.cpp`,
`tests/sqlite_contract/review_cycle_storage_tests.cpp`, their CMake
registrations; the `review-cycles.sqlite3` entry in the profile-purge inventory
(`src/adapters/sqlite/src/release_maintenance_files.cpp` and its test) and in
[storage and diagnostics](../development/storage-and-diagnostics.md); the store
paragraph in the
[domain model](../architecture/domain-and-state-model.md#review-cycle-identities-rfc-bpr-0053);
and a total scope-byte bound (256 KiB) added to the BPR-1144 core check so a
scope fits one stored value. No existing pull-request schema or record changed.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^sqlite_review_cycle_storage$"` passes on macOS
arm64. Against the production SQLite store it covers: create, empty R0 capture
and reopen through a new store object with the same incarnation; a second
profile refused on the same catalog; a foreign incarnation refused; identical
retries replayed, a reused operation refused, refused requests leaving no
receipt; stale expected revisions refused with the current state; captures
refused when their manifest is missing or is not the capture's material;
corrupt and missing object bytes reported rather than substituted; draft
revisions, freeze replay, reuse for the same draft revision and payload, a
different payload refused, and a successor round leaving the first unchanged;
a 64-byte budget refusing an install with the inventory, `reclaim` removing
only the unreferenced object, and pinned evidence still readable; a stale
removal preview refused, removal releasing only the cycle's own bytes, and the
removed identity not reissued; a neighbouring store's bytes unchanged and an
unexpected schema refused. The crash course forks a writer and delivers a real
SIGKILL at `COMMIT`, before and after it completes, for object install,
capture and freeze: each reopens with the whole record or none, never a
partial round, and the same operation then completes once or replays.
A mutated expectation was checked to fail. `sqlite_release_maintenance_files`
passes with the cycle database and sidecar purged; `sqlite_review_tour_storage`,
both architecture-boundary tests, tidy and clang-format pass.

Owed: the SIGKILL course is POSIX-only, as in the other SQLite contracts. A
Linux station should run
`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^sqlite_review_cycle_storage$|^sqlite_release_maintenance_files$" --output-on-failure`
and a Windows station
`ctest --test-dir build\dev -R "^sqlite_review_cycle_storage$|^sqlite_release_maintenance_files$" --output-on-failure`
after building those two targets (`test_review_cycle_storage`,
`test_release_maintenance_files`). `live_mutation_isolation` needs every
test executable built and was not run in this targeted build.

### BPR-1146: local runtime and material bridge

Owned paths: `src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_runtime.hpp`,
`src/application/src/review_cycle_runtime.cpp`,
`tests/sqlite_contract/review_cycle_runtime_tests.cpp`,
`tests/sqlite_contract/review_cycle_dependency_closure.cmake`, their CMake
registrations, and the runtime paragraph in the
[domain model](../architecture/domain-and-state-model.md#review-cycle-identities-rfc-bpr-0053).

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^sqlite_review_cycle_runtime$|^review_cycle_dependency_closure$"`
passes on macOS arm64. The runtime opens, creates, captures an empty R0 and
reopens with only a profile root and executors. Work queued for one selection
completes as `cancelled` after a switch; a pull-request selection is refused
for load and capture; switching back reads the untouched cycle. On a root
holding a pull-request tour draft, creating a cycle leaves the tour database
bytes identical, the draft reads back exactly, and the cycle list contains only
the cycle created (RC53-07). The bridge serves a cycle object by subject,
revision and recorded object; refuses another catalog's subject, an object the
revision did not record, and any pull-request blob when no pull-request port is
composed; reports an unknown revision as missing and an oversize read as too
large; and with a pull-request port composed, passes pull-request requests
through unchanged while cycle requests never reach it. The dependency check
computes the runtime executable's link closure from the target graph at
configure time: `better_pull_review_{adapter_sqlite,application,sqlite3,platform,core}`
and `Threads::Threads`, with no agent, MCP, AI, GitHub, Git, Qt or composition
target; a closure containing `better_pull_review_adapter_agent` was checked to
fail.

Remaining packaging couplings, for BPR-1179: the desktop composition
(`src/app/CMakeLists.txt`, `better_pull_review_composition`) links
`AdapterAI`, `AdapterAgent` and `AdapterMcp` unconditionally in both its Qt
and non-Qt forms; the application library compiles its agent hosts
(`agent_*` sources) into the same static library as the cycle runtime, so an
agent-free build must split them; there is no `BPR_BUILD_AGENT_INTEGRATIONS`
switch yet. Material bridging stops at the stored manifest object: per-file
material inside a manifest waits for the capture format of BPR-1149, and
loading cycle comparisons into the workbench is BPR-1151.

### Domain milestone result

All four cards are Done. `domain:exit:1`: the core owns cycle identities,
transitions and the disclosure decision; the profile-private store owns
durable records, receipts and pins; the runtime and bridge compose them with
no provider, account or agent. `domain:exit:2`: each owned row above has direct
evidence with its limits stated; shared rows (RC53-01/02/03, RC53-10, RC60-04/05)
record only this milestone's share, and RFC closeout BPR-1186/BPR-1193 judges
them whole. No row requires human judgment at this milestone. Owed, not
gating: the Linux and Windows runs of the two SQLite contracts named on
BPR-1145.

Integration pass at the BPR-1146 candidate: `ctest --test-dir build/dev -j6 -R
"^sqlite_|^core_|^architecture_boundar|^test_network_isolation_manifest$|^review_cycle|^live_mutation_isolation_contract$"`
passed 77 of 77. The full dev build stops only on the two failures recorded at
baseline on BPR-1211 and BPR-1214 (`test_scoped_writing_agent` compiled as
C++98 by this checkout's Apple clang, and the `agent_profile_tests.cpp` layout
assertion); neither file changed here.

Next eligible milestone: `snapshots` (BPR-1148–1152), whose prerequisite is
this milestone.

### BPR-1148: bind local folders, files and Git workspaces

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_workspace.hpp`,
`src/core/src/review_cycle_workspace.cpp`,
`src/core/src/review_cycle_path_folding.cpp` (Unicode 16 decomposition and
lowercase tables generated from Python's `unicodedata`), the binding additions
to `review_cycle.hpp`/`review_cycle.cpp` (`bind_cycle_workspace`,
`cycle_capture_observation::WorkspaceVersion`, refusals `invalid_workspace`,
`unconfirmed`, `stale_workspace`; `atomic_snapshot` renamed `immutable_source`
to match SNAPSHOTS.md, stored value unchanged),
`src/application/include/bpr/application/workspace_source_port.hpp`, the new
`src/adapters/workspace/` adapter (`better_pull_review_adapter_workspace`,
links the application library only), the SQLite store's schema 2 (a
`bindings` table and `revisions.workspace_version`, with in-place migration of
schema 1), `tests/core/review_cycle_workspace_tests.cpp`,
`tests/workspace_contract/`, the storage-test additions, and the domain-model,
threat-model and AGENTS.md adapter entries.

Commands on macOS arm64 (APFS, case- and normalization-insensitive), dev
preset: `ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle|^workspace_source$|^sqlite_review_cycle|^review_cycle_dependency|^architecture_boundar|^conditional_branch"`
passes; the three `core_review_cycle*` tests also pass under `core-only`.
clang-format and clang-tidy (`--warnings-as-errors`) are clean on the touched
translation units.

- RC54-01: `workspace_source` opens a plain folder, a single file (identified
  by its directory, so an atomic-save replacement keeps the root), a Git
  checkout with no commit and only uncommitted content, and a linked worktree
  made by the build's Git (`git worktree add`), whose repository identity
  equals the main checkout's and whose worktree identity differs. The test runs
  network-isolated; nothing needs an account or remote.
- RC54-03: unsafe descendant paths (`..`, absolute, drive, empty and dot
  segments, `.git`) are refused before any open; a directory link, a file link
  and a directory swapped for a link after it was read are refused as
  `escape`; the link target is kept as data. A root renamed away and replaced
  at its location is reported `replaced`, and the held session keeps reading
  the original root, not the impostor.
- RC54-10: two same-named checkouts with identical bytes have different root
  identities; binding one and presenting the other is `replaced`, and the core
  refuses the rebind without confirmation. A moved root keeps its identity but
  still needs a confirmed rebind; rebind over captured material also needs the
  material check, which is recorded. Captures naming a stale binding version
  are refused in the core and through the store. Imported projects resolve only
  through an explicit mapping. The binding survives reopen exactly.
- Collisions: the core flags case-fold and normalization pairs (Latin, Greek,
  Cyrillic; other scripts compare by exact bytes, which can miss a collision
  but never merges one). On this APFS volume both spellings resolve to one
  file, so the native row exercised only byte preservation; the two-file rows
  run on a case-sensitive Linux volume.

Owed native runs, not gating this card's macOS evidence:
Linux `ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^workspace_source$|^core_review_cycle_workspace$|^sqlite_review_cycle_storage$" --output-on-failure`
(case- and normalization-sensitive rows, `st_mtim`/`st_ctim` branch);
Windows `ctest --test-dir build\dev -R "^workspace_source$|^core_review_cycle_workspace$|^sqlite_review_cycle_storage$" --output-on-failure`
(the Win32 branch was written without a Windows compiler here: reparse-point
refusal, held-root rename refusal, WTF-8 names; symbolic-link rows need the
symlink privilege and report themselves as not run without it).

### BPR-1150: bytes, modes and special files

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_material.hpp`,
`src/core/src/review_cycle_material.cpp`, the streaming `sha256_stream` added
to `bpr/core/sha256.hpp` (the one-shot `sha256` now uses it),
`tests/core/review_cycle_material_tests.cpp`, the port's optional execute bit
(`workspace_entry_observation::Executable`, absent on Windows) and the
BPR-1150 rows of `tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_capture_tests.cpp`.

`core_review_cycle_material` classifies a byte-golden corpus (empty, LF, CRLF,
CR, mixed, trailing CR, no final newline, UTF-8 BOM, UTF-16 LE/BE marks,
overlong, surrogate, truncated and Latin-1 bytes, NUL binary, multibyte) and
gets the same profile when the bytes arrive one at a time; recognizes an LFS
pointer and not materialized content; maps byte offsets to columns only at
scalar boundaries; and streams the same SHA-256 as the one-shot function.
`review_cycle_capture` (macOS arm64) captures the corpus from disk and reads
every retained object back byte-for-byte equal to the original file, with the
raw hash equal to SHA-256 of those bytes; CRLF, BOM and a missing final newline
are kept; UTF-16 and invalid UTF-8 are file-level only; the execute bit maps to
100755/100644 and removing it alone produces new material; a link is kept as
its target bytes and never followed; a FIFO is visible as unsupported; a file
over the request's budget is visible as too large and not read. In a Git root
with index version 4, a submodule gitlink is a boundary carrying its staged
commit and is not recursed, and `*.bin filter=lfs` marks both a pointer blob
(recognized) and materialized bytes (kept as read, never cleaned) as
filter-managed.

Limits: case/normalization collisions are the core rule from BPR-1148; the
Windows row that a new file gets no invented execute bit is written but owed
to the Windows station, as are Windows link targets (reported unsupported).
Encodings other than UTF-8 are not decoded at all, by design.

### BPR-1149: scoped quiescent capture

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_git.hpp` and `.cpp`
(index v2-v4, `extensions.objectFormat`, `HEAD`, loose and packed refs),
`review_cycle_ignore.hpp`/`.cpp` (Git wildcard matching, `.gitignore`
precedence, the `filter` attribute, the disclosed default exclusion set),
`review_cycle_manifest.hpp`/`.cpp` (canonical manifest, pass comparison,
retry bound), `src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_capture.hpp`
and `src/application/src/review_cycle_capture.cpp`,
`review_cycle_runtime::capture_workspace`, the store's content pins
(`cycle_capture_commit::Content`, `revision_objects` in schema 2,
`revision_holds`), `tests/core/review_cycle_manifest_tests.cpp`,
`tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_capture_tests.cpp`, and the
domain-model and threat-model paragraphs.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle|^review_cycle_capture$|^workspace_source$|^sqlite_review_cycle|^architecture_boundar|^conditional_branch"`
passes on macOS arm64; the core tests also pass under `core-only`; clang-tidy
and clang-format are clean on the touched units. A mutation that skipped the
pass comparison made `review_cycle_capture` fail at "retried once, then
stable".

- RC54-02: a Git checkout with a hook for every relevant event, a
  `core.fsmonitor` command, `filter.*.clean/smudge/process` and
  `diff.*.textconv` drivers pointing at a canary script, `* filter=evil`
  attributes and a promisor remote is captured with dirty, staged-only,
  untracked and ignored content. The whole tree including `.git` (index,
  refs, config, objects) hashes identically before and after, and the canary
  never runs. Working bytes are captured, not `HEAD` or the index; the staged
  object identity is kept separately and equals `git rev-parse :src/b.txt`.
- RC54-04: one change between passes is retried once and the stable bytes are
  recorded; a writer that never stops exhausts exactly three retries and
  reports `unstable` naming the path, recording nothing; a file appearing
  mid-capture is caught by re-enumeration; the only quality produced is
  `observed_stable`. Cancellation records no revision and leaves only
  unreferenced objects that `reclaim` removes without touching pinned ones.
- RC54-05: a narrowed scope reads nothing outside it; widening the scope makes
  scope version 2, and a path first seen there compares as unknown, not added;
  a tracked file removed from the working tree is an explicit deletion, an
  untracked one is absent from a complete capture and unknown in a partial
  one; a skip-worktree (sparse) entry is visible, unread, not a deletion and
  never fetched; ignored, default-set and requested exclusions are recorded
  with their reasons; a scope path naming nothing is `missing_scope`.
- The runtime captures the selected cycle and refuses a pull-request
  selection; the rebind material check compares a candidate checkout with the
  latest revision and installs nothing.

Limits and owed runs: the reviewer's global excludes file is not read (such
paths stay visible as untracked); split indexes are flagged and make the
capture partial; POSIX character classes in ignore patterns are unsupported;
Windows runs `ctest --test-dir build\dev -R "^review_cycle_capture$|^core_review_cycle_(material|manifest)$" --output-on-failure`
and Linux runs `ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^review_cycle_capture$|^core_review_cycle_(material|manifest)$" --output-on-failure`
are owed. Remembered per-cycle exclusions are supplied per request and
recorded in each manifest; storing them on the cycle waits for the capture
surface in the authoring milestone.

### BPR-1151: full source and exact cycle comparisons

Owned paths: the subject-neutral entry to the shared diff engine
(`material_comparison_key`, `material_alignment` and `validate_alignment` in
`bpr/core/diff_model.hpp`; `material_diff_input`, `computed_material_diff`,
`compute_material_diff` and `validate_computed_material_diff` in
`bpr/core/diff_compute.hpp`; the alignment and computed-diff rules in
`diff_model.cpp` and `diff_compute.cpp` are now templates shared by both
comparison kinds, with the pull-request path unchanged),
`src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_comparison.hpp` and `.cpp`,
`src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_comparison.hpp` and
`.cpp`, `review_material_bridge.hpp` (the bridge declarations moved out of
`review_cycle_runtime.hpp`, which still includes it; the bridge now serves any
object the named revision pinned), `review_cycle_runtime::compare_selected`
and `compare_file_selected`, the store's `install_all` (one transaction per
batch of content), read buffers sized to the file in the workspace adapter,
`tests/core/review_cycle_comparison_tests.cpp`,
`tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_comparison_tests.cpp`,
`review_cycle_scale_bench.cpp`, the shared `cycle_course.hpp`, and the
domain-model paragraph.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_|^review_cycle|^sqlite_review_cycle|^workspace_source$|^application_(anonymous_diff_authority|diff_host|workbench_host|workbench_snapshot_observation)$|^qt_diff_workbench_scale_100$|^qt_epoch_comparison_scale_100$|^qt_diff_link_map_scale_100$|^architecture_boundar|network_isolation"`
passes on macOS arm64, 76 tests (all 52 core tests, so the templated diff engine keeps
every pull-request diff rule). The full dev build compiles every target except
the pre-existing `test_scoped_writing_agent` (BPR-1211).

- RC57-02 (this card's share): full source resolves to one revision and is
  served as its exact retained bytes, not a diff; all-cycle, since-checkpoint
  and this-round views resolve to the intended pair; an explicit pair and a
  round with a named result never retarget after a newer capture and only
  signal it; semantic views move on explicit refresh. The native tab surface
  is BPR-1163's.
- RC54-09 (with BPR-1152): after the workspace file is edited and the folder
  deleted, and after a restart with a new store object, full source reads the
  captured bytes; a corrupted chunk reads as `corrupt` and a removed object as
  `missing`, each with no bytes and no diff, never the live file.
- RC54-08 (this card's share; native latency is BPR-1184's): comparison pages
  are bounded to 256 paths and resumable, cancellation returns `cancelled`,
  every read runs on the effect lane, and decoded manifests are cached two at
  a time. A late file completion for a superseded selection completes
  `cancelled` in the runtime and is dropped by the core view state, which
  keeps the previous content while newer material loads. Measured with
  `build/cycle-bench/tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_scale --files 100000 --lines 10`
  (Release, Apple M4 Pro, 48 GiB, macOS 26.6.2, APFS; 100,000 files, 1,000,000
  lines, 25.9 MB): initial capture 15.5 s, unchanged recapture 8.9 s,
  recapture with 1,000 changed files 10.0 s, comparison page p50 0.46 ms / p95
  0.55 ms over 391 pages, file diff p50 2.5 ms / p95 3.3 ms over 100 files,
  peak resident 99 MiB. Capture is cancellable per file on the effect lane,
  never on the UI thread; its per-file cost is filesystem system time
  (descendant resolution reopens each directory chain), a known optimization
  left for the qualification milestone.
- Shared engine: `compute_material_diff` gives the same alignment rules and
  validation as `compute_file_diff`, under a cycle-derived identity; binary,
  missing-side and invalid-key cases behave as for pull requests; a widened
  scope or an unavailable side yields no diff rather than an invented
  addition.

Owed: Linux and Windows runs of
`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle_comparison$|^review_cycle_comparison$|^review_cycle_scale$" --output-on-failure`
(Windows: `build\dev`). The pre-existing `bugprone-std-exception-baseclass`
finding in `diff_compute.cpp` (`primary_index_limit`, from 901a9c069) is not
this card's; tidy is otherwise clean on the touched units.

### BPR-1152: capture recovery and retained-material budgets

Owned paths: `explain_capture` and `cycle_capture_explanation` in
`bpr/core/review_cycle.hpp`/`.cpp` (with a core test in
`tests/core/review_cycle_tests.cpp`), the capture outcome's explanation, the
store's verification and repair of an existing object on install and its
single-sum budget check, and `tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_recovery_tests.cpp`.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle|^review_cycle|^sqlite_review_cycle|^workspace_source$|network_isolation|^architecture_boundar"`
passes on macOS arm64 (23 tests); tidy and clang-format are clean on the
touched units.

- RC53-04 (capture share): a forked writer receives SIGKILL before and after
  the commit of the content install, the manifest install and the revision
  record (six kills). Each time the reopened cycle is exactly as before, or
  holds the whole new revision; the earlier revision reads intact; retrying
  the same operation completes once or replays; exactly one new revision
  exists and reads fully; `reclaim` leaves no unreferenced candidate. Drafts
  and rounds were covered on BPR-1145.
- RC54-04 (with BPR-1149): a capture that settled after retries is marked for
  attention with its retry count; an ordinary stable capture raises nothing;
  no path claims atomicity (only `immutable_source` could, and no capture
  produces it); retry exhaustion stays `unstable` with the moving paths.
- RC54-09 (with BPR-1151): a corrupted pin reads `corrupt` with no bytes and
  is never replaced by the live file; recapturing the same bytes repairs it
  with bytes that hash to its identity, while damage no capture can re-verify
  stays reported.
- Budgets and failures: a child whose file-size limit equals the store's size
  (a full disk as SQLite sees it) is refused as a store failure and the cycle
  is unchanged, then succeeds once space returns; a 64 KiB budget refuses a
  128 KiB addition with the inventory, reclaim keeps every pin, the refusal
  repeats rather than evicting, and an explicitly larger budget admits it; a
  scope path naming nothing is unavailable, not deleted, and noted.

Limits and owed runs: the kill and full-disk courses are POSIX-only (fork,
SIGKILL, RLIMIT_FSIZE); Linux should run
`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^review_cycle_recovery$|^core_review_cycle$" --output-on-failure`
and Windows `ctest --test-dir build\dev -R "^review_cycle_recovery$|^core_review_cycle$" --output-on-failure`
(which runs the corruption, quota and explanation rows only). Verifying
existing objects is included in the 100,000-file measurement on BPR-1151.

### BPR-1153: source-attached private feedback and related locations

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/private_thread_lineage.hpp` and `.cpp`
(the shared slice moved out of `private_review.hpp`, which now includes it and
uses its rules), `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_feedback.hpp` and
`.cpp`, `valid_cycle_path` and six feedback refusals in `review_cycle.hpp`,
`src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_feedback.hpp` and `.cpp`,
the feedback methods of `review_cycle_store` and `review_cycle_runtime`, the
SQLite store's schema 3 (`feedback`, `feedback_revisions`; schemas 1 and 2
migrate in place; removal counts and deletes feedback),
`tests/core/review_cycle_feedback_tests.cpp`,
`tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_feedback_tests.cpp`, the schema-2
migration row in `review_cycle_storage_tests.cpp`, the
`review_cycle_feedback` isolation pin, and the domain-model paragraph.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle_feedback$|^core_private_review$|^review_cycle_feedback$|^sqlite_review_cycle|^core_review_cycle|^review_cycle_|^workspace_source$|^architecture_boundar|network_isolation_manifest"`
passes on macOS arm64 (dev preset). clang-format and clang-tidy
(`--warnings-as-errors`) are clean on the touched translation units. Two
mutations were checked to fail: choosing the first of repeated snippets fails
`core_review_cycle_feedback` at "repeated text is ambiguous", and skipping the
retained-byte check fails `review_cycle_feedback` at "bytes the revision does
not hold".

- RC55-01 (this card's share): through the production runtime and store, one
  discussion anchors a range in `src/parser.cpp` with a related range in
  `src/lexer.cpp`, from full-source retained bytes, with only a body; file,
  module and cycle notes need no range and carry no line; notes on an older
  revision after newer captures and on a file unchanged across four captures
  are accepted. No title, label, severity, recipient or model is asked for.
  The native authoring surface is BPR-1156/BPR-1157's.
- RC55-02: the original attachment (revision, path, content digest, half-open
  byte range, exact bytes, two lines of context) is never rewritten; against
  later captures it observes `exact_material`, `exact_mapped` (moved down,
  range 49–61), `ambiguous` (copied into two new contexts, two candidates, no
  range), `deleted`, and `unavailable` for unknown, out-of-scope or unread
  paths; rebinding appends a reference that names what it rebinds. A note
  naming bytes only the live workspace holds, another file's digest, a path
  the revision lacks, or forged bytes is refused. Authoring leaves every
  workspace byte unchanged.
- RC55-10: cycle threads use `private_thread_lineage` (header rules,
  completion, derivation, digests, bounds and the four provenance classes),
  and `core_private_review` passes unchanged on the refactored pull-request
  thread code, whose actors map onto the same provenance classes. RFC-BPR-0023
  has since landed Auto-Verified; this adapts its live component and changes
  none of its pull-request scope, binding, store, promotion or composition.
  Threads persist with lineage and reopen through a new store object; 70
  threads and 74 revisions page within 64 entries and 512 KiB.

Limits: labels are BPR-1154's; suggestions BPR-1155's. The Windows and Linux
runs are owed: `ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle_feedback$|^core_private_review$|^review_cycle_feedback$|^sqlite_review_cycle_storage$" --output-on-failure`
(Windows: `build\dev`, plus `test_network_isolation_manifest` for the new
`review_cycle_feedback` pin).

### BPR-1155: exact suggestion records and conflict decisions

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_suggestion.hpp` and
`.cpp`, `cycle_feedback_thread::NextSuggestion`, the suggestion helpers of
`application/review_cycle_feedback` (preview, preflight, applied
confirmation), the suggestion methods of `review_cycle_store` and
`review_cycle_runtime`, the SQLite `suggestions` table (schema 3 is not yet
published, so it gained the table and `feedback.next_suggestion` in place),
`tests/core/review_cycle_suggestion_tests.cpp`,
`tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_suggestion_tests.cpp`, the shared
`tests/workspace_contract/cycle_session.hpp` (moved out of the feedback
contract), the `review_cycle_suggestion` isolation pin and the domain-model
paragraph. The suggestion header was drafted before the claim commit
(`e5688006c` was the base); no product file was committed before it.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^review_cycle_suggestion$|^review_cycle_feedback$|^core_review_cycle|^sqlite_review_cycle|^core_private_review$"`
passes on macOS arm64; clang-format and clang-tidy are clean on the touched
units. Three mutations were checked to fail: applying in ascending order
(`core_review_cycle_suggestion`, "original-coordinate application"), skipping
the whole-blob precondition ("nothing applied"), and accepting any changed file
as applied (`review_cycle_suggestion`, "similar text is not an application").

- RC55-04: a selection of retained R1 bytes with CRLF, a tab and a two-byte
  scalar becomes a suggestion whose original and replacement bytes, range
  (20–28) and intent read back exactly after reopen; insertion (empty range)
  and deletion (empty replacement) are the empty sides; an exact edit that
  changes nothing is refused; the preview returns the whole after file and
  its lines (3 before, 3–4 after) from the retained original, in memory.
- RC55-05: a set of three edits of different lengths given out of order
  applies in original coordinates; overlapping, contained and identical
  ranges, two insertions at one boundary and an insertion at either end of a
  replaced range are conflicts and apply nothing; a file changed elsewhere
  whose range still reads the same is `stale_blob`; a set naming two files is
  refused; revalidation binds a successor only to a range holding exactly the
  original bytes.
- RC55-06: suggesting, previewing and preflighting leave every workspace byte
  and the store's retained objects unchanged. A similar edit in a later
  capture is unknown and refused as an application; the exact result is
  recorded `observed_already_applied` with its revision, preflight then
  reports `already_applied` rather than applying it again, and the suggestion
  takes no further revision. Illustrative edits are never applied.

Limits: no exact application to a workspace exists or is offered; preflight
results stay in memory for review and export. Owed native runs:
`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle_suggestion$|^review_cycle_suggestion$|^review_cycle_feedback$|^sqlite_review_cycle_storage$" --output-on-failure`
on Linux, and on Windows with `build\dev` plus `test_network_isolation_manifest`.

### BPR-1154: labels and provenance without rewriting text

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_disclosure.hpp` and
`.cpp`, `tests/core/review_cycle_disclosure_tests.cpp`, the label row of
`tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_feedback_tests.cpp`,
`check_labelled_bodies` in `tests/adapters_qt/markdown_body_view_tests.cpp`,
and the domain-model paragraph. Provenance records and the shared seam are
BPR-1153's.

Compatibility decision: the card asks to reuse the all-twelve-label/Plain
parser. None exists: it is [BPR-1076](../issues/10-backlog/BPR-1076-implement-the-complete-catalog-and-bounded-lossless-prefix-recognizer.md)
of RFC-BPR-0047, held in Backlog by the owner's start hold for
RFC-BPR-0044–0051. Implementing a recognizer here would be the second
implementation the spec forbids and would start held work. Cycles therefore
keep the raw body authoritative with no label field and no cycle-only label
set, so the shared recognizer reads the same bytes when it lands. Label
recognition, pickers and icons in the cycle surfaces wait for BPR-1076;
RFC-BPR-0055 already depends on RFC-BPR-0047, and its closeout (BPR-1188)
judges RC55-03 whole then.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle_disclosure$|^review_cycle_feedback$|^qt_markdown_body_"`
passes on macOS arm64 (the Qt rows at 1.0, 1.25 and 2.0 scale); clang-format
and clang-tidy are clean on the touched units.

- RC55-03 (the cycle share): all twelve labels (praise, nitpick, suggestion,
  issue, todo, question, thought, chore, note, typo, polish, quibble), Plain,
  and malformed, quoted, code, unknown, CRLF, emoji and NUL-bearing bodies are
  stored with exact bytes and digests, reopened unchanged after a restart,
  disclosed as authored, and rendered by the shared native Markdown reader
  with the label shown as written and `copy_source` returning the authored
  bytes. No label creates a suggestion, lifecycle, provenance or state.
- RC55-08: human, adapter-observed (with adapter, provider, model and effort),
  self-reported (a pasted answer claiming "I am Claude Opus and I verified
  this" stays `self_reported`) and unknown revisions stay distinct. A note
  discloses only its current wording by default; replies, observed and pasted
  answers, the superseded wording, private references and author details are
  included only when named; lineage links never travel; an archived note or an
  incomplete history is refused.
- RC55-10: relabelling ("suggestion:" to "issue (blocking):") and undo are
  successor revisions in the shared lineage (`edited_from`, predecessor kept)
  that survive restart through the production store, and the note's
  suggestion keeps revision 1 with its exact bytes.

Owed: `ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle_disclosure$|^review_cycle_feedback$|^qt_markdown_body_" --output-on-failure`
on Linux and Windows (`build\dev`).

### BPR-1156: the native focused suggestion editor

Inventory: no multi-caret, box-selection or virtual-space editor existed.
The shared editors are `QPlainTextEdit`-based (`scoped_prose_editor`,
`review_draft_editor`), and RFC-BPR-0036 is Markdown rendering only. The card
therefore adds the narrow piece and nothing IDE-like.

(it reuses `core::text_line_ending` from `diff_compute.hpp`),

platform: automated Linux evidence, not a native desktop session. The owner's Mac observation and the Windows run remain

### BPR-1158: deterministic complete round payloads

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_json.hpp` and `.cpp`
(the `bpr.cycle.canonical-json.v1` reader and writer, and the strict base64
the BPR-1155 suggestion wire now shares instead of its private copy),
`src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_round.hpp` and `.cpp`,
`freeze_named_round` in `review_cycle.hpp`, the optional `Round` of
`cycle_freeze_commit` and its use in the SQLite store's `freeze`,
`src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_round.hpp` and `.cpp`,
the runtime's round methods, `tests/core/review_cycle_round_tests.cpp`,
`tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_round_tests.cpp`, their CMake
registrations, the `review_cycle_round` isolation pin and the domain-model
paragraph. No store schema changed: drafts keep their selection as their body
and rounds their payload object, as BPR-1145 laid out.

Design decisions. The payload names its own round (`F<n>`), so its digest is
computed before the store allocates the round; the freeze names the round it
expects and `freeze_named_round` refuses any other, so the payload and the
record cannot disagree. Item wire identities are the notes' own identities
(`I<n>`), stable across rounds; suggestions are `SG<note>.<n>`; the logical
project is `P` and the hex of the identity-derived project, never a path.
A draft pins each note's wording and each suggestion's revision; a stale pin
is refused and named rather than silently refreshed. Selected context naming
one file of one revision is included once (the whole file if any selection
asks for it, otherwise one excerpt spanning the selected ranges), because the
pack's reference oracle refuses two sources for one file and revision.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle|^review_cycle_(round|suggestion|feedback|workbench)$|^sqlite_review_cycle_storage$|^architecture_boundar|network_isolation_manifest" --output-on-failure`
passes 19 of 19 on macOS arm64 (dev preset, Homebrew LLVM) at `09e3ce3c3`;
clang-format and clang-tidy (`--warnings-as-errors`) are clean on the new and
touched translation units. A mutation that skipped the stale-wording check
fails `core_review_cycle_round` at "an older wording is not substituted".

- RC56-01: through the production runtime and SQLite store, a draft selecting
  two notes, one exact CRLF suggestion and one context file freezes into
  `F1` holding the selected wording, primary and included related
  locations, the suggestion's exact bytes and the file's exact bytes, in the
  chosen order, with explicit counts and `instruction_set_complete`. In the
  core course, four notes of every target kind (range, file, module, cycle)
  with a per-item `discuss` override, a requested relationship and two
  excerpts plus a full file assemble to a payload whose digest is pinned
  (`67b0832c…455e77`). The pack's `examples/round.json` is admitted and its
  canonical digest is exactly `061dd800…8d86acd`; its typed reading
  re-encodes to the same bytes.
- RC56-02: the same inputs give the same bytes; reordering two notes or
  changing an item action changes the digest; repeating a freeze of the same
  draft revision reuses `F1`, a retried operation replays, and after the
  note is edited the old selection is refused `stale_wording` (item 0) while
  saving the new wording as a new draft revision freezes `F2` with a new
  digest; `F1` reads back byte for byte, including after reopening the store.
- RC56-06: the canonical payload contains none of: an unselected note, a
  reply or pasted answer, the superseded wording, a `private_only` related
  location, adapter/provider/model names, the workspace root's absolute
  location or the store root. The pinned core course and the store course
  both assert this on the bytes.
- RC56-09: a freeze cancelled before its transaction leaves no round; a
  payload installed by a process that died before the round commit remains
  only as one unreferenced object, and the retried freeze adopts exactly
  those bytes, leaving nothing unreferenced; retrying the operation replays
  and a new request reuses the round, so there is exactly one logical round.
  The SIGKILL-at-COMMIT course for freeze is BPR-1145's and unchanged.
- Freeze readiness: an open IME composition or unsaved typing is refused
  `unflushed`, and an editor showing an older draft revision `stale_draft`,
  before anything is read or installed. The native editor that reports this
  state is BPR-1161's surface.

Limits: context files are read through the comparison host, so one file
larger than its display bound is `unavailable_context` rather than admitted up
to the 64 MiB context maximum. Owed native runs: Linux
`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle_round$|^review_cycle_round$|^core_review_cycle_suggestion$|^review_cycle_suggestion$|^sqlite_review_cycle_storage$" --output-on-failure`,
and the same on Windows with `build\dev` plus `test_network_isolation_manifest`
for the new `review_cycle_round` pin.

### BPR-1159: the complete round document, copied and saved

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_round_markdown.hpp` and
`.cpp`, `src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_export.hpp`
and `.cpp`, `src/adapters/workspace/include/bpr/adapters/workspace/local_export_destination.hpp`
and `src/adapters/workspace/src/local_export_destination.cpp` (a separate
`BetterPullReview::AdapterExport` library, so the workspace source stays
read-only), `tests/core/review_cycle_round_markdown_tests.cpp`,
`tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_export_tests.cpp`, the shared
`round_drafts` helper moved into `tests/workspace_contract/cycle_session.hpp`,
their CMake registrations, the `review_cycle_export` isolation pin and the
domain-model paragraph.

Compatibility decision. The pack's reference renderer and its example
`review.md` define the document; the product renderer reproduces that file
byte for byte from `round.json`, so a bundle this build writes also passes the
pack's own oracle for C++ content. It departs from the reference only where
the reference would mislead: the fence language follows the file extension
(the reference always says `cpp`); empty constraint and write-scope lists and
the reviewer's omissions are stated (the reference prints an empty list and
omits omissions); a body whose Markdown would change the document's structure
is shown verbatim; empty sides explain the insertion or deletion; trailing
spaces, other controls and a missing final line break also carry base64; and
invalid UTF-8 source is labelled. Line numbers are not added to locations,
which keeps the exact byte ranges the payload holds.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle|^review_cycle_(round|export|suggestion|feedback)$|^sqlite_review_cycle_storage$|^architecture_boundar|network_isolation_manifest"`
passes 20 of 20 on macOS arm64 (dev preset) at `10c3154cd`; clang-format and
clang-tidy are clean on the new units. `architecture_boundaries` first failed
on a `"bash"` string literal in the fence-language table (its shell-API rule);
shell files now get no fence language. A mutation that fixed the fence at
three backticks fails `core_review_cycle_round_markdown` at "nested fences
contained".

- RC56-03 (this card's share): the clipboard text, the saved file and the
  document rendered from the round read back through a restarted store are
  the same bytes, carrying the canonical digest and the item identities; a
  repeated save of the same round writes nothing. The bundle's share is
  BPR-1160's; CLI retrieval and adapter delivery belong to BPR-1166 and the
  optional-adapter milestone.
- RC56-04 (automated share; owner judgment pending): the pack's eight-item,
  two-suggestion round renders to exactly its `review.md`; all eight item
  headings lie outside every fence and in order, every suggestion side is
  present as text or base64, and both CRLF sides carry base64. An unclosed
  backtick or tilde fence, a forged `## I9` heading, a setext heading and an
  HTML comment in a body are contained verbatim while a balanced fence and a
  lower heading render as written; suggestion bytes holding four backticks get
  a five-backtick fence; an empty replacement, invalid UTF-8, trailing spaces
  and a missing final line break are explained with exact base64; invalid
  source text is labelled. Whether a person can act on the document without
  BPR is asked in the attended session.
- RC56-08: a document over its channel budget is refused with its full size,
  item and source counts (clipboard budget 16 MiB, file 256 MiB), never cut;
  exactly at budget it is complete. Round assembly already refuses an
  oversized selection with its measure (BPR-1158), so splitting stays the
  reviewer's decision.
- Save conflict: an existing file with other bytes is a conflict that names
  their digest and writes nothing; after someone else edits the file, a
  confirmation of the old digest is still a conflict; confirming the current
  digest replaces it; `replace` re-checks the digest itself; a directory and a
  symbolic link are `not_regular` and the link target is unchanged; a missing
  folder and a relative path are refused; no temporary file is left beside
  the destination and the workspace bytes are unchanged; no absolute workspace
  or store path appears in the document.

Limits: the Qt clipboard write and the share preview are BPR-1161's. Owed
native runs: Linux
`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle_round_markdown$|^review_cycle_export$|^review_cycle_round$" --output-on-failure`
(the `renameat2` path), and the same on Windows with `build\dev` plus
`|^test_network_isolation_manifest$` (the `MoveFileExW` path; the symbolic
link row runs only where the course may create links).

### BPR-1160: bounded portable review bundles

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_zip.hpp` and `.cpp` (a
deterministic stored-member writer, a bounded reader and an RFC 1951 inflater
after Mark Adler's `puff`, with CRC-32), `review_cycle_bundle.hpp` and `.cpp`,
`review_cycle_import.hpp` and `.cpp`,
`src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_import.hpp` and `.cpp`,
the store's `admit_import`, `imports` and `load_import` with schema 5
(`imports`, `import_objects`, and `import_forks` for BPR-1162; schemas 1–4
migrate in place; quarantined objects count as referenced), the runtime's
import methods, `read_chosen_file` in the export adapter,
`tests/core/review_cycle_bundle_tests.cpp`,
`tests/scripts/round_bundle_oracle_tests.py`,
`tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_bundle_tests.cpp`, the schema-4 row
and v5 drops in `tests/sqlite_contract/review_cycle_storage_tests.cpp`, the
`review_cycle_bundle` isolation pin, and the domain-model and threat-model
paragraphs. The store's receipt replay now accepts cycle zero, which only an
import's receipt uses.

Design decisions. The core owns the container as byte functions, so no
archive library enters the build and nothing is extracted to a named file;
the writer stores members uncompressed with fixed timestamps, so one round
always yields the same bundle. The reader accepts deflate because other
producers (the pack's own example) compress. Only the four member kinds the
format defines are admitted; `review.md` must be exactly the document the
round renders to, so a readable file can never be a competing edit.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle|^review_cycle_|^sqlite_review_cycle|^architecture_boundar|network_isolation_manifest"`
passes 30 of 30 on macOS arm64 at `674889e6c`; clang-format and clang-tidy
are clean on the new and touched units. A mutation that skipped the overlap
check fails `core_review_cycle_bundle` at "overlapping members".

- RC56-05: the pack's deflated `example.bpr-review.zip` opens with digest
  `061dd800…8d86acd` and its exact `review.md`; a rebuilt bundle is
  byte-identical on repeat, reopens to the same digest and document, and the
  pack's own `reference_contract.validate_bundle` accepts it
  (`core_review_cycle_bundle_pack_oracle`). Refused before admission, each
  with its named problem: traversal, absolute, drive (`C:/`, `C:x`), UNC,
  backslash, single- and double-percent-encoded traversal, encoded control
  and backslash, `.` segments, reserved device names (`con.md`, `lpt1`),
  trailing dot or space and colon names; NUL and non-UTF-8 names; duplicate,
  case-fold and NFC/NFD alias members; unexpected and executable members;
  symbolic-link, directory and DOS-directory entries; encryption, bzip2 and
  AES methods; CRC mismatch; bytes after the end record or before the
  members; stored sizes that disagree; a real fixed-Huffman stream over
  100:1, a stream that inflates past its declared size, corrupt deflate data,
  too many entries and too many decoded bytes; member data hidden inside
  another member; manifest version, duplicate manifest keys, unlisted and
  absent members and a hash mismatch; invalid base64, invalid UTF-8,
  duplicate keys, an unsupported version and a digest mismatch in the round;
  an edited `review.md`; attachment bytes that are not their name and an
  attachment the round does not name.
- Through the production path: a frozen round saved as `RC-1-F1.bpr-review.zip`
  to a chosen folder, read back through `read_chosen_file`, opens into a
  second profile's quarantine with no account, provider or agent, keeping
  `RC-1`/`F1` and the digest, and reads back as the same canonical round and
  document; a repeat import, a restart and a retried operation find the same
  record; a damaged bundle leaves the store's inventory unchanged; the pack's
  bundle quarantines beside it under its own names; a link at the chosen path
  is not followed and an over-bound file is not read; the folders around the
  bundle and the project are byte-for-byte unchanged, so nothing is
  extracted. A cancelled import commits nothing, and after a simulated crash
  between installing the payload and admitting it the retried import admits
  once and leaves nothing unreferenced.

Limits: the native open and save dialogs, and the read-only view of a
quarantined round, are BPR-1161's surface. Owed native runs: Linux
`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle_bundle|^review_cycle_bundle$|^sqlite_review_cycle_storage$" --output-on-failure`,
and the same on Windows with `build\dev` plus `|^test_network_isolation_manifest$`
(the `CreateFileW` read path and the `review_cycle_bundle` pin).

### BPR-1162: external rounds attached through explicit mapping

Owned paths: the association and fork decisions in
`src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_import.hpp` and `.cpp`
(`imported_project_key`, `associate_import`, `plan_import_fork`, the stored
association), `begin_cycle_feedback::Provenance` in
`review_cycle_feedback.hpp`/`.cpp` (a note may start as `unknown` for text
taken from another's round; the store's request identity is unchanged for
`human`), `attach_imported_round` and `fork_imported_round` in
`application/review_cycle_import`, the store's `associate_import`,
`fork_import`, `import_forks` and association release on cycle removal, the
runtime's attach and fork methods, `tests/core/review_cycle_import_tests.cpp`,
`tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_attach_tests.cpp`, their CMake
registrations, the `review_cycle_attach` isolation pin and the domain-model
paragraph.

Design decisions. The sender's project name is mapped through the existing
`workspace_import_mapping` and `resolve_imported_project` of BPR-1148, keyed
by a digest of the name alone, so no label, folder name or path can stand in
for a mapping. An association needs a mapping for every sender revision the
round names, and every file it names by bytes must be exactly those bytes in
the mapped local revision as retained. A fork re-anchors each location on the
mapped local bytes and starts notes of `unknown` provenance: the sender was
never observed, so the text is neither the reviewer's nor a claim. Forks do
not copy the sender's suggestions, which stay readable in the quarantined
round.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle|^core_private_review$|^review_cycle_|^sqlite_review_cycle|^architecture_boundar|network_isolation_manifest"`
passes 33 of 33 on macOS arm64 at `51be3f6cb`; clang-format and clang-tidy
are clean on the touched units. A mutation that skipped the byte check fails
`core_review_cycle_import` at "mismatched bytes name the path".

- RC56-05 (this card's share): a second profile opens a colleague's bundle
  with no account, provider or agent; with two projects titled `cache`, no
  mapping or a mapping to the other project is `unmapped_project`, and a
  path-like project label resolves nothing; a project whose root is gone and
  holds no capture is `invalid_mapping`; mapping the sender's `R1` to a local
  revision whose `src/cache.cpp` differs is `mismatched_source` naming that
  file and records nothing; the correct mapping associates, repeats as no
  change and is never replaced; a fork into another cycle is
  `not_associated`; the fork starts four notes of `unknown` provenance at the
  mapped bytes, a repeated fork adds nothing, and the reviewer's edit is a
  local revision; the quarantined record, its canonical payload and the
  sender's `RC-1`/`F1` names are byte-for-byte unchanged after every refused
  and accepted change; a third profile imports the same bundle independently;
  an import over quota admits nothing; removing the cycle releases the
  association and forks and leaves the round readable; the recipient's project
  folders are unchanged throughout, so nothing is applied.
- RC57-08 (this card's share): after `F1`, a colleague's plain prose (as
  `unknown`) answers one note and a pasted agent answer claiming verification
  (as `self_reported`) another, the third note gets nothing; the files change
  through ordinary tools and `R2` is captured with no structured response or
  import; the unanswered note stays as written, the claim stays
  self-reported, and `F1` is unchanged. Structured responses are BPR-1164's.

Owed native runs: Linux
`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle_import$|^review_cycle_attach$|^core_review_cycle_feedback$|^review_cycle_feedback$" --output-on-failure`,
and the same on Windows with `build\dev` plus `|^test_network_isolation_manifest$`
for the `review_cycle_attach` pin.

replacement, and a replace whose bytes changed after confirmation. The

an existing name comes back `exists`. `replace` re-hashes the destination first
and refuses a link or reparse point as `not_regular`. Then

`rename` are not exercised by any row. A read-only destination, and one that
another process holds open without `FILE_SHARE_DELETE`, both fail the save as

no specific reason. The logs are station records in

### BPR-1163: manual capture and named comparison views

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_views.hpp` and `.cpp`
(reviewer checkpoints, the named views and their resolution, round starts,
diff rows, the revision history), the comparison fields and gestures of
`review_cycle_tabs.hpp`/`.cpp` (`cycle_tab_comparison`, `shown_pair`,
`show_comparison`, `show_full_source`, `refresh_active`, restoration checks),
`src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_views.hpp` and `.cpp`
(`load_view_facts`, `load_cycle_history`), the store's `mark_reviewed`,
`last_review`, schema 6 (`checkpoints`; schemas 1–5 migrate in place;
removal deletes a cycle's checkpoints) and the tab-record comparison trailer,
the runtime's `mark_reviewed_selected`, `view_facts_selected` and
`history_selected`, the workbench's `capture_revision`, `compare`,
`full_source`, `refresh_view` and `mark_reviewed` with the projection's
`Comparison`, `NewerAvailable`, `Facts`, `History` and `Notice`, the Qt
page's Capture revision (with Record another observation), view chooser, Mark
reviewed, Show newer and comparison pane (`review_cycle_view.hpp`/`.cpp`),
the desktop dispatch in `src/app/src/review_cycle_desktop.cpp`,
`tests/core/review_cycle_views_tests.cpp`, the iteration course in
`tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_workbench_tests.cpp` (now given the
build's Git), the v5 migration row in `review_cycle_storage_tests.cpp`, the
Qt and desktop additions, and the domain-model paragraph. No new test
executable or isolation pin.

Design decisions. The workbench and page call the first capture `R1`, as the
wire names do; the RFC's `R0` is that capture. A named view is kept on the
tab with the left revision it resolved to; the right revision is the tab's
own, so every gesture that moves a full-source tab (another file, duplicate)
keeps a comparison too. Round starts come from each round's verified payload
(`SourceRevision`), never from delivery or response records. A reported
result is used only when it names a captured revision; otherwise the view is
`unknown_revision` rather than the latest. The history reads each listed
revision's manifest for the checkout head, so no capture format changed;
commit ancestry is not read, so a moved head says the commits between were
not captured rather than guessing whether it descends. Comments and
suggestions stay in full source; a comparison pane is for reading.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -j 8 -R "^core_|^review_cycle|^sqlite_review_cycle|^qt_review_cycle_view_|^qt_suggestion_editor_|^application_review_cycle|^workspace_source$|^architecture|network_isolation"`
passes 92 of 92 on macOS arm64 (dev preset, Homebrew LLVM, `build/dev`) at
`48ba9741d`, after a full `-k 0` dev build in which only the pre-existing
`test_scoped_writing_agent` (BPR-1211) fails. clang-format is clean on every
touched unit; clang-tidy reports nothing new on them beyond the
`bugprone-branch-clone` pattern it also reports on unchanged lines of the
same files. A mutation that let `refresh_active` move a pinned pair fails
`core_review_cycle_views` at "a pinned pair never refreshes".

- RC57-01 (this card's share): in `review_cycle_workbench` (real folder, real
  store, the build's Git), R0 is captured on open, a note is made and `F1`
  frozen against it through the runtime while no desktop host runs; the
  folder is then edited and committed with ordinary tools and nothing is sent
  back. Reopening captures nothing by itself; Capture revision records R1
  with the folder's bytes unchanged by the capture; a second capture of the
  same bytes records nothing and says "No change since R2; nothing new was
  recorded."; a deliberate capture records another observation; another
  branch and edit are captured as R3. No receipt, response, agent, commit
  check or callback is read anywhere in the course. In the desktop public path
  the same happens through the real window's Capture revision action. The
  first-value course (restart, eight notes, Copy/Save) is BPR-1166's.
- RC57-02 (with BPR-1151's share): This round (F1 from its bound R0 to the
  latest, and with a named result), Since my last review (refused in words
  until the reviewer marks one; opening files never counts), All cycle
  changes and Choose revisions resolve the intended retained material, with
  rows from the shared engine. A chosen pair and a named-result round are
  pinned: after newer captures they signal newer material, refresh refuses to
  move them, and they survive a restart exactly; semantic views move only on
  refresh; full source moves to the latest only on refresh. A reported result
  that was never captured is unavailable, not the latest. A capture
  superseded by closing the cycle records nothing and changes nothing shown.
  The core course covers the same-source discussion round (`F2` on R1 shows
  no change until R2).
- RC57-09: local capture order, the checkout head each capture recorded and
  sender revisions an attached round mapped here are separate fields of each
  history entry. With Git, R1 is `head_moved` (commits between not captured,
  ancestry not claimed), the deliberate capture `same_head` with the same
  bytes, and the branch switch `branch_changed` naming `refs/heads/feature`;
  a plain folder is `no_repository`. A missing capture number and a recorded
  parent that is not the previous capture are explicit (`Unlisted`,
  `ParentGap`), a page does not guess the capture before it, and
  `ProviderChronology` is always false: no upload order is inferred.

Limits: comparison rows are text only (no intraline highlight or moved-block
marks yet), and binary or over-bound files state that they have no text
comparison. The file list is the latest capture's. The history lists at most
256 captures in the page.

Owed native runs: Linux
`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle_(views|tabs|comparison)$|^sqlite_review_cycle_storage$|^review_cycle_workbench$|^qt_review_cycle_view_|^application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path$" --output-on-failure`
(the Git chronology rows and libstdc++ assertions), and the same on Windows
with `build\dev` plus `|^test_network_isolation_manifest$` (the workbench row
now takes `--git`).

### BPR-1164: structured responses and hand-entered replies

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_response.hpp` and `.cpp`
(the `bpr.cycle.response.v1` reader and canonical writer, correlation with a
frozen round, manual replies and their stored form, item statements, claimed
result resolution), `valid_cycle_wire_identifier` (now public in
`review_cycle_round.hpp`, which uses it), the store's `append_response`,
`responses` and schema 6's `responses` table (pinned as referenced, removed
with the cycle), `src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_response.hpp`
and `.cpp`, the runtime's `import_response_selected`, `attach_reply_selected`
and `responses_selected`, the workbench's `set_file_reader`,
`import_response`, `attach_reply` and the projection's note `Statements` and
`Responses`, the Qt page's Add reply menu and reply editor and the statement
rows in the notes tray, the composition's response-file dialog and reader
(`read_chosen_file`, so the composition now links `AdapterExport`),
`tests/core/review_cycle_response_tests.cpp`,
`tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_response_tests.cpp` with its CMake
registration and `review_cycle_response` isolation pin, the Qt, desktop and
storage additions, and the domain-model and threat-model paragraphs.

Design decisions. Statements are appended, never merged into a note: a
structured response and each pasted reply are separate records with the
channel this profile observed (`response_file`, `manual_entry`), while the
author, model and result they name stay claims. The wire's five dispositions
are kept as sent rather than mapped onto the domain's claim words. Content is
stored as canonical bytes, so replay is judged on content rather than
whitespace; a structured response is keyed by its identifier within the
cycle. A reply names round items (`I<n>`) or none; the reviewer's selected
note becomes its item. Appending does not advance the cycle's revision, so a
reply never makes a concurrent capture or note stale. Output queued before
the selection changed completes `cancelled` through the runtime's existing
generation check.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -j 8 -R "^core_|^review_cycle|^sqlite_review_cycle|^qt_review_cycle_view_|^qt_suggestion_editor_|^application_review_cycle|^workspace_source$|^architecture|network_isolation"`
passes 94 of 94 on macOS arm64 at `b74cbe414`, after a full `-k 0` dev build
(only `test_scoped_writing_agent`, BPR-1211, fails). clang-format is clean on
the touched units; clang-tidy adds no finding class. A mutation that skipped
the item-revision check fails `review_cycle_response` at "another item
revision".

- RC57-04: through the production runtime and store, a structured response
  naming `RC-1`, `F1`, F1's digest and `I1`/`I2` at revision 1 is appended as
  statement 1 with channel `response_file`; a colleague's pasted prose about
  `I2` and another's about the round as a whole are statements 2 and 3 with
  channel `manual_entry`; all three read back exactly after a restart and
  survive `reclaim`. In the real window the reviewer pastes a reply to the
  selected note and opens a response file; both appear beside the note as
  claims ("claims agent …", "response file", "entered by hand").
- RC57-05: the same response again (another operation, other whitespace)
  changes nothing; a retried operation replays; the same identifier with
  another body is `reused_identifier`; another cycle, another round name, a
  round never frozen, the wrong round digest, an unknown item and another item
  revision are refused and append nothing; a response queued before the
  reviewer switched cycles completes `cancelled` and appends nothing.
- RC57-08 (with BPR-1162's share): prose-only replies need no JSON or
  identifier; a partial response covers only the items it names, and an item
  nobody answered has no statement rather than an invented "not attempted";
  two recipients' statements about one item stand side by side.
- RC57-10: a response claiming an agent and model ("Claude Opus
  (verified)"), a `test_report` of passing tests and command-like evidence
  text is kept exactly as claims: the command never runs (its canary file is
  never created), the project folder is byte-for-byte unchanged, no revision
  is captured, no note, lifecycle or version changes, F1 and the round count
  are unchanged, and no review mark moves. `"verified":true`, a `verified`
  disposition, an item approval and a trust field are refused as shape. A
  claimed result `R2` resolves only after the reviewer captured R2.

Limits: the pack's `response.json` is admitted and correlates with the pack's
round in the core course; responses to rounds received from others (imports)
are not attached here. Responses are listed whole (a cycle's statements are
read on every load).

Owed native runs: Linux
`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle_response$|^review_cycle_response$|^sqlite_review_cycle_storage$|^qt_review_cycle_view_|^application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path$" --output-on-failure`,
and the same on Windows with `build\dev` plus `|^test_network_isolation_manifest$`
(the new `review_cycle_response` pin is not yet measured contained; the
response file is read through `CreateFileW`).

### BPR-1165: reconciliation, explicit verification and the next round

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_verification.hpp` and
`.cpp` (decision records, `verification_material`, `check_verification`,
currency, `reconcile_item`, `round_assignment`, `plan_next_round`),
`src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_verification.hpp` and
`.cpp` (`record_item_decision`, `reconcile_cycle_items`,
`prepare_next_round`), the store's `record_verification`, `verifications` and
schema 6's `verifications` table (removed with the cycle), the runtime's
`decide_selected`, `reconcile_selected` and `prepare_next_round_selected`,
the workbench's `decide`, `prepare_next_round`, the notes' `Reconciliation`
and the projection's `NextRound`, the Qt page's decision menu on a note, the
status and suggestion lines in the notes tray and the tray's Next round
button, the composition's dispatch, `tests/core/review_cycle_verification_tests.cpp`,
`tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_verification_tests.cpp` with its CMake
registration and `review_cycle_verification` isolation pin, the Qt, desktop
and storage additions, and the domain-model paragraph.

Design decisions. Schema 6 now holds `checkpoints`, `verifications` and
`responses`; it is unpublished until this milestone is pushed, so all three
cards share it. A decision's relevant material is each file a note's
locations name (primary and related), by exact blob or absence, or the whole
manifest for a module or whole-cycle note: an unrelated file changing keeps a
verification current. The reviewer can only decide about the note's current
wording; an amended note makes an older decision history. The next round is
planned from the latest frozen round's payload rather than its draft, which
may have been edited since, so the carried assignment is exactly what was
sent; carried items keep the suggestions they carried at their current
revisions and new notes bring their proposed suggestions. Resolved means the
reviewer's current decision is `verified` or `withdrawn`; `deferred` and
`needs_work` stay open.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -j 8 -R "^core_|^review_cycle|^sqlite_review_cycle|^qt_review_cycle_view_|^qt_suggestion_editor_|^application_review_cycle|^workspace_source$|^architecture|network_isolation"`
passes 96 of 96 on macOS arm64 at `48412c586`, after a full `-k 0` dev build
(only `test_scoped_writing_agent`, BPR-1211, fails). clang-format is clean on
the touched units; clang-tidy adds no finding class. A mutation that kept
every verification current fails `review_cycle_verification` at "the checked
material changed: recheck needed".

- RC57-03: against R2, a note whose code was replaced exactly as suggested is
  `unmapped` with the suggestion's result `already_applied`; a note on a
  deleted file is `deleted`; a snippet copied into two new contexts is
  `ambiguous` with two candidates; an illustrative suggestion done
  differently stays `unknown`; a module note has no correspondence. Each is
  shown in its own words, and none of them resolves its note: with a
  recipient's "addressed" the first two only need verification, the dispute is
  `reported` and the rest stay `open`. Moved and exact correspondences are
  BPR-1153's rows.
- RC57-06: recipient claims and reviewer decisions are separate: `verified`,
  `needs_work` and `withdrawn` change status only by the reviewer's gesture
  (in the real window, the note's decision menu), bound to the checked
  revision, the note's wording and the material digest. A change to an
  unrelated file keeps the verification current; a change to the note's file
  makes it `recheck_needed` with the R2 decision kept and still valid
  against R2; amending the note makes it `item_amended`; an uncaptured
  revision or unknown note is refused; an explicit clearing returns to the
  claim.
- RC57-07: the next round's draft carries F1's action, goal and constraints
  against the latest capture, its four unresolved items at their current
  wording (the amended one included) and carried suggestion revisions,
  leaves the withdrawn note out, and adds the note written after F1; replies,
  earlier wordings and context are not dumped in. It is saved as a draft only:
  no round is frozen and nothing is copied, sent or written until the reviewer
  freezes it as F2. In the real window, Next round saves the draft with the
  verified note left out and the other note added. Sharing it is BPR-1161's
  surface.
