## Publication milestone

Bound on the coordinator's assignment: run `bpr-cycles-20260929`, segment
`publication`, mandate revision `1`, strategy `coordinator-implementer`
recipe revision 2; definition `sha256:6b313ca2…e73e1f`, obligations
BPR-1172–1178, exits `publication:exit:1` and `publication:exit:2`. Base:
local `main` at `52f28dd1b`. The binding names every earlier milestone,
`external-consumers` included, as accepted because the checkpoint helper
requires it. Starting `publication` while `portable-rounds` and
`manual-iteration` are partial (parked reviews) and before
`external-consumers` is the coordinator's scheduling decision, not an owner
verdict. BPR-1177 depends on BPR-1161 and BPR-1178 on BPR-1177, so both stay
in Backlog and the milestone is bound as partial.

Side-effect boundary: the owner has named no safe GitHub target, so no remote
GitHub mutation of any kind is made. Publication is implemented and verified
against fixtures, recordings, fake transports and local repositories only;
every criterion that needs a live branch, push or pull request stays owed.

### BPR-1172: selected outgoing content over an explicit Git base

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/sha1.hpp` and `.cpp` (SHA-1 for Git
identities only), `src/core/include/bpr/core/git_objects.hpp` and `.cpp`
(Git blob, tree and commit encodings and identities in both object formats,
`verified_commit_tree`, `line_feed_form`),
`src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_outgoing.hpp` and `.cpp`
(`plan_outgoing`, `render_outgoing_preview`),
`src/application/include/bpr/application/publication_candidate_port.hpp`
(the separate local publication catalog), `src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_outgoing.hpp`
and `.cpp` (`resolve_reference`, `review_cycle_outgoing_host::plan`),
`src/adapters/git/include/bpr/adapters/git/installed_git_publication.hpp` and
the `installed_git_publication` class and `make_installed_git_publication`
factory in `installed_git_process.cpp` (with the shared
`bootstrap_installed_git` extracted from `make_installed_git_process`, a
per-command environment list on the runner's request, and the runner reserving
at most 64 KiB of standard output up front), `tests/core/git_objects_tests.cpp`,
`tests/core/review_cycle_outgoing_tests.cpp`,
`tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_outgoing_tests.cpp` with its CMake
registration and `review_cycle_outgoing` isolation pin, and the
domain-model paragraph.

Design decisions. P's complete tree is the candidate's start: a path a
capture did not enumerate, excluded, or holds beneath a sparse or submodule
boundary keeps P's entry, and a complete capture that refused some names is
treated as partial for absence. P and T are named by the reviewer and read as
data (`HEAD`, loose references, one level of symbolic reference,
`packed-refs`); short names are not guessed and reftable storage is not read.
The publication catalog is new and separate from the epoch cache's: installed
Git under the same isolated runner, commands in a BPR-owned bare store per
object format (`publication/v1/{sha1,sha256}.git`), with the checkout's
objects directory supplied only as `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES` for one
command, so the checkout's configuration, hooks, refs, index and attributes
are never read and nothing is written there. A listing is admitted only when
the commit object's bytes hash to P, name the tree listed, and the listing
rebuilds that tree in the core; a legacy non-canonical tree is therefore
refused rather than silently rewritten. "Relevant" starting work under the
first choice is a tracked change, a deletion, or a path or folder a note
names; untracked scratch nothing touched is listed as unrelated. CRLF bytes
whose LF form is P's blob are not starting work. Filter-managed (LFS) paths
cannot be compared with P or built natively, and a submodule or file/folder
collision blocks a native build; each is visible.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -j 8 -R "^core_git_objects$|^core_review_cycle_|^review_cycle|^sqlite_review_cycle|^application_review_cycle|^workspace_source$|^architecture|^core_boundary|network_isolation|^git_process_contract$|^git_cache_contract$|^git_sparse_cache_contract$"`
passes 51 of 51 on macOS arm64 (dev preset, Apple clang, `build/dev`, Apple
Git 2.50.1) at `b7fc9328f`, after a full `-k 0` dev build in which only the
pre-existing `test_scoped_writing_agent` (BPR-1211) fails; the Git process and
cache contracts cover the runner change. clang-format is clean and clang-tidy
reports nothing on the new units. Every Git identity in `core_git_objects` was
produced by installed Git (`hash-object`, `write-tree`, `commit-tree`) in both
formats. A mutation that let an out-of-scope path read as absent fails both
`core_review_cycle_outgoing` and `review_cycle_outgoing` (P's `README.md`
deleted from the candidate).

- RC59-01: `review_cycle_outgoing` (real SHA-1 and SHA-256 checkouts made by
  the build's Git, real store, the product's installed-Git catalog): R0 is the
  first capture with dirty `src/a.cpp`, an untracked scratch file and a draft a
  note names; Rn adds improvements, `git mv` and an executable bit; P is the
  exact commit `HEAD` names and T `refs/remotes/origin/main`, which moved on
  and changed `src/b.cpp`. The plan keeps R0, Rn, P and T separate; `a.cpp`
  shows starting work and improvement and goes out whole; the scratch file is
  unrelated and stays out; the draft is relevant; `b.cpp` is marked as changed
  on the target since P, and the preview says T moved and that it is the
  checkout's local reference, not the remote now. Under improvements-only
  `a.cpp` waits for an explicit choice (include takes the final bytes,
  starting work and all; exclude keeps P's). Packed references, an exact
  commit, a missing or unsafe reference and a missing commit behave as named.
  The readable preview (`render_outgoing_preview`) states each baseline,
  what goes out, what needs a decision, what cannot be built and what stays out
  and why. The checkout and its `.git` are byte-for-byte unchanged by
  planning. The window's publication preview that shows this is BPR-1177's.
- RC59-03: the candidate is P's complete listing with only selected rows
  changed. A module-only cycle (`src`) leaves `README.md`, `docs/` and `tools/`
  as P's; a complete capture that excluded a folder leaves P's files there; a
  path only a later scope saw has no known origin and is not proposed (it can
  be included deliberately); a path the final capture narrowed away keeps P's;
  deletion, rename (named as such) and mode change are explicit; a file/folder
  collision blocks the build instead of producing an invalid tree; a candidate
  equal to P is "No change"; and a plain folder is refused as
  `repository_required` with no synthetic root commit.

Limits: T is read from the checkout's local references here; the provider's
answer for the destination branch is read at publication (BPR-1175/1176).
Relevance for untracked starting work comes only from notes naming it.
Capture bytes are hashed from retained material, one object at a time.

Owed native runs: Linux
`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_git_objects$|^core_review_cycle_outgoing$|^review_cycle_outgoing$|^git_process_contract$" --output-on-failure`
(libstdc++ assertions, GCC `-Werror`, `/proc` paths), and the same on Windows
with `build\dev` plus `|^test_network_isolation_manifest$` (the alternate
object directory list separator is `;`, the store's private-directory checks,
and the new `review_cycle_outgoing` pin, not yet measured contained).

### BPR-1173: isolated squashed candidates and exact transformation limits

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_candidate.hpp` and
`.cpp` (`prepare_candidate`, `candidate_message`, `render_candidate_preview`,
`prepare_external`), the size carried on `outgoing_side`, the port's
`write_blob`, `write_tree`, `write_commit` and `diff_trees` with the new
`identity_mismatch` failure, their `installed_git_publication` implementations
(private index under the isolation root's `tmp` behind the process boundary
lock, per-command `GIT_INDEX_FILE` and author/committer variables), the host's
`build` and `prepare_external` over a shared `assemble`,
`tests/core/review_cycle_candidate_tests.cpp`,
`tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_candidate_tests.cpp` with its CMake
registration and `review_cycle_candidate` isolation pin, the shared
`tests/workspace_contract/publication_course.hpp`, and the domain-model and
threat-model paragraphs.

Design decisions. The candidate is one commit, P its only parent, the plan's
tree, the reviewer's confirmed author, committer, times, title and body, and
no trailer or signature. The one byte-changing transformation is a named
CRLF-to-LF normalization of a selected text path whose captured bytes have
CRLF endings; it is recorded with both blobs, and it makes another tree and
commit. The host re-hashes every retained byte (after that normalization)
against the planned identity before Git sees it, so a corrupt retained object
never becomes a candidate. Blobs go in with `hash-object --no-filters`, the
tree is built by `read-tree`/`update-index -z --index-info`/`write-tree` in a
private index, and the commit by `commit-tree --no-gpg-sign` with explicit
author and committer variables; every answer must be the core's identity or
the build is refused as `identity_mismatch`. A filter-managed change, a
submodule change, a file/folder collision, required signing, an undecided
improvement and an unchanged tree are named limits. External preparation writes
the selected captured bytes (filter-managed ones as bytes) into the private
store and returns `diff-tree -p --binary --full-index --no-ext-diff
--no-textconv` against P, with instructions that say it is not native
publication and which paths the reviewer's filters must still process.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -j 8 -R "^core_git_objects$|^core_review_cycle_|^review_cycle|^sqlite_review_cycle|^application_review_cycle|^workspace_source$|^architecture|^core_boundary|network_isolation|^git_process_contract$|^git_cache_contract$"`
passes 52 of 52 on macOS arm64 (Apple Git 2.50.1) at `b94e2dd02`, after a
full `-k 0` dev build (only `test_scoped_writing_agent`, BPR-1211, fails).
clang-format is clean; clang-tidy reports nothing on the new units and nothing
in the new adapter lines (the one remaining `installed_git_process.cpp`
finding, `bugprone-signed-bitwise` at an unchanged line, predates this card).
A mutation that used the checkout's own repository as the store fails
`review_cycle_candidate` at "the checkout's files, index, references and
configuration are byte-for-byte unchanged".

- RC59-02: in `review_cycle_candidate` (real SHA-1 and SHA-256 checkouts, real
  store, the product's installed-Git catalog), the whole checkout including
  `.git` (index, references, `HEAD`, configuration, hooks, objects) is hashed
  before and after a limited build, a native build and a refused signed build,
  and is unchanged. The checkout carries `core.fsmonitor`, `core.hooksPath`,
  hook scripts in both hook directories, a required `filter.evil` clean and
  smudge on a tracked path, `diff.external`, `commit.gpgsign` with
  `gpg.program`, `core.pager`, `core.sshCommand`, `core.alternateRefsCommand`
  and `uploadpack.packObjectsHook`, each a canary; none fires during planning
  or building, and afterwards ordinary Git in the checkout does fire the
  filter canary (positive control). The candidate lives only in the private
  store; an independent clone that borrows the store's objects reads the
  commit back byte for byte as the core encoded it, with P as its parent and
  every tree entry as planned.
- RC59-04: the LF normalization is previewed with both blobs and read back as
  LF bytes; a binary file with NUL bytes, an executable bit (POSIX) and a
  symbolic link (its target bytes, never followed) are exact; a changed LFS
  path (raw bytes over a committed pointer) is refused natively as
  `filter_managed` naming the path, and when left out P's pointer stays;
  external preparation's patch, applied at P with ordinary `git apply
  --cached --binary`, reproduces the prepared tree and names the path the
  reviewer's LFS tooling must process; required signing is refused as
  `signing_required` rather than committed unsigned; SHA-1 and SHA-256
  candidates build and read back in their own format (provider admission of
  a format is BPR-1175's). The core course covers normalization refusals,
  metadata validation, undecided improvements and unchanged trees.

Limits: normalization is the only transformation; no filter, signing program
or hook is ever run, so LFS and signed-commit repositories publish through
external preparation. The patch is bounded at 64 MiB and built in memory.
Blobs are written one process each. The private store keeps every object it
was given; nothing prunes it yet.

Owed native runs: Linux
`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle_candidate$|^review_cycle_candidate$|^review_cycle_outgoing$|^git_process_contract$" --output-on-failure`
(libstdc++ assertions, GCC `-Werror`, the canaries under `/bin/sh`), and the
same on Windows with `build\dev` plus `|^test_network_isolation_manifest$`
(Git for Windows' `sh` for the canaries and the positive control, the private
index path, symbolic links skipped when not permitted, and the new
`review_cycle_candidate` pin, not yet measured contained).

### BPR-1174: finite publication authorization and recovery journal

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_publication.hpp` and
`.cpp` (the plan, its canonical encoding and digest, the journal and its
encoding, `apply_publication`, `next_publication_step`,
`render_publication_preview`), the store's `record_publication`,
`change_publication`, `load_publication` and `publications` with schema 7's
`publications` table (schemas 1–6 migrate in place; removal deletes a
cycle's publications) and the store error's `Publication` refusal,
`src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_publication.hpp` and
`.cpp` (`prepare`, `authorize`, `change`, `recover_after_restart`),
`tests/core/review_cycle_publication_tests.cpp`, the v6 migration and
publication rows in `review_cycle_storage_tests.cpp` (and `publications`
added to the older downgrade fixtures),
`tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_publication_tests.cpp` with its CMake
registration and `review_cycle_publication` isolation pin, and the
domain-model and threat-model paragraphs. The card was moved to Ready and
claimed after its implementation commit `da6445e5e` rather than before; the
content is unchanged by that order.

Design decisions. A plan and its journal are one row, both canonical bytes,
the row's digest checked against the plan on every read; every journal change
goes through the core inside one store transaction with an expected revision,
so a stale writer conflicts and a retried operation replays. The `publish`
decision is the existing cycle policy's: only the reviewer, with the plan's
digest, under the plan's own account. Plans are immutable; an edit is a new
plan, and recording it supersedes older plans nothing was attempted for (a
plan with effects must be recovered or cancelled explicitly). Effects follow
one order — distinct blobs, tree, commit, new branch, pull request — and one
attempt at a time; a confirmed effect is never attempted again. Uncertainty,
a restart or cancellation spends the authorization; an uncertain effect must
be observed by a read before any write, and `not_sent` alone may be retried
under the same authorization. The recovery reads themselves and the provider
catalog are BPR-1175 and BPR-1176's; here the outcomes are supplied by the
courses.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -j 8 -R "^core_|^review_cycle|^sqlite_review_cycle|^qt_review_cycle_view_|^qt_suggestion_editor_|^application_review_cycle|^workspace_source$|^architecture|network_isolation"`
passes 103 of 103 on macOS arm64 at `da6445e5e`, after a full `-k 0` dev build
(only `test_scoped_writing_agent`, BPR-1211, fails). clang-format and
clang-tidy are clean on the new and touched units. A mutation that kept the
authorization across a restart fails `core_review_cycle_publication` at
"restart: uncertain" and `sqlite_review_cycle_storage` at "uncertain, with no
authorization left".

- RC59-06: in `review_cycle_publication` (real checkout, real candidate
  built by the installed-Git catalog, real store) preparing records a plan
  and authorizes nothing; its preview names the cycle revisions, host,
  account and login, owner and repository, new branch, commit, parent, the
  changed path, author and committer, commit message, pull request title and
  body, that private history is not uploaded, and the digest a confirmation
  names. An imported approval and a consumer's grant are `not_reviewer`,
  another account is `authority_changed`; an edited title is another plan
  whose predecessor is superseded and whose old confirmation is refused;
  body, account, base and a new capture each give another digest. Only the
  reviewer's confirmation of the current digest authorizes it, and an account
  switch at any later write is refused. The core course covers the same over
  every field, the plan's exact bytes and a damaged commit identity.
- RC59-07 (this card's share): each attempt is journaled before it is made
  and a retried operation replays without a second record; a lost answer
  makes the effect uncertain, refuses a blind retry and a new confirmation
  until a read observes it, and after that read the remaining writes wait for
  the reviewer again; a restart with the commit in flight (a new store
  connection) turns it uncertain with no authorization left, a second
  recovery changes nothing, and a read that finds no commit lets it be tried
  again only after a fresh confirmation; cancellation keeps the confirmed blob
  and tree and claims nothing undone. The core course adds duplicates,
  ordering, `not_sent` retry, a refused write failing the publication and a
  branch held elsewhere as target drift. Provider reads, uniquely evidenced
  pull request association and live timeout cases are BPR-1176 and BPR-1178's.

Owed native runs: Linux
`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^core_review_cycle_publication$|^sqlite_review_cycle_storage$|^review_cycle_publication$" --output-on-failure`,
and the same on Windows with `build\dev` plus `|^test_network_isolation_manifest$`
(the new `review_cycle_publication` pin, not yet measured contained).

### BPR-1175: exact Git objects and a new branch through native REST

Owned paths: `src/application/include/bpr/application/github_publication_port.hpp`
(the separate catalog: repository, branch, commit, tree, blob and pull
request reads; blob, tree, one-parent commit, new branch and pull request
creation), `src/adapters/github/src/github_publication_contract.hpp` and
`.cpp` (typed requests, exact compilation, validation by recompiling,
strict parsers, ISO dates) and `github_publication_session.cpp` (the
account-bound session), `github_json_preflight.hpp` (the mutation
contract's JSON preflight, moved unchanged apart from configurable element
and value ceilings), the native HTTPS transport's `perform_publication`
(a `publication` request surface, account-binding match, 404 admitted for
publication reads, `Link` allowed, POSTs never replayed through another
proxy), `src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_publisher.hpp`
and `.cpp` (`publish`, `recover`), the exported destination-name
validators in `review_cycle_publication.hpp`,
`tests/github_contract/github_publication_contract_tests.cpp`, the
`publication-course` scenario in `native_https_transport_tests.cpp` and
`verify_native_https_transport.py`,
`tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_publisher_tests.cpp` with
`simulated_github.hpp`, their CMake registrations and the
`review_cycle_publisher` isolation pin, and the domain-model and
threat-model paragraphs.

Design decisions. The catalog is new and separate: no review-mutation
operation, lease or witness is reused, and the old comment publisher gains
nothing. Native publication is admitted for the GitHub.com server contract
only; GHES is refused as `unsupported_host` until it has evidence of its own.
A request is valid only if it recompiles from its own typed request; blobs
are uploaded only when their bytes hash to the named object, and created
objects are admitted only when the server names the locally computed
identity (a commit also with the same tree and parent). Deletions are
explicit `"sha": null` entries over P's tree. The branch is created with
`POST git/refs`; 422 "Reference already exists" is an observation, never an
update. Workflow files are refused because the `workflow` scope is not
observable through the transport (`X-OAuth-Scopes` stays outside its header
allowlist); blobs over 128 KiB and SHA-256 candidates are refused; all three
are prepared externally. The publisher reads capability before any write,
journals each step first, and maps a server refusal to `refused`, a request
that never left to `not_sent`, and anything that may have taken effect
(including a rate-limit answer) to `uncertain`.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -j 8 -R "^core_|^review_cycle|^sqlite_review_cycle|^qt_review_cycle_view_|^application_review_cycle|^workspace_source$|^architecture|network_isolation|^github_|^native_https|^rfc0017_credential_free_native_https$|^live_review_|^staged_review|^git_process_contract$"`
passes 133 of 133 on macOS arm64 at `3419781ad`, after a full `-k 0` dev build
(only `test_scoped_writing_agent`, BPR-1211, fails); the native HTTPS
loopback suite runs every earlier scenario unchanged plus
`publication-course`. clang-format and clang-tidy are clean on the new
units. A mutation that skipped the pre-check for an existing branch fails
`review_cycle_publisher` at "a branch of that name that this plan never made".

- RC59-04 (this card's share): the provider path refuses what it cannot
  carry exactly: SHA-256 candidates are never converted or sent
  (`object_format`, zero writes), workflow files are `protected_path`, a blob
  over the upload bound is `blob_too_large`, a gitlink entry is not
  compiled, bytes that do not hash to the named blob are refused before any
  request, and a server identity other than the local one is refused and
  marked as possibly stored. Symbolic links and executable bits travel as
  their modes. Local preparation's share is BPR-1173's.
- RC59-05 (this card's share: objects and the new branch): the session
  refuses another host, another account and the GHES contract before a
  request exists and sends every request under its own credential context;
  the native loopback course sends the exact repository, parent, base,
  new-branch (404 admitted), blob, tree, commit and reference requests
  through the production transport with the fixture's authorization and
  receives the computed identities, and a second branch creation answers 422
  and is reported as existing, never updated. No update, delete, force, fork,
  merge or close request exists in the catalog, and installed Git has no push
  operation (no credential helper, every protocol off). In
  `review_cycle_publisher` (real candidate, real store, simulated server)
  capability is read first: no push, archived, unadmitted host, P missing,
  base moved, an existing branch and another account each stop with zero
  writes; a full run writes blobs, tree, the one-parent commit and the new
  branch in journal order and never writes twice. A lost tree answer is
  recovered by reading and continued after a fresh confirmation without a
  second tree write; a lost branch answer is recovered as this plan's branch
  only at the planned commit; a branch taken by another actor at creation is
  target drift and never overwritten; a request that never left is tried
  again under the same authorization; a 403 and a rewritten commit identity
  fail the publication before any branch. Pull request creation is
  BPR-1176's share. Live evidence on a designated target is RC59-10's and is
  not run.

Owed native runs: Linux
`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^github_publication_contract$|^native_https_transport_fixture$|^review_cycle_publisher$|^core_review_cycle_publication$" --output-on-failure`
(OpenSSL-backed loopback, libstdc++ assertions, GCC `-Werror`), and on
Windows `ctest --test-dir build\dev -R "^github_publication_contract$|^review_cycle_publisher$|^core_review_cycle_publication$|^test_network_isolation_manifest$" --output-on-failure`
(the loopback suite is not registered on Windows; measure the new
`review_cycle_publisher` pin contained).

### BPR-1176: the resulting pull request, created once and reconciled

Owned paths: the pull request step of
`src/application/src/review_cycle_publisher.cpp` (`pull_request`,
`match_pull_request`, the `pull_request_exists` limit, pull request recovery
and the link's drift rule), the `AfterPull` hook and pull request faults of
`tests/workspace_contract/simulated_github.hpp`, the pull request course in
`review_cycle_publisher_tests.cpp`, the pull request reads and creation added
to the native loopback `publication-course`, and the domain-model
paragraph. The REST contract for pull requests is BPR-1175's catalog.

Design decisions. A pull request candidate is one whose head is the plan's
new branch in the destination repository (owner and repository compared
without case) and whose base is the plan's base; it is this plan's only at the
planned commit under the plan's login. Before creating, every candidate,
open or closed, is read, and any stops the run: a pull request the plan never
asked for is the reviewer's to associate explicitly (BPR-1177), never
adopted or duplicated. A 422 is read again. After a lost answer, recovery
associates exactly one of ours and nothing else; several candidates stay
uncertain. The created pull request is read again by number; a head other
than the planned commit, another base branch, or a base head other than the
target the plan saw is recorded as drift on the link rather than hidden.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -j 8 -R "^core_|^review_cycle|^sqlite_review_cycle|^qt_review_cycle_view_|^application_review_cycle|^workspace_source$|^architecture|network_isolation|^github_|^native_https|^rfc0017_credential_free_native_https$|^live_review_|^staged_review|^git_process_contract$"`
passes 133 of 133 on macOS arm64 at `fab189080`, after a full `-k 0` dev build
(only `test_scoped_writing_agent`, BPR-1211, fails). clang-format and
clang-tidy are clean. A mutation that associated a pull request whenever one
of several candidates was ours fails `review_cycle_publisher` at "ambiguous
matches stay uncertain".

- RC59-05 (this card's share: the pull request): the native loopback course
  reads the pull requests for the exact head and base (the query
  `state=all&head=octo-org%3Abpr%2Fharden&base=main&per_page=100`), creates one
  and reads it again by number through the production transport. In
  `review_cycle_publisher` the pull request is created only after the branch
  is confirmed, with the confirmed title and draft choice, and a linked
  publication never creates another.
- RC59-07 (this card's share): a lost create answer followed by a restart is
  recovered as the one pull request of ours, never recreated; two candidates
  after a lost answer stay uncertain; another actor's pull request opened
  just before ours makes the create refused and is recorded as foreign
  (target drift) with no second pull request; a pull request already there
  stops the step with no write; a 403 fails it; another actor pushing to the
  head right after creation is recorded as drift on the link; a restart
  between recording the attempt and making it reads nothing, and the pull
  request is created once after a fresh confirmation. The fault-injection
  matrix across every boundary is BPR-1178's.
- RC59-10: **pending**. It needs one separately approved disposable GitHub
  target course per claimed host. The owner has named no safe target, so no
  branch, commit or pull request has been created on GitHub, GHES is not
  admitted for native publication, and the card is Blocked on
  `safe-github-publication-target`. Fixture and loopback evidence here is not
  live qualification.

Owed native runs: Linux
`ctest --test-dir build/dev -R "^review_cycle_publisher$|^native_https_transport_fixture$|^github_publication_contract$" --output-on-failure`,
and Windows `ctest --test-dir build\dev -R "^review_cycle_publisher$|^github_publication_contract$|^test_network_isolation_manifest$" --output-on-failure`.

Owed live course (not authorized): on an owner-designated disposable
GitHub.com repository, publish one plan end to end (blob, tree, commit whose
server identity must equal the local one — including whether a non-zero
author offset survives the commit endpoint — deletion by null identity, new
branch, draft and ready pull requests), a lost-answer recovery at the branch
and at the pull request, and a foreign-branch refusal; then the same on a
designated GHES host before GHES is admitted.

new pull request exists end to end — outgoing plan over an explicit P, a
squashed candidate built and read back in a private store, a finite plan
whose digest only the reviewer confirms, a journal written before every
effect, and the GitHub catalog through blobs, tree, commit, new branch and
pull request with read-first recovery — and it is exercised against real
checkouts, the real store, an in-memory server and the production native
transport on loopback. It has not published anything to GitHub (no safe
target), the window's Publish as PR preview and local continuity are
BPR-1177's, and the crash and drift matrix is BPR-1178's.

### Windows station (publication) — bpr-main-win, 2026-09-30

Windows 11 Home 10.0.26200, MSVC 19.51, Qt 6.11.2, `build/windows-dev`
Debug with warnings as errors, at `e9e65eab`.

**Build.** One MSVC failure. In `installed_git_publication::source_objects`
a lambda was named `small`, which the Windows SDK defines as a macro for
`char` (`rpcndr.h`). `9af5eb2d` renames it `small_text`, and then the whole graph
builds with no `warning` line.

| Row | Result |
|---|---|
| `core_git_objects`, `core_review_cycle_{outgoing,candidate,publication}` | 4 of 4 pass |
| `sqlite_review_cycle_storage` | pass, 2.35 s |
| `review_cycle_outgoing`, `review_cycle_publication`, `review_cycle_publisher` | pass, 5.69, 3.21 and 12.66 s |
| `review_cycle_candidate` | **fails** at `a link is its target bytes, never followed`. This machine can make symbolic links, so the course adds `docs-link`. The Windows workspace source cannot read a link (`read_link` is `unsupported`), so the link is captured unavailable. The candidate leaves it out, and the preview names it nowhere. Carded as [[BPR-1244]] |
| `github_publication_contract`, `git_process_contract` | pass, 0.03 and 51.29 s |
| `test_network_isolation_manifest` | fails for the same 47 older rows ([[BPR-1237]]). It names none of this milestone's rows |

**The rest of `review_cycle_candidate` passes on Windows.** In a probe, not
committed, only the link check was bypassed. Then both the SHA-1 and SHA-256
passes printed `candidate built in the private store, read back exactly,
checkout untouched, no canary`, and the row passed. That includes the Git for
Windows canary. The product never ran the checkout's planted filter or
hooks. The positive control, `git add` in the checkout, did run the
`canary.sh` filter through Git for Windows' shell. So the canary is live on
Windows, and its silence under the product is evidence.

**The alternates separator.** `source_objects` takes `;` as the
`GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES` separator under `Q_OS_WIN`. That macro is
defined in this translation unit, which includes Qt. A drive letter's `:` is
therefore not a separator on Windows, and the check refuses only a `;`, a
quote or a newline in the object path. The candidate, publication and
publisher courses read the checkout's objects through it on Windows, and pass.

**The four new uncontained pins are confirmed.** Run by hand through
`better_pull_review_network_sandbox.exe`, contained, `review_cycle_outgoing`,
`_candidate`, `_publication` and `_publisher` each stop at `canonical: Access is
denied.` on the Git executable (the BPR-0812 shape). `9f8c34cc` records it in the
pin comment. The logs are station records in
`C:\Users\mikem\bpr-main-helpers\run\station-0929\` (`pub.log` and
`.xml`), and are not committed.

### BPR-1176: the RC59-10 live course on MikeMarcin/BPR-Test — 2026-09-30

On 2026-09-30 the owner designated github.com `MikeMarcin/BPR-Test` for
live work: "You can do anything you need to with the MikeMarcin/BPR-Test
github repo". GHES has no designated target.

Environment: macOS 26.6.2 arm64, Apple clang 21.0.0, `build/dev` (Debug)
configured with `-DBPR_BUILD_LIVE_PUBLICATION_COURSE=ON` for the run and
back to `OFF` afterwards, Apple Git 2.50.1 (`/usr/bin/git`) as the
publication catalog's Git, `gh` 2.101.0 signed in as `MikeMarcin` (admin on
the repository; no workflow files on any of its branches). Product source at
`634e8ac6c`, course at `4ce2d0529`.

`python3 tests/scripts/run_live_publication_course.py --tool build/dev/tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_live_publication --git /usr/bin/git`
passes (run stamp `20260930T102712Z`). The driver clones the repository,
pushes a base branch with `README.md`, `src/parse.cpp` and `src/old.cpp`
(`7ee7dd91a`) and a second branch at the same commit made by `git push` (the
"foreign" branch), and hands the credential to the tool on standard input.
The tool runs the shipped store, installed-Git catalog, publication host,
`review_cycle_publisher`, `github_publication_session` and the production
native HTTPS transport (system trust and proxy). R0 is the base; R1 changes
`src/parse.cpp`, adds `src/validate.cpp` and deletes `src/old.cpp`. Author
`+05:30`, committer `-07:00`.

| Case | What GitHub did | Result |
|---|---|---|
| One plan end to end, ready PR | blobs, tree with `src/old.cpp` as `"sha": null`, the commit and the new branch were each named by the server with the locally computed identity (`8a741d1fb`, tree `757b718be`); PR [#12](https://github.com/MikeMarcin/BPR-Test/pull/12) linked, not drifted, not draft, author `MikeMarcin`; a second publish is `unauthorized` with no write | Pass |
| Draft PR | [#13](https://github.com/MikeMarcin/BPR-Test/pull/13) created with `draft: true` | Pass |
| Lost branch answer | the branch was created, its answer dropped: `uncertain`, no blind retry; `recover` read it at the planned commit (`branch_created`); after a fresh confirmation the run made PR [#14](https://github.com/MikeMarcin/BPR-Test/pull/14) with no second branch write | Pass |
| Lost PR answer, then restart | the PR was created, its answer dropped: `uncertain`, no blind retry; after `recover_after_restart`, `recover` associated exactly [#15](https://github.com/MikeMarcin/BPR-Test/pull/15), one PR for the head | Pass |
| Foreign branch (pushed by `git` before the plan) | `limited` / `branch_exists`, zero writes | Pass |
| Branch raced between the read and the write | another create put the name at P first; GitHub answered ours 422; recorded `foreign`, phase `target_drift`, the branch still at P | Pass |
| PR raced between the read and the write | another PR for the head ([#16](https://github.com/MikeMarcin/BPR-Test/pull/16)) opened first; GitHub answered ours 422; re-read, recorded `foreign`, `target_drift`; one PR for the head | Pass |

A second client confirms it. `git fetch` of the ready branch gives
`8a741d1fb` with tree `757b718be` and the one parent `7ee7dd91a`. The raw
commit reads `author Ada Lovelace <ada@example.invalid> 1790000000 +0530` and
`committer BPR live course <bpr-live-course@example.invalid> 1790000060 -0700`.
So the commit endpoint keeps a non-zero offset exactly, and the server's
identity equals the local one only because it does. `src/old.cpp` is absent
from the tree and `src/validate.cpp` present, so a null identity deletes.
`gh api` reads #12 as not draft, head `8a741d1fb` on the ready branch, base
the base branch at `7ee7dd91a`, author `MikeMarcin`, and #13 as draft.

The first attempt (stamp `20260930T102136Z`) stopped at the lost-PR case on a
fault in the course, not the product: the branch write consumed the fault
meant for the pull request, so PR #6 was created and answered normally. Its
earlier cases passed the same way (PRs #3–#6). The course now takes only a
fault meant for the operation at hand. A run at the uncommitted course
(`20260930T102240Z`, PRs #7–#11) passed every case; the evidence above is
the run at the committed course.

Nothing was deleted, closed or force-updated on GitHub. These objects are
left on BPR-Test as evidence, each under `bpr-rc59-10/<stamp>/`:

- `20260930T102712Z` (the recorded run): branches `base`, `foreign`,
  `ready`, `draft`, `lost-branch`, `lost-pull`, `raced-branch`,
  `raced-pull`; PRs #12–#16.
- `20260930T102240Z`: the same eight branches; PRs #7–#11.
- `20260930T102136Z` (stopped by the course fault): branches `base`,
  `foreign`, `ready`, `draft`, `lost-branch`, `lost-pull`; PRs #3–#6.

- RC59-10 (this card's share): **pass for GitHub.com**, the one host native
  publication claims. GHES stays refused as `unsupported_host`, so it claims
  nothing; admitting it needs a designated GHES target and the same course.
  The course drives the application host and adapters from a harness, not

### BPR-1156: syntax colours in Suggest change

macOS arm64, Apple clang, Qt 6.11.2, `build/dev` Debug, at `f3327b74b`.

The owner rejected `BPR-1156-composer-layout-v001` (RC61-04) at session event
713: "There's no syntax highlighting." The editor and the before/after preview
now take their colours from the same packaged classifier
(`adapters::syntax::packaged_syntax_classifier` over the shared definition
catalog) and the same role colours and span-to-format rule
(`code_sample_painter.hpp`) as the diff panes. There is no second highlighter.

- The core decides the classified bytes. `core::splice_text` puts the proposal
  into its whole file, so a selection that starts inside a comment or string
  reads the way the file does.
- The host performs the classification. `review_cycle_desktop` holds the
  composer's in-memory texts by blob identity and classifies them on the
  appearance effect lane, the lane the panes' classifier already runs on,
  through its own `syntax_classification_application_host` (four cache
  entries, at most two classifications at a time, latest text wins on each
  side).
- Qt presents. The composer adopts only spans of the text it still shows. The
  editor paints each glyph in its role colour and keeps a line's colours only
  while the line's content is unchanged.

`ctest --test-dir build/dev -j 6 -R "^core_suggestion_text_editing$|^core_review_cycle_(feedback|suggestion|tabs)$|^qt_suggestion_editor_|^qt_review_cycle_view_|^review_cycle_workbench$|^application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path$|^application_review_round_trip_composition_contract$|^syntax_"`
passes 12 of 12. The composed smoke rows `composed_application_hosts`,
`qt_composed_home_sign_in`, `qt_composed_github_scale_100`,
`application_github_public_path_github_com`,
`application_diff_workbench_public_path` and
`application_workbench_composition_contract` pass. Before the pins were
resealed, `architecture_boundaries`, `architecture_boundary_contract` and
`live_mutation_isolation` passed too. The full dev graph builds.
clang-format is clean on the touched files, and the project's clang-tidy
script is clean on the touched units.

- Rendered state. `qt_suggestion_editor_1_0`/`_2_0` grab the editor and the
  preview. No pixel carries the keyword colour before a classification
  arrives. After one is adopted, the keyword and number colours are painted.
  Spans of other or earlier text are refused, and a line that only moves
  keeps its colours. The preview's removed row carries the control-flow
  format after its marker.
- The public path. `application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path` drives the
  real window and the real classifier, with no stub classification.
  `check(` and then `validate(` are painted in the function colour, and
  `return` carries the control-flow colour in both preview rows.
- Negative control. With the editor's colour lookup disabled, the Qt course
  fails at "the editor paints the keyword and the number in their role
  colours".

The round-trip composition contract's full-application and production-manifest
pins are resealed in the same commit, with the review recorded beside them.
The mutation pin is unchanged.

The candidate is frozen at `/Users/jaeger/bpr-suggestion-syntax-review.w9G5mr`
(app sha256 `6c3f20c00f5e87bb71d379352554c02e867f8d5e75e1fc9f968949b18dbef85c`,
a copy of the dev bundle; an eight-second offscreen trial start ran clean).
It is asked as `BPR-1156-composer-layout-v002`. The v001 protection is
released and its verdict kept. `BPR-1156-editor-gestures-v001` is unchanged.

Not covered: the cycle page's own source pane is still plain text, and it is
BPR-1157's. The colours are checked offscreen, not in a native cocoa render.
Linux and Windows runs of the changed rows are owed.

### BPR-1156: Suggest change in the main pane, from the caret (2026-10-01)

The owner rejected `BPR-1156-composer-layout-v003` at session event 742 with
a product decision, recorded as the
[owner amendment in RFC-BPR-0055](../rfcs/RFC-BPR-0055-source-feedback-and-exact-suggested-edits.md#owner-amendment-2026-10-01-suggest-change-edits-the-file-in-place)
and amending RC55-04 and RC61-04. `da459c4e1` implements it: Suggest change
from the caret and the source pane's context menu, the whole file edited in the
main pane, one exact suggestion per separate changed run of whole lines on one

### BPR-1161: Compose feedback, Share feedback and delivery observations (2026-10-02)

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_share.hpp` and `.cpp`
(routes, Compose edits, delivery admission) with `channel_permission` in
`review_cycle_policy`; `src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_share.hpp`
and `.cpp`, `preview_cycle_round` in `review_cycle_round`, `current_note_items`
in `review_cycle_verification`, the runtime's share calls, the workbench's
`compose`, `edit_composition`, `share` and `resolve_replace`, and the share
view in `review_cycle_projection.hpp`; the store's `record_delivery` and
`deliveries` with schema 8's `deliveries` table; `src/adapters/qt/include/bpr/qt/review_cycle_share_page.hpp`
and `.cpp` in the cycle page's centre; the platform clipboard, save chooser
and dispatch in `src/app/src/review_cycle_desktop.cpp`; and the tests named
below.

Compatibility decision. The pack names a Share feedback menu; the page offers
the same choices as buttons, Copy to Clipboard and Save to File first, so
neither is behind a menu. Use in external agent and Send to Agent are listed
only when the host reaches them; this build reaches neither (no CLI or
adapter yet), so they do not appear, not even disabled. The external route's
invocation (`bpr cycle round read RC-n --round Fm`) is shown only behind that
capability and is exercised in the host and Qt courses with it injected.
A source's classification is not yet observed, so a bound folder or checkout
is `unclassified`; the shipped policy is the default one.

Commands at `7a385389c`, macOS arm64, dev preset (Debug), offscreen:
`ctest --test-dir build/dev -j6 -R "qt_review_cycle_view|application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path|review_cycle_|sqlite_review_cycle|architecture_boundaries|test_network_isolation_manifest|core_review_cycle|qt_suggestion_editor"`
passes 49 of 49; the `core-only` preset passes its 22 `core_review_cycle`
rows; clang-format is clean and clang-tidy is clean on every changed
translation unit (one pre-existing `bugprone-optional-value-conversion` in
`review_cycle_verification.cpp` fixed). Ten negative controls each fail the row
meant to catch them: a route ignoring policy and `copied` admitted from any
channel (`core_review_cycle_share`); a refused route shared anyway, an
unconfirmed clipboard recorded as copied and the clipboard budget ignored
(`review_cycle_workbench`); a refused route hidden and a save worded as
"Delivered" (`qt_review_cycle_view_1_0`); the store recording without the
core's admission (`sqlite_review_cycle_storage`); and the Copy dispatch
saving instead (`application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path`).

- RC56-07: the core admits copied only from the clipboard, saved only to a
  local file under its name (never a folder), retrieved only through the local
  interface or a terminal, accepted only by an adapter and acknowledged only
  through an adapter or the local interface, each for this cycle's round and
  digest. The host records copied only when the platform clipboard hands the
  same text back (otherwise uncertain); the page lists each kind in its own
  words, repeats counted, under "None of them means the round was received,
  read, acted on or accepted"; sharing changes no note's status. Observations
  persist across a restart (desktop course).
- RC56-10: every route's decision equals `authorize`'s reviewer export for
  every classification, channel and setting (225 rows), and a granted
  harness's retrieval gets the same answer as the desktop's external route.
  A channel denied for the clipboard copies, records and freezes nothing,
  says so, writes no file instead, and leaves Save decided on its own; a
  plain folder of unknown origin gets the default allowances.
- RC61-05 (automated share; owner judgment pending): Compose opens the
  first draft with every open note and previews the exact document; one click
  of Copy to Clipboard freezes and copies it; Save writes the same bytes under
  the round's own name to the file chosen; a dismissed save dialog writes
  nothing; Save as Bundle carries the same round; other bytes at the file are
  replaced only on the reviewer's word; an edit after sharing is an amendment
  (F2 amends F1, which stays as shared); order, action and goal are the
  reviewer's; no prompt per note and no AI setup. A round over the clipboard's
  16 MiB is refused with its full size and counts, nothing frozen or cut.
- RC61-06 (automated share; owner judgment pending): the page fits the 900
  pixel window with every route, each Tab stop on it shows the focus ring,
  Escape and Back leave it, and the external route, when reachable and
  allowed, shows the exact round's invocation, records no delivery and starts
  nothing.

### BPR-1161: a note's code aligned, in the code font, and coloured (2026-10-03)

The owner rejected `BPR-1161-compose-share-v002` at event 895, decision
`ab7105e5…`:

> Something weird here these lines arent aligned, or syntax highlighted, is it usiing the proper monospace font?

The screenshot attached to the decision is the v002 media render
`cycle-share-sheet-code.png`. Its frozen root's `home/` is empty and it has no
`owner-app.log`, so the owner judged the render, not a launch. That render
came from the offscreen course at `324c17826`.

Cause, measured on the v002 render and on one from main with the same course.
The table gives the first code ink of each excerpt row, in pixels:

| Row | v002 (`324c17826`) | main before this change (`4c8bcda0b`) |
|---|---|---|
| Context rows, column 0 | 405 | 418 |
| Removed row `17 -`, column 0 | 408 | 418 |
| Added row `17 +`, column 0 | 410 | 418 |
| Tab-indented removed row `14 -` | 423 | 447 |
| Tab-indented added row `14 +` | 425 | 447 |

- **A proportional face.** At v002 the excerpt took the appearance's code font
  raw. Offscreen, and on Windows, its generic `Monospace` family resolved to
  a proportional face, so the different widths of `-`, `+` and the bullet
  moved the code 3 to 5 pixels. Fixed on main by `07f853492`
  (`fixed_pitch_code_font`) and by BPR-1270's bundled JetBrains Mono NL.
- **Tab stops counted from the row's first column.** At v002 a tab went to
  the next stop counted from the line number, so one tab indented 3 cells,
  not 4. Fixed on main by `07f853492`, which expands tabs from the code's
  first column. The negative control below brings the misalignment back.
- **No syntax colours.** Still missing on main. Added by this change.

Change (`3b5f405be`):

- `note_code_view` builds two documents: the file's rows before the suggested
  changes (context, noted and removed rows) and after them (context, noted and
  added rows), each row's code as shown. When the view is shown it asks for
  them through `SyntaxWanted`, and `adopt_syntax` colours each row from its
  own document through `code_syntax_format_ranges_at` and the panes' role
  colours. The gutter stays in the placeholder colour.
- The sheet forwards the requests (`review_cycle_share_sheet::SyntaxWanted`)
  and hands classifications back (`adopt_code_syntax`). The larger view does
  the same with the file path.
- `review_cycle_desktop` classifies them on a second
  `syntax_classification_application_host`. It uses the panes' packaged
  definitions and effect lane, one text at a time, at most 16 waiting (the
  newest kept). A sheet of open notes therefore never crowds out the file pane
  or Suggest change. Shutdown and quiescence include it.
- `source_syntax_request` moved into `review_cycle_share_sheet.hpp`, which
  `review_cycle_view.hpp` already includes.

Checks (macOS arm64, Debug, offscreen, private build tree `build/bpr1161`):

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `qt_review_cycle_view_1_0` and `_2_0`, new `aligns_and_colours_a_notes_code` | Pass. At 1×, the first ink of each row's `X` was 47, 76, 76, 76, 104, 104 and 76: column 0 on a context row; column 4 on the noted, removed and added rows by tab, by spaces and by both; column 8 by two tabs and by eight spaces. The stop was 28.75 px, equal to `code_tab_stop_distance()`. Both views use the profile's code font at its size, resolved to JetBrains Mono NL. `return` is in the control-flow colour and `X` in the text colour on every row kind (local role/text rule). Spans of another text are refused. The same holds at 2× and 1.25× |
| In-test control: the same rows in the proportional system font | Columns found apart |
| `application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path` | Pass. Through the real composition and classifier, the note's code and the whole file are in the bundled family, with `return` in control flow and `check` in the function colour |
| Negative controls, each rebuilt and reverted | The colour offset off by one cell fails "return in the control-flow colour"; tabs left to the widget's stops fail "column 4 is one pixel" (76 against 61); the view's own fixed font in place of the profile's fails "the profile's code font"; the composition not handing spans back fails "the note's code in the panes' syntax colours" |
| The BPR-1161 selection: `core_review_cycle_share`, `_policy`, `sqlite_review_cycle_storage`, `review_cycle_{workbench,round,export,bundle,verification}`, `qt_review_cycle_view_*`, `qt_suggestion_editor_*`, `application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path`, `application_review_tour_composition_*`, `architecture_boundaries`, `test_network_isolation_manifest` | 18 of 18 pass |
| All `qt_` rows | 118 of 118 pass |
| `format_check.py` on the changed files; `tidy.py --paths` on the four changed translation units; `git diff --check` | Clean. The one tidy finding, a pre-existing `std::endl`, is fixed |

## External consumers milestone

### BPR-1167: the agent-free headless cycle CLI (2026-10-02)

Owned paths: `src/app/src/cycle_cli.hpp` and `.cpp` (the `bpr cycle`
commands, their output and typed errors), `src/app/src/cycle_session.hpp` and
`.cpp` (the command's composition), `src/app/src/profile_admission.hpp` and
`.cpp` and `src/app/src/terminal_text.hpp` and `.cpp` (moved out of the tour
runtime and `tour_cli.cpp`, now shared by both families), `tour_main.cpp`'s
dispatch; `core::parse_cycle_name`, `parse_round_name`,
`parse_revision_name`, `parse_item_name`, `core::line_span_bytes` and
`core::bound_to` (the bound-root rule Open Project now calls);
`application::export_frozen_round` and `export_frozen_round_to_file` with
the runtime's `export_round_selected` and `export_round_to_file_selected`;
`src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_words.hpp` and `.cpp`
(moved from the workbench, which now calls them); the tests named below; and
[the developer page](../development/cycle-cli.md).

Compatibility decisions. The CLI contract's commands are reconciled with the
live tree as `capabilities`, `list`, `open`, `show`, `capture`, `note`,
`review`, `freeze`, `export`, `compare` and `response import`; `round read`
and its pages and grants are BPR-1169's and are not offered, and
`publication` is not offered here. The first capture is `R1`, as the desktop
and the wire names say (the contract's examples write `R0`). The terminal
composes `application::review_cycle_runtime`, the store, the workspace source
and the export destination the desktop's workbench composes, not the
workbench itself, which is the desktop's presentation sequencing; the
open-or-create rule is shared through `core::bound_to` and the words through
`review_cycle_words`. `--lines` takes `FIRST:LAST` as the contract writes it.
Exports go by `local_file` or `terminal_output`, each decided by
`core::authorize`; `--output -` records `retrieved` only when it wrote the
exact bytes to a pipe or file, and shows a terminal neutralized text it does
not record. Machine edits and every freeze name the draft revision read
(`--expected-revision`), a stricter form of the contract's expected revisions.
A note is bounded by the core's 16 KiB reviewer body.

Commands at `0fcb6c4d1`, macOS arm64, `build/dev-tour-cli` (the `dev` preset
with `-DBPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI=ON`, Debug, Homebrew LLVM): a full build, then
`ctest --test-dir build/dev-tour-cli -j6 -R "^cycle_cli_public_path$|^tour_cli_|^skill_pr_tour_package$|^core_review_cycle|^review_cycle_|^sqlite_review_cycle|^qt_review_cycle_view_|^qt_suggestion_editor_|^application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path$|^application_profile_tour_handoff_public_path$|^application_profile_activation_public_path$|^architecture_boundaries$|^test_network_isolation_manifest$|^workspace_source$"`
passes 59 of 59; the `core-only` preset passes its 22 `core_review_cycle`
rows; clang-format is clean and clang-tidy is clean on every changed
translation unit. The tests-disabled `tour-cli` preset (Release,
`BPR_BUILD_QT=OFF`, `BUILD_TESTING=OFF`) was built and its `TourCLI`
component installed to a scratch prefix; `tests/scripts/cycle_cli_tests.py`
passes against the installed `bin/bpr`, directly and under the macOS
network-deny sandbox runner (`scripts/run_network_isolated_test.py`), and
`otool -L` lists only QtCore and QtNetwork beside the system libraries.
`0fcb6c4d1` landed as `2a79d08eb` after a rebase over two BPR-1161 records;
no source or test path differs.

Ten negative controls each fail the row meant to catch them:
`cycle_cli_public_path` fails when the session opens a profile another
process holds (a second writer), when a note without a terminal reads
standard input, when a JSON call writes to standard error, when `freeze`
freezes without the revision read, when `--schema-version 2` is accepted and
when output is JSON by default; `review_cycle_export` fails when an export
ignores the channel decision; `core_review_cycle_round` when `RC-0` or a
leading zero parses, `core_review_cycle_feedback` when a line span keeps its
ending, and `core_review_cycle_workspace` when `bound_to` ignores the root's
kind.

- RC58-01: `cycle_cli_public_path` runs every command as a real process with
  no HOME, account, provider, credential or desktop, and with
  `QT_QPA_PLATFORM` naming a platform plugin that cannot exist, so any GUI
  start would fail: a fixture folder is opened and captured as R1, noted on
  lines, a whole file and through the trusted editor, reviewed, frozen,
  exported to a file, a bundle and standard output, recaptured, compared and
  answered with a structured response. The same course passes against the
  tests-disabled installed `bpr`, which links no GUI, widget or display
  library. Nothing is shelled out to the desktop: the terminal composes the
  desktop's runtime, store, workspace source and export destination, the
  desktop course still passes with the workbench calling the shared
  `bound_to` and words, and `review_cycle_export` drives the new runtime
  exports host-side (a named round after its amendment, a bundle, a denied
  channel, an unknown round, a confirmed replacement recorded as `saved`).
  A profile another `bpr` process holds is refused with
  `capability_unavailable`, and nothing it was asked to write appears.
- RC58-02: default output is prose and short lists (asserted not JSON);
  `--json` writes exactly one `bpr.cycle.cli-result.v1` line with the
  envelope's seven fields and nothing on standard error; a human refusal
  writes nothing to standard output and says why, with a `Next:` command, on
  standard error. Typed codes and exits are asserted for
  `unsupported_version`, `unsupported_option`, `unsupported_command`,
  `invalid_input`, `not_found` (with `bpr cycle open .`),
  `interaction_required`, `stale_revision`, `conflict` (with the existing
  file's digest), `over_limit` and `capability_unavailable`. Without a
  terminal, `note` without text refuses even with text waiting on standard
  input, and `freeze` and scripted `review` changes refuse without the
  revision read; in a pseudo-terminal the editor note is saved, Enter at the
  freeze prompt freezes nothing (exit 130) and the word `freeze` freezes.

Limits: target ambiguity between two active cycles bound to one root is
asserted by the rule (`bound_to`) and the code path, not by a course, because
Open Project never makes a second such cycle. The pseudo-terminal half of the
course runs on POSIX only. Forwarding to a running desktop is BPR-1168's; the
refusal is this card's.

Owed station runs, not gating (the card has no `platforms` requirement). The
`dev` presets leave `BPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI` off, so each station configures a
second binary directory. Linux:

```sh
cmake --preset dev -B build/dev-tour-cli -DBPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI=ON && cmake --build build/dev-tour-cli && ctest --test-dir build/dev-tour-cli -j4 --output-on-failure -R "^cycle_cli_public_path$|^tour_cli_|^skill_pr_tour_package$|^core_review_cycle_(round|feedback|workspace|share)$|^review_cycle_(export|workbench)$|^application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path$|^architecture_boundaries$"
cmake --preset tour-cli && cmake --build --preset tour-cli && cmake --install build/tour-cli --component TourCLI --prefix "$PWD/build/tour-cli-install" && python3 tests/scripts/cycle_cli_tests.py build/tour-cli-install/bin/bpr
```

Windows (the pseudo-terminal half and the library listing skip there):

```powershell
cmake --preset windows-dev -B build/windows-dev-tour-cli -DBPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI=ON; cmake --build build/windows-dev-tour-cli --config Debug; ctest --test-dir build/windows-dev-tour-cli -C Debug -j4 --output-on-failure -R "^cycle_cli_public_path$|^tour_cli_|^core_review_cycle_(round|feedback|workspace|share)$|^review_cycle_(export|workbench)$|^application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path$|^architecture_boundaries$|^test_network_isolation_manifest$"
```

`cycle_cli_public_path` is registered contained. If Windows contained stops
at the profile root or the cycle store, as its sibling store courses do
(`dos-volume-name`), pin it `WINDOWS_UNCONTAINED` with the measured line in
`scripts/verify_test_network_isolation.py` rather than weakening the course.

### BPR-1168: one owner across the desktop and `bpr cycle` (2026-10-02)

Candidate `7e5a093cc`. Owned paths: `core::cycle_command`
(`src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_command.hpp` and `.cpp`: the finite
command registry, which commands write or touch the round draft, operation
identities and `admit_owner_command`); the owner channel's `cycle` operation
and `cycle_owner_request`/`cycle_owner_response`
(`application/tour_owner_channel.hpp`, `adapters/sqlite/src/tour_owner_codec.cpp`)
and protocol 3, `version_mismatch` and the Windows named pipe
(`adapters/sqlite/src/profile_activation.cpp`, `tour_owner_channel.hpp`);
`src/app/src/cycle_commands.hpp` and `.cpp` (the commands, moved out of
`cycle_cli.cpp` and run by the terminal and the owner alike),
`cycle_cli.cpp` (parsing, inputs, prompts and the choice between running here
and forwarding), `cycle_session` (an owner's session and operation seeds),
`cycle_owner_service.hpp` and `.cpp`; `github_application`'s
`desktop_cycle_owner_route`, `cycle_draft_editors` and `cycle_written` with the
profile's `CycleOwnerEffects` lane; `review_cycle_desktop`'s draft queries; the
workbench's `observe_external_change`, `saving_draft`, the note retry and the
kept goal; the share page's composing and unsaved goal; the cycle store's busy
timeout; the tests and documents named below.

Compatibility decisions. The contract's "already admitted same-user
authenticated owner channel" is the RFC-BPR-0045 tour owner channel, extended
with one `cycle` operation (protocol 3; either side of another version is
refused before a payload is sent). Its operation registry is the CLI's own
command table, now in the core; a forwarded request carries a command and that
command's options only, never the caller's profile, data root or output form,
and the owner admits nothing else. Commands run on a session of the owner's
store beside its windows (each window already has its own connection), not
through a window's runtime, whose selection is the reviewer's. The contract's
"request IDs" are `--operation-id`, which the result envelope already named
`operation_id`; store operations derive from it, so a repeat is returned, not
reapplied, by the store's existing receipts, and the owner also returns its
first answer to an exact repeat. Review Cycles have no undo in either interface,
so "cross-interface undo" is exercised as reversal: a counter-change from the
other interface under the revision read. "Dirty-editor flush" is the round
draft's goal, the one draft text a window holds unsaved; a note being written
is not part of any cycle state until saved, and is kept, not flushed. Second-
OS-user refusal is the transport's peer check (`getpeereid`/`SO_PEERCRED`, the
pipe's client process token); a same-user course cannot exercise it.

Commands at `7e5a093cc` (tested on its parent tree plus the change, rebased
over `551551c9` without conflict), macOS arm64, `build/dev-tour-cli`: a full
build, then
`ctest --test-dir build/dev-tour-cli -j6 -R "^cycle_cli_public_path$|^tour_cli_|^skill_pr_tour_package$|^core_review_cycle|^review_cycle_|^sqlite_review_cycle|^qt_review_cycle_view_|^qt_suggestion_editor_|^application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path$|^application_profile_tour_handoff_public_path$|^application_profile_cycle_handoff_public_path$|^application_profile_activation_public_path$|^application_profile_owner_admission_public_path$|^sqlite_tour_owner_channel$|^sqlite_profile_activation$|^architecture_boundaries$|^test_network_isolation_manifest$|^workspace_source$|^application_review_tour_|^sqlite_review_tour"`
passes 80 of 80; after the rebase, the manifest, structure contract,
`application_review_round_trip_composition_contract` (resealed),
`architecture_boundaries` and the new rows pass again. The `core-only` preset
passes its 23 `core_review_cycle` rows. clang-format is clean; clang-tidy is
clean on every changed translation unit (its only findings are eight existing
ones in `review_tour_codec.hpp`). The tests-disabled `tour-cli` preset was
built and installed, and `tests/scripts/cycle_cli_tests.py` passes against the
installed `bin/bpr`. The Windows branches of `profile_activation.cpp` and
`tour_owner_channel_tests.cpp` compile under `x86_64-w64-mingw32-g++ -std=c++23
-fsyntax-only` with Windows headers; MSVC and a Windows run are owed.

`application_profile_cycle_handoff_public_path` failed once in nine runs under
`-j6` load, at 63 s, the first run after relinking; the output was not kept.
Eight further runs under `-j6` and `-j8` load and the isolated runs pass in
about 7 s. Its owner-line wait is now 120 s, as a cold first launch of a
freshly linked desktop can be slow on macOS; a recurrence should be kept and
traced, not retried away.

Four negative controls each fail the row meant to catch them:
`application_profile_cycle_handoff_public_path` fails when the draft guard
always serves (a freeze against the revision before the window's goal
succeeds), when windows are not told of a write (the window never shows the
terminal's note) and when the owner forgets its answers (a repeated request is
not answered alike); `review_cycle_workbench` fails when the window does not
retry its note on the cycle as it now is. The busy-timeout fix was found by the
course itself: before it, a forwarded `review` beside a window's write was
refused as `unsupported_version`.

- RC58-05: `application_profile_cycle_handoff_public_path` drives a real
  desktop owner (the production profile desktop with its lease and command
  endpoint) and the shipped `bpr` as separate processes. With no desktop the
  terminal opens the cycle and notes it itself; with the desktop open nothing
  else can take the lease, and `show`, `note`, a working-folder lookup,
  `review`, `freeze` and `export` reach the owner and land in its store, its
  window showing them. Four concurrent callers each get their own answer,
  bound to their operation and step. After the owner is killed, its socket
  left behind, the next command runs in the terminal on the same store; a
  replacement owner replaces the stale endpoint and is reached in turn. An
  owner of protocol 2 (a POSIX stand-in holding the lease) is answered
  `unsupported_version` and receives no request byte; one with no channel is
  `capability_unavailable`, and nothing lands. Messages are bounded by the
  codec (5 MiB, per-field bounds, a command's own options only) and the
  transport; `sqlite_tour_owner_channel` checks the cycle codec's refusals
  and, on Windows, the pipe's authority, binding, large frames, owner death
  and cancellation. `cycle_cli_public_path` refuses with
  `capability_unavailable` while another process holds the lease without a
  channel.
- RC58-06: in the same course the reviewer's unsaved note text survives a
  terminal note, focus stays in it, and saving it adds a note beside the
  terminal's; a terminal draft change reaches the window and a stale one is
  `stale_revision` naming the current revision; a window check-box reversal
  reaches the terminal; a goal typed and not entered is saved before a
  terminal `freeze`, which is then `stale_revision`, and `review` reads it;
  while an input method composes in the goal, `review` and `freeze` are
  `draft_busy` and change nothing while a note is still served, and after the
  commit the composed text is read and frozen into F1 (the exported round
  carries it). A repeated `--operation-id` returns the first answer and adds
  one note; the same identity for another request is `conflict`.
  `review_cycle_workbench` shows the window's note kept beside another
  interface's writes, a goal refused for a newer draft kept unsaved and saved
  again, and the window reading the cycle again when told.

Limits. The Windows pipe is compiled here only for syntax; its first MSVC
build and run are owed, and `sqlite_tour_owner_channel`, registered contained
by BPR-1237 when its Windows half checked only that no endpoint existed, now
binds a pipe inside the container. Linux runs are owed. A second OS user is not
exercised. Two windows composing the same draft at once are covered by the
same per-window rule, not by a course.

Owed station runs, not gating (no `platforms` requirement). Linux:

```sh
git pull --rebase && cmake --preset dev -B build/dev-tour-cli -DBPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI=ON && cmake --build build/dev-tour-cli && ctest --test-dir build/dev-tour-cli -j4 --output-on-failure -R "^application_profile_cycle_handoff_public_path$|^application_profile_tour_handoff_public_path$|^cycle_cli_public_path$|^sqlite_tour_owner_channel$|^core_review_cycle_command$|^review_cycle_workbench$|^application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path$|^application_review_round_trip_composition_contract$|^architecture_boundaries$|^test_network_isolation_manifest$"
```

Windows (Debug, the contained rows as registered):

```powershell
git pull --rebase; cmake --preset windows-dev -B build/windows-dev-tour-cli -DBPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI=ON; cmake --build build/windows-dev-tour-cli --config Debug; ctest --test-dir build/windows-dev-tour-cli -C Debug -j4 --output-on-failure -R "^application_profile_cycle_handoff_public_path$|^application_profile_tour_handoff_public_path$|^cycle_cli_public_path$|^sqlite_tour_owner_channel$|^core_review_cycle_command$|^review_cycle_workbench$|^application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path$|^application_review_round_trip_composition_contract$|^architecture_boundaries$|^test_network_isolation_manifest$"; cmake --build build/windows-dev-tour-cli --config Debug --target coding-format-check
```

`application_profile_cycle_handoff_public_path` and `sqlite_tour_owner_channel`
run contained on Windows as registered. If either stops inside the container
(at the profile root, the cycle store or the pipe), pin it
`WINDOWS_UNCONTAINED` with the measured line in
`scripts/verify_test_network_isolation.py`, rerun it uncontained, and record
both; do not weaken the course.

### BPR-1169: one frozen round for a harness, under a stored grant (2026-10-02)

Owned paths: `core::cycle_command`'s round, response-append and policy entries
with `authority_of` and `last_cycle_command`
(`src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_command.hpp` and `.cpp`);
`src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_retrieval.hpp` and `.cpp` (the round's
measure, whole-item pages, cursors bound to round and digest, budgets,
attachment listings and slices); `core::encode_round_item`; the policy's stable
names, `configured_permission`, `with_permission`, `combine_policies` and
`read_only_grant`; the compose edits `include_round_context` and
`exclude_round_context`; the response channel `local_interface` with
`structured_response`; `src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_retrieval.hpp`
and `.cpp` (grant, revoke, the consumer read and append, the effective policy)
with the runtime's calls, the share and export decisions combining the stored
policy, `cycle_share_draft::Policy`, the workbench's external route storing its
grant and its routes following the stored policy; the store's `record_grant`,
`load_grant`, `grants`, `disclosure_policy` and `set_channel_permission` with
schema 9's `grants` and `channel_policy` tables; the owner channel's protocol 4
and the codec's command range; `cycle_commands.cpp`'s `round grant`, `round
revoke`, `round read`, `round page`, `round source`, `response append`,
`policy`, `review --context`, `--context-lines` and `--drop-context`, and the
grants `show` lists; `cycle_cli.cpp`'s two-word commands, `BPR_CYCLE_ROLE` and
capabilities; the Qt view's words for a statement a harness appended; the tests
named below; [the developer page](../development/cycle-cli.md) and the threat
model's Review Cycles section.

Compatibility decisions. The contract's `round read`, `round page` and `round
source` are offered under those names, with `round grant` and `round revoke` as
the reviewer's "expose this ready round" and its withdrawal, `response append`
as the harness's response, and `policy` as the profile's channel settings. A
grant is the core's existing `cycle_round_grant`, stored one per frozen round:
`round grant` and the desktop's Use in external agent store the response-only
grant (read and append), `--read-only` reading alone. Granting is decided as the
reviewer's export of that round through `external_retrieval`, so it obeys the
channel policy the core already has: for a folder or checkout, which is
`unclassified` until a classification is observed, that channel is not allowed
until configured. The configuration the core always took as an input
(`cycle_disclosure_policy`) is now the profile's, stored in the cycle store and
set with `bpr cycle policy`; every decision combines it with any policy the host
imposes, a denial in either standing, so the desktop's routes, Copy and Save,
the terminal's exports, granting and every harness read get one answer. A
consumer command acts only on the stored grant (`core::authorize` with
`granted_consumer`): a reviewer typing it gets no more. Reads are pages of whole
canonical items, at most 2 MiB each, so they fit the owner channel's 5 MiB
messages; the consumer verifies by the payload digest every page names and the
item counts. `--budget-bytes` is how a consumer states what it can hold, and an
item larger than any page is `requires_partition`, never split. A retrieval
records no delivery observation: the contract allows a receipt, and reading
changes nothing in this build. Contexts reach a round from the terminal through
`review --context`, as the first interface to select them. The owner protocol
goes to 4 because the registry grew; an owner of 3 is `unsupported_version`.
`BPR_CYCLE_ROLE=consumer` is a harness's declaration, not containment.

Commands, macOS arm64, `build/dev-tour-cli` (the `dev` preset with
`-DBPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI=ON`, Debug, Homebrew LLVM): a full build with no warning,
then
`ctest --test-dir build/dev-tour-cli -j6 -R "^cycle_cli_public_path$|^tour_cli_|^skill_pr_tour_package$|^core_review_cycle|^review_cycle_|^sqlite_review_cycle|^qt_review_cycle_view_|^qt_suggestion_editor_|^application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path$|^application_profile_tour_handoff_public_path$|^application_profile_cycle_handoff_public_path$|^application_profile_activation_public_path$|^application_profile_owner_admission_public_path$|^sqlite_tour_owner_channel$|^sqlite_profile_activation$|^architecture_boundaries$|^test_network_isolation_manifest$|^workspace_source$|^application_review_tour_|^sqlite_review_tour|^application_review_round_trip_composition_contract$"`
passes 82 of 82 in 61 s; after the tidy fixes the nine rows they reach
(`review_cycle_{workbench,response,export,round}`, `cycle_cli_public_path`,
the desktop and handoff courses, both `qt_review_cycle_view` rows) pass again.
The `core-only` preset passes its 24 `core_review_cycle` rows. clang-format is
clean on every changed file; clang-tidy is clean on every changed translation
unit except the eight existing `review_tour_codec.hpp` findings
`tour_owner_codec.cpp` includes (it found a moved stop token in the share
draft's policy read and two existing optional conversions in
`review_cycle_response.cpp`, all fixed). The round-trip composition contract
passes unchanged: no `src/app` file was added, and no pinned digest moved.
`tests/scripts/cycle_cli_tests.py` passes against the tests-disabled
`tour-cli` install's `bin/bpr` (see the card for the exact run).

Seven negative controls each fail the rows meant to catch them: a consumer
read decided on the reviewer's authority (`cycle_cli_public_path`: a round read
with no grant succeeds); a page that skips the item at its boundary
(`core_review_cycle_retrieval`, "every item, once", and the course's page
walk); no consumer role (the course's declared harness is not refused); a
revoked grant read back as live (the course's page after revocation succeeds);
an export that ignores the stored policy (the course's terminal export under a
stored denial succeeds); a budget that is not checked
(`core_review_cycle_retrieval` and the course's `requires_partition`); and a
cursor not bound to its round and digest (`core_review_cycle_retrieval` and the
course's forged cursor).

- RC58-03: commands name the round by `RC-n --round Fn`, or find the cycle the
  working folder is bound to; every result carries the cycle, round, logical
  project and `payload_sha256`, and every page and cursor is bound to that
  digest. In `application_profile_cycle_handoff_public_path`, with a real
  desktop owning the profile and its window moved to a second active cycle
  (RC-2), `round read --round F1` in RC-1's folder reads RC-1's F1 and nothing
  of RC-2, and from a folder bound to no cycle it is `not_found`: no window's
  selection is consulted. In `cycle_cli_public_path` a grant for RC-1 F2 reads
  neither RC-1 F1 nor RC-2 F1, and RC-2's folder finds RC-2.
- RC58-04: a read returns the frozen round's assignment and canonical items
  only, and `round source` only the round's own attachments (a whole file or
  range, bounded); another file of the capture, a path for an attachment, a
  note written after freezing and the next round's draft goal are not
  reachable, and the round reads byte for byte as frozen. Nothing scrapes the
  store or serves files: the terminal runs the command itself or at the owner
  over the existing same-user channel (protocol 4), and naming a round without
  a stored grant is `policy_denied` (`not_granted`). Granting is decided as the
  reviewer's export (`channel_unconfigured` for a plain folder until `bpr cycle
  policy` allows `external_retrieval`), and a later denial or revocation reads
  nothing.
- RC58-07: a 31-item round read at 4 KiB pages takes at least three pages,
  each naming the same digest and its first item; the pages hold every item
  once, in order, and equal the single-page read. A cursor of another round,
  a forged digest or a position past the end is refused; `--budget-bytes`
  below the instruction bytes is `requires_partition` with the measure and no
  items, and exactly enough reads it whole; an item over the largest page is
  `requires_partition`, never cut (`core_review_cycle_retrieval`).
- RC58-09: a read-only grant cannot append; a response grant appends one
  structured response recorded as `local_interface`, with its claimed provider
  and model kept as claims, and no note, wording or round changes; a response
  claiming `verified` is refused, and one for another round or digest is a
  conflict or not granted. There is no verify or publish command; with
  `BPR_CYCLE_ROLE=consumer` every other command (show, list, compare, export,
  grant, revoke, policy, review, freeze, capture, note, response import) is
  `policy_denied` and changes nothing. The desktop's Use in external agent
  stores the same response-only grant for exactly the round it froze, and its
  routes follow the stored policy (`review_cycle_workbench`).

Limits. The desktop does not list Use in external agent (BPR-1161 keeps it
hidden); its host side and the stored grant are exercised in
`review_cycle_workbench`. A forwarded `policy` change does not refresh an open
Compose page until it is opened again, though every share decides on the
stored policy when it acts. Ambiguity between two active cycles bound to one
folder is still asserted by rule, as for BPR-1167. A retrieval records no
delivery observation. `BPR_CYCLE_ROLE` and the application checks are not
containment of a same-user process. Windows and Linux runs are owed.

Owed station runs, not gating (no `platforms` requirement). Linux:

