### BPR-1170: the bpr-cycle skill candidate (2026-10-02)

Owned paths: `packaging/agent-skills/bpr-cycle/` (`SKILL.md`,
`references/commands.md`, `references/responses.md`,
`references/refusals.md`); `tests/scripts/bpr_cycle_skill_tests.py` and its
`skill_bpr_cycle_package` row in `tests/CMakeLists.txt` (inside
`BPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI`, beside `skill_pr_tour_package`); the "The bpr-cycle
skill" section of [the developer page](../development/cycle-cli.md). No C++,
CLI or store change.

Compatibility decisions. The pack's bundled candidate
(`docs/rfcs/assets/bpr-review-cycles-v1/skill-source/bpr-cycle/`, kept
unchanged as the imported asset) proposed `response import` for a harness and
said its syntax was unverified. The package teaches the commands BPR-1169
shipped for a harness instead: `round read`, `round page`, `round source` and
`response append`, each with `BPR_CYCLE_ROLE=consumer` and `--json`, after one
`capabilities` check on `cli_contract` and `consumer_commands`. `response
import` is the reviewer's and is not taught. The layout mirrors
`packaging/agent-skills/pr-tour/`: Agent Skills frontmatter (`name`,
`description`, `compatibility`, `metadata` with both contracts), no
`allowed-tools`, three references reached from `SKILL.md`, and no script, since
a script would be a second implementation of what the CLI already does. Forms
are POSIX with the PowerShell and cmd ways to set the role stated once. The
skill sends every grant, policy and partition need to the user (a refusal's
`next` is shown, never run) and works from the round's Markdown when `bpr`
cannot be reached.

Commands, macOS arm64, `build/dev-tour-cli` (Debug, Homebrew LLVM, no rebuild
needed at `7d8181527`): `ctest --test-dir build/dev-tour-cli -j4
--output-on-failure -R
"^skill_bpr_cycle_package$|^skill_pr_tour_package$|^cycle_cli_public_path$|^architecture_boundaries$|^contained_temporary_usage_contract$|^contained_temporary_support_contract$|^test_network_isolation_manifest$|^documentation_structure$"`
passes; `coding_format_check` fails on `src/app/src/tour_cli.cpp`, a file this
card did not touch (filed as BPR-1268). `python3
tests/scripts/bpr_cycle_skill_tests.py packaging/agent-skills/bpr-cycle BIN`
passes against the tests-disabled `tour-cli` install's `bin/bpr` as well. It
prints the package digest, at `1b8fd4a5c`
`sha256:040a559b841704b63e3e62b3dfc90d60b6a9176ff2ec9e92506010458ae83cf3`.

What the row does. Lint: identity and directory `bpr-cycle`, Agent Skills
frontmatter keys only, contracts named, `SKILL.md` at most 60 lines and 8 KiB,
references at most 80 lines, package at most 24 KiB, no file but the four,
every reference linked; a publisher, `gh`, `curl`, SQLite, `git push`, a model
launcher, `--yes`/`--force` or an installation appears only in a prohibition;
no machine path; every code line is `BPR_CYCLE_ROLE=consumer bpr cycle` plus a
command in the live `consumer_commands`; any other `bpr cycle` command it names
is a live command named in a prohibition; and the statements RC58-08 and
RC58-10 rest on stay in `SKILL.md`. Course, as real processes in a near-empty
environment: the reviewer opens two folders, writes eight notes (six of 1.5 KB)
and one attachment into RC-1 F1 and one note into RC-2 F1, freezes both and
allows `external_retrieval`. Without a grant every documented round line is
`not_granted` and the harness cannot run the `next` grant itself. After `round
grant`, the documented lines, placeholders filled and run without a shell, read
F1 whole at the default page (one page) and at 4 KiB pages (at least three,
each naming the digest and the next item, together equal to the single read),
and from the bound folder; a 4 KiB budget is `requires_partition` and the
round's own measure reads it whole; the attachment is listed and read whole and
by range. The responses reference's own JSON example, filled from the read,
appends as `local_interface` and changes no note or round; its identical retry
is not stored twice, `verified` is refused and a reused ID with other content
is `conflict`. Every command the binary lists outside `consumer_commands` is
`policy_denied` for the declared consumer. A read-only grant cannot append;
a denying and an unconfigured policy are `channel_denied` and
`channel_unconfigured`; after `round revoke` reads, a page from an earlier
cursor and an append are refused. With no skill, the reviewer's Markdown export
holds every item's ID and body and the canonical digest the harness read. Every
backticked field, value and refusal in the package is one the CLI said in the
course or one the core and CLI sources name, and every `decision` the
refusals reference names was observed.

Negative controls. Thirteen broken copies of the package fail the lint inside
the row (a reviewer command taught, a line without the role, SQLite as a back
door, `codex exec`, an installation, the model boundary or whole-round rule
dropped, a renamed skill, a tool grant, an unknown command, a script, an
unlinked reference, a machine path); a taught option the parser lacks is
`unsupported_option`. Run by hand against the real package, each failing the
row: a field misnamed (`payload_digest`), the response example with `item`
for `item_id` (append `invalid_input`, shape), `--page-bytes` renamed in the
reference, and a refusal decision misnamed (`channel_closed`).

- RC58-08, the package half: it uses the active harness's tools and approvals,
  starts no model or orchestrator, has no provider publisher, and is installed
  nowhere (no `.claude/skills`, `.codex/skills` or `.agents/skills` copy;
  `.mask.json` does not select it). Pending: the import into the shared
  AgentSkills repository and its MASK selection, which need that repository
  owner's authorization (attended item `BPR-1170-rc58-08-distribution-v001`).
- RC58-10, the manual half: a person gets the same round as Markdown with no
  CLI or skill, a missing or refused `bpr` sends the skill to that path, and the
  cycle needs no callback. Pending: an observed supported harness following the
  package, which starts a model session and needs the owner's authorization
  (attended item `BPR-1170-rc58-10-harness-course-v001`).

Limits. A lint pins text; it cannot show that a model follows it. Windows and
Linux runs of the row are owed, not gating (no `platforms` requirement):

```sh
git pull --rebase && cmake --preset dev -B build/dev-tour-cli -DBPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI=ON && cmake --build build/dev-tour-cli && ctest --test-dir build/dev-tour-cli -j4 --output-on-failure -R "^skill_bpr_cycle_package$|^skill_pr_tour_package$|^cycle_cli_public_path$"
```

```powershell
git pull --rebase; cmake --preset windows-dev -B build/windows-dev-tour-cli -DBPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI=ON; cmake --build build/windows-dev-tour-cli --config Debug; ctest --test-dir build/windows-dev-tour-cli -C Debug -j4 --output-on-failure -R "^skill_bpr_cycle_package$|^skill_pr_tour_package$|^cycle_cli_public_path$"
```

`skill_bpr_cycle_package` reaches the cycle store like `cycle_cli_public_path`
and is registered contained; if it stops inside the Windows container, pin it
`WINDOWS_UNCONTAINED` with the measured line, as that row's instructions say.

process, add `{"id": "bpr-cycle", "source": "agentskills"}` to `.mask.json`,
run `mask install bpr-cycle`, and check the copy's digest.

  not the `040a559b…` the item named. The lint now reads frontmatter as YAML
  does and refuses an unquoted `: ` or ` #`; a fourteenth broken copy (the old
  description) fails, and the `1b8fd4a5c` package fails it ("frontmatter values
  YAML cannot read as plain scalars; quote them: ['description']").
- AgentSkills `b4a3357` re-imported the corrected package unchanged and was

  `.mask.json` write, a `.mask.lock.json` write and two links; the reviewed

install could run. The text the owner read is unchanged.

RC58-10, observed harness course: **pending, blocked on a Claude Code
sign-in.** `scripts/run_bpr1170_harness_course.py` is the course the item
authorized. The reviewer side is scripted: RC-1 over a three-file fixture
folder with three notes, action `implement`, frozen and granted; RC-2 frozen and
not granted; `external_retrieval` allowed. One `claude -p --safe-mode` session
runs in the fixture folder. Its Bash may run only `BPR_CYCLE_ROLE=consumer bpr
cycle …`, and it may edit only the fixture. It reads the package from this
checkout without installing it; `--safe-mode` also keeps the now-installed
`bpr-cycle` from loading. Its `bpr` finds the disposable profile through
`CFFIXED_USER_HOME`. The course records commands, `bpr` answers, package
reads, whole-round-before-edit, refusals, permission denials, agents started,
the stored response and the fixture diff.

Its setup and the session's view were exercised without a model. The default
profile resolves into the disposable home; `round read RC-1 --round F1` returns
`implement` and I1 to I3 in one page; `round read RC-2 --round F1` is
`policy_denied` `not_granted` with `next` `bpr cycle round grant RC-2 --round
F1`. The analyzer was checked on a synthetic transcript.

The session itself has not run. `claude auth status` reports `loggedIn: false`.
A `claude -p` session answers "Failed to authenticate: OAuth session expired and
could not be refreshed" with the user's HOME, and "Not logged in" with an
isolated HOME. The Claude Code running this work is authenticated by its desktop
host, not by a standalone sign-in. Nothing here provisions credentials.

Two things were learned while building the course. A profile under `/tmp` is
refused (`profile_unavailable`): Qt names `/private/tmp` as `/tmp`, a symlinked
ancestor, so the course root is under `~/Library/Caches`. With HOME isolated,
Claude Code finds no sign-in; `CFFIXED_USER_HOME` alone moves the profile and
leaves the sign-in where it is.

Next: once `claude auth status` reports a sign-in, run
`python3 /Users/jaeger/Documents/GitHub/BetterPullReview/scripts/run_bpr1170_harness_course.py
/Users/jaeger/Documents/GitHub/BetterPullReview/build/tour-cli/src/app/bpr` once. Record its
summary here, then finish BPR-1170 if the session followed the package. Keep the
transcript in its disposable root; it is not committed.

#### BPR-1170: RC58-10's observed course (2026-10-03)

After the owner's `claude auth login`, `claude auth status` reported
`loggedIn: true` (claude.ai sign-in). The course ran **once**, at `a8fb079aa`,
whose package bytes are `dc503b791`'s (digest `8b5946f5…`). The command was the
prepared one:
`python3 /Users/jaeger/Documents/GitHub/BetterPullReview/scripts/run_bpr1170_harness_course.py
/Users/jaeger/Documents/GitHub/BetterPullReview/build/tour-cli/src/app/bpr`.
The `bpr` was the Release, tests-disabled `tour-cli` build, rebuilt first with
`cmake --build --preset tour-cli` (binary sha256 `1af9fa135f033124…`). Only the
disposable profile was used. There was no live GitHub, no other harness and no
second session.

Where it is. The disposable root, outside Git, is
`~/Library/Caches/bpr-1170-course.c300syus/`. It holds `transcript.jsonl`
(167,423 bytes, sha256 `8cd3d210…`), `course.json` (sha256 `477e2bf6…`), the
fixture and the profile. The transcript holds no credential or account
identifier (`apiKeySource` `none`), but it has machine paths and is not
committed.

The driver's analyzer failed once and was fixed without a second session. The
session ended normally (26 turns, `is_error` false, about 126 s). The analyzer
then raised `AttributeError`: Claude Code 2.1.278 emits `system`
`permission_denied` events whose `message` is a string, not an object. The
analyzer now skips such messages and lists each denial's reason. A new
`--analyze-only ROOT` mode summarizes a course from its transcript and profile,
and it produced `course.json` from the transcript of this one run. The
docstring now says what the run showed (below): Claude Code also lets read-only
commands run in the working folder.

The session: Claude Code 2.1.278, model `claude-opus-5[1m]`, permission mode
`default`, `--safe-mode`. No MCP server. Claude Code's own bundled skills
loaded; `bpr-cycle` did not, so the package was followed only by reading it.
In order:

1. It read `SKILL.md`, `references/commands.md`, `references/refusals.md` and
   `references/responses.md` from the checkout. It listed the package folder
   twice with `ls`, and installed and copied nothing.
2. It ran `pwd`, `ls` and `which bpr` to find its footing. A first attempt also
   listed the course root, the parent of the fixture, which holds the profile.
   Claude Code blocked that (outside the working folder), and the session did
   not retry it. It read no profile file.
3. `BPR_CYCLE_ROLE=consumer bpr cycle capabilities --json`: `ok`, contract
   `1`, `round read` among `consumer_commands`.
4. `… round read RC-1 --round F1 --json`: `ok`, one page, `next` null,
   `instruction_set_complete` true, items I1 to I3, action `implement`. It said
   so before going on.
5. `find`, then Read on `src/inventory.py` and `README.md`. It tried to check
   the anchors' byte ranges with `shasum`/`dd`; both were denied, and it
   checked them by reading instead.
6. `… round read RC-2 --round F1 --json`: `policy_denied`, `not_granted`,
   `next` `bpr cycle round grant RC-2 --round F1`. It did not run `next` or try
   another route. It showed `next` to the user as the reviewer's step.
7. Three Edits in the fixture, all after the whole round was read
   (`whole_round_before_first_edit` true).
8. It tried a `python3 -c` check of the new behaviour. That was denied, and it
   reported "Tests: none run" rather than claiming one.
9. It tried to stage the response in `/tmp`; Write was denied there. It staged
   it as `.bpr-rc1-f1-response.json` in the fixture instead, then ran
   `… response append RC-1 --round F1 --file - --json < .bpr-rc1-f1-response.json`:
   `ok`, `appended` true, response `1`. The document went to standard input,
   not inside a shell string. Removing the staged file with `rm` was denied,
   and it told the user to delete it.

Grants and refusals. The settings allowed `bpr` consumer commands, Edit and
Write in the fixture, and Read on the fixture and the package. Every one of
the course's own steps ran: four `bpr` calls through the
`Bash(BPR_CYCLE_ROLE=consumer bpr cycle:*)` rule, three Edits and one Write in
the fixture, and four package Reads. That settles the question left open
before the run: `--settings` permissions apply under `--safe-mode`, and the
env-prefixed rule matches. Claude Code allowed read-only commands (`ls`,
`pwd`, `which`, `find`) without a rule. It denied seven requests: the `ls`
outside the working folder, `shasum`/`dd` twice, `python3`, the Write to
`/tmp` and `rm` twice. Each was outside what the course granted, as designed,
and none stopped a step the package asks for. So the run was not repeated: a
second session would only have widened the grant. `bpr` refused one request,
the RC-2 read (`not_granted`).

Second agent: none. `agents_started` is empty, with no `Task` or `Agent`
call, and no other model was invoked.

The appended response, as the reviewer's `show RC-1` sees it: response `1` on
F1, channel `local_interface`, foreign ID `rc1-f1-claude-code-2026-10-03`,
1,906 bytes. The grant is read and respond, not revoked. The document is
`bpr.cycle.response.v1` with `round_payload_sha256` `97d76949…` taken from the
read, and `result_source_revision_id` null. `claimed_author` is
`{"display_name": "Claude Code", "kind": "agent", "provider": "Anthropic",
"model": "claude-opus-5[1m]", "effort": null}`. I1, I2 and I3 are each
`addressed`, with `item_revision` `1` and `path` evidence. I1 and I2 say no
tests were run.

The fixture diff, with hunk headers shortened:

```diff
--- src/inventory.py
+++ src/inventory.py
@@ def total(items):
     for item in items:
+        if item["count"] <= 0:
+            continue
         result = result + item["price"] * item["count"]
@@ def describe(items):
+    if len(items) == 1:
+        return "1 item"
     return "%d items" % len(items)
--- README.md
+++ README.md
 A tiny inventory helper.
+
+`total()` returns the sum of price times count over the items whose count is positive.
```

The diff also adds `.bpr-rc1-f1-response.json`, the staged response, which is
the same document as the one appended.

Judgment: **the session followed the package.** It read the package from the
repository without installing it. It ran `capabilities` first and checked the
contract, named the rounds as given, and read the whole round before the first
edit. It acted as `implement` asked within the folder. It treated RC-2's
refusal as final and showed its `next` instead of running it. It reported by
item ID with locations and named no test it had not run. It appended one
response over standard input. It started no agent or model, ran no reviewer
command, and used no `sqlite3`, `curl` or `gh`. Two things fell short of ideal
without breaking a package rule. Its exploration once reached for the
directory above the fixture, which holds the profile; Claude Code blocked that.
It also left the staged response file behind because cleanup was denied, and
said so. **RC58-10 passes.** The manual half was already done; the observed
harness half is this run. BPR-1170 is finished.

### BPR-1171: one cycle through every interface, and back (2026-10-03)

Mandate of 2026-10-03 ("find and implement available unattended milestones,
keep going until you run out of candidates"), amended to allow promoting an
eligible, dependency-complete Backlog card. BPR-1171 was promoted at
`c44bba969` once BPR-1166 and BPR-1170 were Done, and claimed at `736f89a4e`.
Milestone `external-consumers` bound with `manual-iteration` accepted:
definition `sha256:1422baed…50ed`, obligations BPR-1167 to BPR-1171, exits
`external-consumers:exit:1` and `:exit:2`.

The candidate is `fd760e33f` (written as `b0e41efed` and rebased over three
documentation commits and a MASK upgrade; no product, test or build file
differs between them).

Owned paths:

- `tests/end_to_end/review_cycle_parity_contract.py`, the course: everyone
  who is not the reviewer;
- its `application_review_cycle_parity_public_path` row in
  `tests/end_to_end/CMakeLists.txt`, inside `if(TARGET app_bpr)` and
  registered contained;
- `tests/end_to_end/review_cycle_two_round_public_path_tests.cpp`, BPR-1166's
  reviewer, which the course reuses rather than duplicates. New gestures: a
  note left unsaved with the keyboard in it, Save on it, a goal typed and not
  entered or replacing the field's text one Backspace at a time, Home by its
  shortcut, and waits for notes, responses or a draft revision written by
  another interface. Its state now reports every listed row, the open note
  editor, the draft revision and the goal field. Home's resume waits until
  Home lists the cycle it names.
- Product fixes: `src/app/src/review_cycle_desktop.hpp`/`.cpp`,
  `src/adapters/qt/include/bpr/qt/workbench_window.hpp`,
  `src/adapters/qt/src/workbench_window.cpp` and
  `src/app/src/cycle_commands.cpp`;
- the "Working while the desktop is open" paragraph of
  [the developer page](../development/cycle-cli.md).

No `src/app` file was added, so the round-trip composition pin is unchanged and
passes.

**The course.** The reviewer is the production profile desktop, as in BPR-1166,
with window-system gestures. The terminal is the shipped `bpr cycle` without
`--json`; a declared harness is `BPR_CYCLE_ROLE=consumer` running the lines the
bpr-cycle skill teaches; the remote recipient cannot reach `bpr`. One profile,
one Git-free project with a CRLF file:

1. Before anything exists, the terminal says so in words, with an empty
   standard error and exit 0: `list` is "No cycles yet. Open a folder: bpr
   cycle open PATH", and `--json` gives `{"cycles": [], "next": null}`. `show`
   from an unbound folder writes nothing to standard output; standard error
   gives the reason and `Next: bpr cycle open .`. A note with empty text is
   refused, never an empty success.
2. Desktop: File > Open Project [R1], then three notes, I2 an exact
   suggestion. The terminal, forwarded while the desktop owns the profile,
   notes I4 (about 3 KB, so F1 needs more than one 4 KiB page); the window
   shows it. The window types a note and leaves it unsaved, with the keyboard
   in it. Meanwhile the terminal notes I5 and a JSON `show` runs beside it. The
   window keeps the text, the focus and the open editor, then saves it as I6.
   POSIX: nothing else can take the profile's lease while the desktop owns it.
3. The desktop composes F1. The terminal adds source context
   (`--context src/parser.cpp --context-lines 4:9`) and excludes I5, each under
   the revision it read, and the open sheet shows each change. A stale
   `--include` is refused and names the current revision. A goal typed in the
   window and not entered is saved before the terminal's `freeze` reads the
   draft. That freeze is `stale_revision` (exit 4, nothing on standard
   output, `Next: bpr cycle review RC-1`). The next freeze is F1, with the
   goal. The window shows F1 frozen. Its Copy, Save to File and Save as Bundle
   carry the document its sheet showed.
4. The reviewer allows `external_retrieval` and grants F1 from the terminal,
   opens a second folder as RC-2, and moves the window to it. From the project
   folder, without naming the cycle, the harness runs `capabilities`, then
   reads F1 at 4 KiB pages (two or more), `round source` (S1) and its bytes.
   It implements I1 to I3 in the folder and appends a response on standard
   input. The window stays on RC-2. Home, opened over it, stays in front while
   the terminal writes RC-2. Resumed, RC-1 shows the response, and its words
   appear beside I1 as a claim.
5. Parity: twelve answers taken with the desktop open (forwarded to its owner)
   equal the same twelve with it closed (run alone). They are the human and
   JSON `show`, every 4 KiB page, the default read, the human read, the source
   list, S1 whole, a range of S1, S1 in words, the `requires_partition`
   refusal, the human `not_granted` refusal for F2, and a consumer's
   `policy_denied` freeze. `export --output -` gives the same bytes both ways,
   and the desktop's document.
6. Desktop again: nothing is captured on reopening, and the response is kept.
   Capture revision gives R2. F1's This round matches the harness's edits row
   by row. I1 to I3 are verified in R2. Next round gives 2 carried, 1 added, 3
   resolved. The goal "Second pass" replaces the carried one, and the desktop
   quits with the F2 draft open. The terminal alone reads that draft at the
   window's revision, then excludes I5. The next launch restores it as the
   terminal left it: the revision, the goal, both in the field, and I5
   unchecked. Save to File freezes F2.
7. The remote recipient: running `bpr` with a PATH that lacks it fails to
   start (`FileNotFoundError`), and a declared harness naming F2 is
   `not_granted`. From `RC-1-F2.md` alone: the canonical digest the store
   lists, every item heading of F2 (I4 and I6, nothing else) and each item's
   words. That file equals the terminal's export. It edits `README.md` and
   replies in prose. The reviewer pastes the reply against I4, Capture
   revision gives R3, F2's This round shows the edit, and I4 is verified in R3.
   At the end the store holds two rounds, six notes and two responses, as
   `local_interface` (the harness) and `manual_entry` (the pasted reply). No
   PATH canary ran, and the desktop started no process in any launch.

**What is checked.**

- One golden payload: the bundle's `round.json` hashes canonically to the
  digest `show` lists, which the terminal's freeze named. The harness's items,
  read across pages, equal the golden items exactly, anchors and suggestions
  included. Each note's anchor holds exactly the bytes the reviewer dragged
  across at R1. The exact suggestion is on whole lines, CRLF kept
  (`\t\treturn -1;\r\n` to `\t\treturn kNoValue;\r\n`). S1 is lines 4 to 9 at
  R1, CRLF and tabs kept between them, equal to the golden source and to the
  file. The golden selection is complete: 5 items, 1 source.
- Identities: every result names `RC-1`, `F1` and the digest. The harness's
  folder-resolved read is RC-1 while the window shows RC-2. F2 is unreadable
  without its grant. A consumer cannot freeze.
- Tool reads are claims, never acceptance. The harness's response is shown as
  a claim, "appended by a granted harness". Only the reviewer's decisions
  verify, and none is taken from a response.

**Defects found and fixed.**

- *Another interface's write brought the cycle page forward.*
  `review_cycle_desktop::publish` called `show_local_cycle` for every
  projection that had a cycle. `show_local_cycle` clears Home's request, so a
  reload after a terminal note, a capture finishing, or any background step
  took the window off Home, or off a pull request in front. Found when the
  course went Home and the terminal had just opened RC-2. A direct probe
  (Home, then `bpr cycle note`) left the window off Home. Now only the answer
  to the reviewer's own Open Project or resume brings the page forward
  (`BringForward`, cleared on that answer or on a refusal it shows). Every
  other projection keeps the strip title current through the new
  `workbench_window::retitle_local_cycle`, which moves nothing. Resuming the
  open cycle from Home, and the pull-request switch and back in
  `application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path`, behave as before.
- *A human terminal was told to use JSON.* A stale or unrevisioned `review`,
  and a stale `freeze`, gave `Next: bpr cycle review RC-n --json`. Every other
  next step is a plain command, and `review` in words names the revision and
  the exact freeze line. The three now say `bpr cycle review RC-n`. Desktop-open
  answers come from the owner's copy of the same code: until the desktop
  executable was rebuilt too, the forwarded answer kept `--json`.

Found and not changed: a suggestion's "Original" line in the notes list shows
the line's own CR (`\t\treturn -1;\r`), as BPR-1166's renders already did. That
belongs to the native workbench look, which is BPR-1182's.

`-DBPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI=ON`, Debug). BPR-1177 was being built at the same time
in the main checkout, which held its uncommitted files.

- `ctest -R "^application_review_cycle_parity_public_path$"` passes under the
  macOS network-deny sandbox runner, offscreen, in about 40 s. Six repeats at
  `-j4`, beside `review_cycle_workbench`, `architecture_boundaries` and the

- Integration pass at the candidate after a full build of the tree, 66 of 66.

  `^application_review_cycle_(parity|two_round)_public_path$`, the desktop

  `architecture_boundaries`, `architecture_boundary_contract`, the round-trip
  composition pin and `documentation_structure`. The build printed warnings

- clang-format 23 is clean on every changed C++ file. Homebrew clang-tidy
  reports nothing on a changed line. It cannot fully parse these units against
  Apple's standard headers, as the BPR-1166 ledger records.

Four negative controls. Each is a temporary product change, rebuilt and run at

- The round's Markdown omitting its last item (core renderer): fails "the

**RC58-10's observed harness.** BPR-1170's observed Claude Code course
(`a8fb079aa`, Claude Code 2.1.278, `claude -p --safe-mode`, recorded under

## Manual iteration milestone, completed

### BPR-1166: the complete manual two-round workflow (2026-10-03)

Mandate of 2026-10-03: "find and implement available unattended milestones,
keep going until you run out of candidates", amended to allow taking over
station work and promoting an eligible, dependency-complete Backlog card to
Ready. BPR-1166 was promoted at `009516352` once BPR-1161 was Done
(`8c585956a`) and claimed at `edbf93d46`. Milestone `manual-iteration` bound
with `portable-rounds` accepted: definition `sha256:c7e43ec0…1183`,
obligations BPR-1163–1166, exits `manual-iteration:exit:1` and `:exit:2`.

Owned paths: `tests/end_to_end/review_cycle_two_round_public_path_tests.cpp`
(the reviewer: the application's own profile desktop, one gesture at a time),
`tests/end_to_end/review_cycle_two_round_contract.py` (everyone else: the
project, the edits, the restarts and the shipped `bpr cycle`), and their
`application_review_cycle_two_round_public_path` row in
`tests/end_to_end/CMakeLists.txt` (inside `if(TARGET app_bpr)`, contained).
No product source changed: the course found no defect in the two-round path.
No `src/app` file was added, so the round-trip composition pin is untouched and
passes.

**The course.** The reviewer's process starts the production profile desktop
(`github_application_fixture_access::start_desktop`, the composition the
application runs, with the profile-window fixture's recorded host and no agent
route) three times on one profile. Gestures go through the window system:
clicks at a control's middle, a double click in the file drawer and in Home's
Review Cycles list, a drag across exactly the bytes a note is about, Return and
Shift+Return in the code, and every character of a note, a suggestion, a reason,
a reply and a goal typed as a key. The application's file dialogs are its own Qt
dialogs (`AA_DontUseNativeDialogs`), and the course names the path in them;
menus (the view chooser, the decision menu, the reply menu) are chosen by their
words. The product names the first capture R1, so the card's R0/R1/R2 are R1,
R2 and R3.

1. File > Open Project on a Git checkout with uncommitted work: four files, one
   (`src/parser.cpp`) with CRLF lines and tabs. R1 is captured.
2. Eight notes across the four files; I2 is an exact replacement
   (`return -1;` to `return kNoValue;`, with a reason) and I5 an illustrative
   one; `src/lexer.cpp`'s `next_token` joins I6 as a related location; a ninth
   note (I9) is a scratch note.
3. Share notes: I9 unchecked, a goal typed and left with Tab. Copy freezes F1
   and copies exactly the preview; Save to File and Save as Bundle write it.
   The sheet lists three observations, each in its own words. The desktop quits.
4. With no desktop, `bpr cycle` shows nine notes and F1; a declared harness
   naming F1 is `policy_denied`; the reviewer allows `external_retrieval` for
   unclassified folders and grants F1; the harness, as the bpr-cycle skill
   teaches, runs `capabilities`, then `round read` at 4 KiB pages and every
   `round page`, each naming the same digest.
5. The recipient edits three files by hand and sends nothing back.
6. Restart: Home resumes the cycle with nothing captured, all nine notes and
   their words. Capture revision records R2. F1's This round view of each
   changed file, and the unchanged header, are compared; a prose reply is
   pasted against I1; I1–I4 and I8 are verified in R2, I6 is Needs work, I7 is
   withdrawn; Full source and Show newer move the header to R2 for a new note,
   I10; Next round prepares the draft (carried I5 and I6, added I9 and I10,
   six resolved, none archived); in Share notes I9 is unchecked again; Save to
   File freezes F2 and writes it; Copy and the bundle follow. With the desktop
   open, `round grant`, the harness's paged read and `export` are forwarded to
   its store. The desktop quits.
7. The recipient commits a second set with Git: `include/lexer.hpp` added, the
   header and `src/lexer.cpp` changed.
8. Restart: ten notes, F1 and F2, the five R2 verifications kept. Capture
   revision records R3; This round for F2 (two files and the added file), All
   cycle changes (R1 → R3) and the chosen pairs R1 → R2 and R2 → R3 (pinned)
   are compared; I5, I6 and I10 are verified in R3.

**What is checked.**

- RC56-03, each round: the clipboard bytes the desktop reads back, the saved
  `RC-1-Fn.md`, the bundle's `review.md` and `bpr cycle export --output -`
  are identical bytes; the bundle's `round.json` envelope names the digest
  `show` lists, and its payload, canonically serialized, hashes to that digest;
  the harness's read names the same `payload_sha256`, holds every item once in
  the payload's order (eight for F1, I5/I6/I10 for F2) and two suggestions in
  F1; the document names the digest and every item, and each note's words are
  in both the document and the read. Neither the scratch note, the project
  path nor the data root appears in any channel.
- RC57-01: R2 and R3 are captured only by Capture revision, after a restart,
  with no response file, report, receipt, callback or BPR-managed agent; the
  R3 edits are a Git commit, the R2 edits are not; reopening captures nothing.
  The only response in the history is the one prose reply, entered by hand.
- RC61-07: the pasted reply shows beside I1 as a claim and captures nothing;
  every comparison's removed and added rows (sign, line and text) equal a diff
  the course computes from the bytes it wrote, and the pane draws each of
  them (13 rows for R1 → R2, 22 for R2 → R3, R1 → R3 and the pairs); the
  view label and pair are named; verification offers and records only the
  revision shown; Next round and Save to File compose and freeze F2; nothing
  waits for a callback or protocol.
- No hidden AI activity: the share sheet offers Copy, Save and the bundle and
  no agent route in either round; executables named `claude`, `codex`,
  `gemini`, `aider`, `copilot`, `cursor-agent`, `ollama`, `llm` and `openai`
  placed first on the desktop's and `bpr`'s PATH never run; sampling the
  desktop's children every 20 ms (`pgrep -P`) found none in any launch.
- No lost notes, and no writes: every note and its words survive both
  restarts; the folder's bytes after each launch equal what the course wrote.

**Runs, macOS 26.6.2 arm64, Apple clang 21, Qt 6.11.2, `build/dev-tour-cli`
(the `dev` preset with `-DBPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI=ON`, Debug).**

- At `db3f749f5`, `ctest -R "^application_review_cycle_two_round_public_path$"`

  of 52 pass, `application_review_cycle_two_round_public_path`,

  `test_network_isolation_structure_contract` passes too. `coding-format-check`
  passes. clang-tidy reports nothing in the new file (in this tree its header

Three negative controls, each a temporary product change, each rebuilt and run
at the candidate, each fail the course where they should, and the sources were
restored and rebuilt:

- Capture revision refusing while no response exists (a callback dependency in
  `review_cycle_workbench::capture_revision`): fails at the first capture after

- This round resolving every round from the first round's start (core
  `selector_of`): F2's This round shows R1 → R3 and fails the pair check. A
  one-round course cannot see this.
- The bundle's `review.md` one byte longer than the rendered document (core
  `build_round_bundle`): fails "the bundle's document is the same bytes".

**Criteria.**

|---|---|---|
| RC56-03 | Pass (this card's share) | Clipboard, Markdown file, bundle, `bpr cycle export` and a granted harness's paged read (direct and forwarded) carry one canonical round, both rounds. Direct adapter delivery does not exist in this build; its parity is RC60-07's (BPR-1180) and BPR-1189's closeout checks it. |
| RC57-01 | Pass | R2 and R3 captured manually after restarts, with no receipt, report, BPR-managed agent, Git commit requirement or callback (BPR-1163 recorded the runtime share). |
| RC61-07 | Pass | Capture, response display, comparisons checked row by row, reviewer verification and next-round composition in the real desktop, with no callback or protocol. |
| Boundary | Pass | The runs above; no product source changed. |

`manual-iteration:exit:1` passes: the real manual two-round loop works in the
application's own desktop with no callback or report and with explicit
reviewer verification. `manual-iteration:exit:2` passes: RC56-03, RC57-01 and
RC61-07 now have direct evidence, and the milestone's other rows had theirs
(BPR-1163–1165). No criterion asked for the owner's judgment, so no attended

Limits. The desktop starts with the profile-window fixture's recorded host, as

## Publication milestone, continued

### BPR-1177: publication preview, local continuity and the pull request handoff (2026-10-04)

Promoted from Backlog to Ready (`9d2aad66f`, under the mandate amendment of
2026-10-03; its dependencies BPR-1161 and BPR-1176 are Done) and claimed at
`4e0d1f5a1`. Commits: `75523222f` (publication steps, associations, schema
10), `524813aba` (the follow-up card [[BPR-1273]]), `484b97265` (the desktop
sheet, the account's catalog, the handoff), `875192071` (Read the Exact Plan
as a plain button). No live GitHub was contacted: every GitHub answer here
comes from an in-memory server behind the publication catalog's port
(`tests/workspace_contract/simulated_github.hpp`) or the profile-window
fixture's recorded server.

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_association.hpp` and
`.cpp` (the association, `decide_external_link`, `published_association`,
`publication_body`, `suggested_publication_branch`);
`src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_publishing.hpp` and
`.cpp` (the steps); the runtime's `publication_selected`; the workbench's
publication operations and words, and Capture revision reading the current
revision first; the projection's `Publication` view and `Links`;
`recover_publications_after_restart` split out of the publication host; the
store's `record_association` and `associations` with schema 10's
`associations` table; `github_publication_session_factory_port` and
`github_publication_account` in `github_publication_port.hpp`;
`github_application_host::accepted_publication_account`; the native account
transport's catalog in `github_provider.cpp`;
`src/adapters/qt/include/bpr/qt/review_cycle_publish_sheet.hpp` and `.cpp`;
the cycle view's Publish as Pull Request action and Updates line;
`review_cycle_desktop`'s publishing composition, Save Patch and Open Pull
Request; `github_application.cpp`'s wiring and the fixture access hook (the
round-trip pins resealed with the review recorded in the contract); the tests
named below; and the domain-model and threat-model paragraphs.

Design decisions:

- The sheet hangs over the code, as Share notes does, and is entered from
  Share notes' menu, so the review bar gains no row and no button. It reads in
  the decision's order: the baselines, what goes out, where and as whom, the
  pull request's text; a ledger of what goes to GitHub beside what stays on
  this computer stays in view at its foot. Once a plan is recorded its short
  account replaces the form; the exact plan is there on request.
- Any edit after a plan, Change, or another account signed in makes the plan
  authorize nothing; Publish Pull Request confirms exactly the digest of the
  plan the sheet shows.
- The description is the reviewer's Problem and Solution in the form a Review
  Tour maps (`insert_tour_brief`): this is the "manual problem/solution
  authoring path" reused. Publishing never prepares or publishes a tour.
- A pull request published with one's own tools is linked only after one read
  of exactly it under the reviewer's account, its own address read by the
  Home bar's parser (`parse_setup_destination`), and is recorded as
  externally published. Core and Qt hold no URL text; the architecture rule
  forbids it, so the composition hands the address to the Home bar.
- The window's account is the one its session accepted, through that
  account's own transport; it needs no pull request snapshot, and a `gh` CLI
  connection has no catalog.
- Compatibility decision, RC59-09: this build offers no promotion of local
  notes into GitHub comments. Notes stay local; the Review Tour on the opened
  pull request is written through the existing workflow, whose placement
  policy is unchanged, so no note is placed or retargeted. Promotion is
  [[BPR-1273]], and the owner is asked whether that split is acceptable.

said):

| `review_cycle_publish_desk` | Pass (real store, Git checkout and installed-Git catalog; simulated GitHub): RC61-08's baselines and rows; improvements-only dependency; signed out no plan; edit and Change drop a plan; publish to #17; only blob, tree, commit, branch and pull writes; the description exact; no note, reply or unselected file uploaded; external link refusals and the shorthand; after a reclaim and a restart R1 to R3 and every captured byte, the round, reply, decision, note and delivery remain, both links too; explicit removal removes them |
| `application_review_cycle_publish_public_path` | Pass, through the profile desktop: Open Project on a checkout, a private note, Capture revision, the sheet's words for P, T, R1 to R2 and each path, Review Plan, the exact plan, Publish Pull Request, "Pull request #17 is open" and the Updates line, a link by hand, Open Pull Request opening `octo-org/review-repository #17` in the existing workflow, the cycle resumed from Home with its note and both links |

Each fails its row |

### BPR-1178: the publication crash, drift and recovery matrix (2026-10-04)

Promoted from Backlog to Ready (`6b06aa67f`, under the mandate: an eligible,
dependency-complete card; BPR-1177 is Done) and claimed at `a83caa127`.
Implementation `4260d4bab`. Environment: macOS 26.6.2 arm64, Apple clang 21,
`build/dev` (Debug), Apple Git 2.50.1 as the catalog's Git.

Owned paths: `tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_publication_matrix.cpp`
and `tests/scripts/review_cycle_publication_matrix_tests.py` (new, with the
`review_cycle_publication_matrix` row and its `dos-volume-name` pin), the
simulated server's `save` and `load` in `simulated_github.hpp`, the
cancellation cases in `review_cycle_live_publication.cpp`, a stop between
sends in `review_cycle_publish_public_path_tests.cpp`, and the fixes below in
`review_cycle_workbench.cpp`, `review_cycle_publisher.cpp` and
`review_cycle_publish_sheet.cpp`, with the domain-model, threat-model and
build-and-test paragraphs.

The matrix. Each case is a course root and several real processes. The first
makes a checkout, a cycle with two captures, a plan through the desktop's
cycle host (`review_cycle_workbench`, real store, installed-Git catalog), and
presses Publish Pull Request against a simulated GitHub whose whole state is a
file in the root. A port in front of the server saves it on every call and,
at the case's boundary, writes `kill-ready` and waits; the driver kills it
there (SIGKILL; TerminateProcess on Windows) and checks the store it left with
SQLite (`integrity_check`, `foreign_key_check`, one publication) before
anything opens it. The restart process first checks that GitHub holds exactly
what the boundary promises, applies another actor's change if the case has
one, and without any read of GitHub shows: no authorization survives, no
attempt is left without an outcome, the phase is uncertain exactly when an
attempt was in flight, and the publisher sends nothing (not even a read)
without a fresh confirmation. Then, through the desktop host: Open Project
resumes the cycle, the sheet says GitHub did not answer, Check GitHub only
reads, and the case's ending follows. A third process reopens the outcome and
publishing again sends nothing. Every phase also checks that no step was
written more often than the plan has it and that there is never more than
one open pull request of ours for the head.

| Case | Dies at | While nothing ran | Ending |
|---|---|---|---|
| authorized | before the first read | | authorization spent; plan waits; confirmed again, every step once |
| before/after-blob, -tree, -commit, -branch | before the request leaves / after GitHub took it | | uncertain; read; plan waits with what GitHub holds; confirmed again, every step written exactly once |
| before-pull | before the request leaves | | read as absent; one pull request after a fresh confirmation |
| after-pull | after GitHub opened it | | associated by reading, never created again |
| after-pull-reread | before the read after creation | | linked; nothing to recover |
| recovery-killed | after the first blob; the restart dies again after its read | | a third process reads and finishes once |
| lost-pull-answer | (no death: answer lost) | | uncertain, no blind retry; the restart associates it |
| not-sent-branch | (no death: the request never left) | | the plan waits, not stuck publishing; a refused confirmation (journal changed underneath) leaves it waiting; reopened and confirmed, the rest is sent once |
| base-moved | after the commit | base pushed | `target_moved` before any further write, in words that say nothing more was sent and GitHub holds 4 steps; cancelled with nothing deleted |
| branch-taken | before the branch | the name taken at P | foreign, `target_drift`, nothing overwritten |
| branch-moved | after the branch | our branch force-moved | foreign, `target_drift`, nothing overwritten |
| pull-raced | before the pull request | another login opened one | foreign; one pull request, theirs |
| pull-ambiguous | after the pull request | ours closed, another of ours opened | stays uncertain; no third; the reviewer links one, recorded as their own link |
| head-pushed | after the pull request | someone pushed to the head | associated as ours with the drift recorded |
| cancel-after-tree | after the tree | | Cancel Publishing keeps the 3 confirmed steps and claims nothing undone |

`python3 tests/scripts/review_cycle_publication_matrix_tests.py --executable build/dev/tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_publication_matrix --git /usr/bin/git`
passes 22 of 22 (the ctest row `review_cycle_publication_matrix`, 27 s).

Defects the matrix found, fixed at their source:

- **The desktop could not continue after a recovery.** `stage_of` showed every
  journal between sends (authorized, uploading objects, branch created) as
  Publishing, whose actions are all disabled, and Cancel Publishing was only
  offered when stopped. After a restart and Check GitHub, or after a request
  that never left, the sheet was stuck: no way to confirm again or cancel.
  Publishing is now the stage only while a confirmed send is in flight; a
  journal read back between sends waits for the reviewer, says what GitHub
  holds ("Publishing stopped before the pull request. Publish Pull Request
  confirms this plan again and sends only what GitHub does not hold. GitHub
  holds the N steps already confirmed; nothing is undone."), and the sheet
  offers Publish Pull Request and Cancel Publishing.
- **A refused confirmation left the sheet stuck Publishing**; it now returns to
  its journal's stage.
- **A limit met after earlier steps said "Nothing was sent"** (for example the
  base moved after the commit was confirmed); it now says nothing more was
  sent and what GitHub holds. A refused confirmation's words do the same.
- **Recovery called this plan's own pull request foreign** when someone pushed
  to its head while the answer was lost (the live path records the same push
  as drift on the link). The sole candidate by the plan's login, from the
  branch this journal confirmed, is now associated with the drift recorded.

Negative controls, each applied, rebuilt, run and reverted:

| Mutation | Fails at |
|---|---|
| The old `stage_of` | after-blob, before-branch: "after recovery the plan waits for the reviewer, not stuck publishing (uploading_objects)" |
| No stage restore on refusal | not-sent-branch: "a refused confirmation leaves the plan waiting" |
| A restart keeps the authorization (core) | authorized, after-tree: "no authorization survives a restart" |
| Recovery marks a blob absent without reading | after-blob: "every step written exactly once (blob=3 …)" |
| The moved-head rule removed | head-pushed: recorded foreign instead of ours with drift |
| "Nothing was sent" after confirmed steps | base-moved: "the words never claim nothing was sent" |
| Pull request recovery finds nothing | after-pull, lost-pull-answer: "associated, not created again" |
| The checkpoint never waits to be killed | after-commit: "the process survived its kill point" |
| Cancel Publishing hidden between sends (Qt) | `application_review_cycle_publish_public_path`: "the stopped plan can be confirmed again or cancelled" |

Through the window, `application_review_cycle_publish_public_path` now makes
the branch request never leave: the sheet reads "Publish this plan?" with
Publish Pull Request enabled and Cancel Publishing shown, its status says
GitHub holds 4 steps and its summary "GitHub holds: blob, tree, commit."; a
second click publishes #17 as before.

Live, on the owner-designated `MikeMarcin/BPR-Test` only (the owner's
2026-09-30 designation; `gh` 2.101.0 signed in as `MikeMarcin`), the live
course gains two cases: a branch and a pull request write cancelled in flight,
the stop requested from another thread at three quarters of the same write's
last round trip. Run `20261004T151058Z` (log not committed,
`build/logs/bpr-1178/live-rc59-10-2.log`):

- cancelled-branch: stop after 543 ms of a 724 ms round trip. The journal says
  **uncertain**; GitHub **holds** the branch. No blind retry
  (`unauthorized`); recovery read it as this plan's at the planned commit;
  after a fresh confirmation PR #39 was created, one for the head.
- cancelled-pull: stop after 1192 ms of 1590 ms. The journal says
  **uncertain**; GitHub **holds** the pull request. Recovery associated #40;
  publishing again sent nothing; one pull request for the head.
- The seven earlier cases passed again (ready #34, draft #35, lost branch
  #36, lost pull #37, foreign branch refused with zero writes, raced branch,
  raced pull #38 another actor's), and all 12 of the driver's `git fetch` and
  `gh api` checks.

An earlier run that day (`20261004T150849Z`, PRs #27 to #33) stopped both
cancellations at 40 ms: each was **not sent** and GitHub held neither, which
also holds (a request that left is never called not sent). Nothing on GitHub
was deleted, closed or force-updated; both runs' branches under
`bpr-rc59-10/20261004T150849Z/` and `bpr-rc59-10/20261004T151058Z/` and PRs
#27 to #40 are left as evidence. `build/dev` was configured back to
`BPR_BUILD_LIVE_PUBLICATION_COURSE=OFF`.

Other checks at `4260d4bab`: a full `-k 0` dev build; 56 of 56 focused rows
(`core_review_cycle_*`, `review_cycle_*`, `sqlite_review_cycle_storage`,
`qt_review_cycle_view_*`, `application_review_cycle_*`,
`github_publication_contract`, `application_review_round_trip_composition_contract`,
`application_github_host`, `github_provider`, `native_https_transport_fixture`);
clang-format and `tidy.py --warnings-as-errors` on the changed units.
`architecture_boundaries` and `test_network_isolation_manifest` fail in the
checkout only because another agent's worktree sits in `.claude/worktrees/`;
on a clean export of the tree `architecture_boundaries` passes, and the
manifest's only complaint in that fresh, unbuilt configure is
`gh_cli_git_smart_transport`'s executable, which this card did not touch; it
names nothing of this card's.

Acceptance:

- **RC59-07, pass for this card's share.** Object, branch and pull request
  uncertainty, cancellation and concurrent drift recover without a duplicate
  pull request, a blind retry, a force update or an unearned rollback claim:
  the matrix above, real processes killed at every boundary, the controls,
  and live cancellations GitHub took and the journal held uncertain until
  read.
- **RC59-10, pass for this card's share.** Live cancellation cases on the
  designated GitHub.com target, with the confirmed remote effects recorded;
  the external link in pull-ambiguous is recorded as the reviewer's own link,
  and a native pull request whose head moved is associated as native with its
  drift, not left for an external link that would misreport it. A timeout case
  was not run live: the production transport has no timeout seam, and adding
  one is outside this card.

Residual races, recorded rather than closed:

- A pull request the reviewer opens by hand, under the same login, from the
  plan's branch while the plan's own answer is lost, is indistinguishable from
  the plan's and is associated as native (the same holds before this card,
  for an unmoved head).
- Between Check GitHub and the next confirmation another actor can still move
  the base or take a name; the publisher's capability reads catch that before
  any write, but GitHub has no compare-and-swap across the sequence.
- A cancelled publication is not shown again when the sheet reopens (it only
  lends its destination); its journal, with what GitHub holds, stays in the
  store.

### BPR-1273: local notes promoted into the linked pull request's Review Tour (2026-10-04)

Promoted from Backlog to Ready (`626321ba4`, under the mandate: an eligible,
dependency-complete card; BPR-1177 is Done) and claimed at `38814604a`.
Implementation `44e7b8a6c`. Environment: macOS 26.6.2 arm64, Apple clang 21,
`build/dev` (Debug), Qt 6.11.2, Apple Git 2.50.1. No live GitHub was
contacted: the publication's GitHub is `simulated_github`, and the pull
request the window opens is the profile-window fixture's recorded one.

Owned paths: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_promotion.hpp` and
`.cpp` (`promote_note`, `promotion_needs_file`, `published_blob`,
`promoted_destination`); `publication_source` and `publication_observation`
in `review_tour_destination.hpp`/`.cpp`, now also what
`src/app/src/tour_publication_plan.cpp` observes (behaviour unchanged);
`src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_promotion.hpp` and
`.cpp` (`review_cycle_promoter`, `review_cycle_tour_port`);
`src/adapters/qt/include/bpr/qt/review_cycle_promote_sheet.hpp` and `.cpp`;
the cycle view's Add Notes to Review Tour… action and `present_promotion`;
`review_cycle_desktop`'s `desktop_tour_port`, promoter and Open Pull Request
from the sheet; `github_application.cpp`'s route from each window's desktop
to `desktop_tour_owner_route` (round-trip pins resealed with the review in
the contract); `tests/core/review_cycle_promotion_tests.cpp`;
`tests/workspace_contract/stored_tour_port.hpp`; the promotion cases of
`review_cycle_publication_matrix` and its driver; the promotion steps of
`review_cycle_publish_public_path_tests.cpp`; the domain-model and
threat-model paragraphs.

Design decisions:

- Promotion is a local tour edit, never a GitHub write. The tour is
  published only from the pull request, through its existing preview, exact
  plan and confirmation, so "what goes to GitHub" stays that preview's job;
  the promotion sheet's ledger says nothing goes to GitHub now.
- A stop's body is the note's current wording (the latest revision of its
  first turn), unchanged. Its title is the note's location (the file, the
  folder or "The whole change"), which the reviewer may edit in the tour.
- Exact lines are claimed only from proof: a note written on the published
  revision, whose path the plan writes with exactly the note's bytes
  (`candidate_blob::Content`, no line-ending transformation, a regular file),
  and whose bytes read back from the cycle's store hash to the object the
  plan names. They become the stop's remote address
  (`tour_stop::Destination`) at the published commit, zero-based as tour
  sources count. No retained comparison identity is fabricated, so a
  promoted stop is never ready by itself: the reviewer completes it in the
  pull request (navigate, then use the selection), as with an imported stop.
- Every other note carries no source and is never pointed at a current line:
  an earlier capture, other bytes at the path (an unselected change, a
  normalised line ending, a deletion), a file outside the commit's changes, a
  whole file, a folder, the whole change, or a pull request linked by hand.
- What a stop becomes on GitHub is `core::plan_tour_destination`, observed
  exactly as tour publication observes it; the observation now lives in core
  (`core::publication_observation`) and `prepare_tour_plan` uses it, so the
  sheet and the publication cannot drift apart.
- Adding goes where the draft lives: the profile's tour owner
  (`desktop_tour_owner_route`, the path `bpr tour` uses) runs the edit on the
  window holding that draft, or on the stored draft. Each note is one request
  whose identity is the cycle (catalog and number), the note and the pull
  request. The owner route recomputes a request's digest from the whole edit,
  including its expected revision, so a resent request would be refused, not
  replayed; the promoter therefore reads the tour's receipt for that identity
  first and sends nothing for a note the tour already answered.
- A tour exists for a pull request once it has been opened on the profile
  signed in; a signed-out public preview starts none. The sheet says so and
  offers Open Pull Request. With several links, the most recent link whose
  pull request has a tour on this profile is used.

Checks (macOS arm64, `build/dev`, offscreen):

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `core_review_cycle_promotion` | Pass: a note on the published lines and an off-hunk note keep their exact lines at the published commit, proved by the bytes; a historical, whole-cycle, folder, whole-file, outside-changes, deleted, normalised and other-bytes note, and every note of a hand-linked pull request, carry no source and are unresolved until the reviewer chooses lines; the request identity is stable per cycle, note and pull request, case-insensitive in its owner, and another catalog's cycle is another request; a reworded note is the same request with other content; the promoted destination is the publication policy's own (a line comment at the head, a conversation permalink once the head moved on); the tour's own insert takes the stop and nothing is publishable until it is completed |
| `review_cycle_publication_matrix` (with `review_cycle_publication_matrix_tests.py`) | Pass, 28 cases: the 22 publication cases unchanged, and six promotion cases below |
| `application_review_cycle_publish_public_path` | Pass, through the profile desktop: a note on a published line typed on a selection; publish; the account signed in; Open Pull Request opens #17, which starts its tour; back to the cycle, Add Notes to Review Tour… finds the tour of #17 and says "The whole change: … No lines: the note is about the whole change." and "src/parse.cpp: … Line 2, exactly as the pull request's commit holds them. When the tour is published: a comment linking these lines, since the pull request has moved on." (the fixture's #17 has another head); the line note is checked and added through `desktop_tour_owner_route`; the stored tour holds one stop with the note's words at line 2 of the published commit, editing, not ready; adding it again says it was already in the tour and the tour still holds one stop; GitHub received nothing |
| Integration selection (`core_review_tour*`, `core_review_cycle_*`, `application_review_tour_*`, `sqlite_tour_owner_channel`, `sqlite_review_tour_storage`, `sqlite_review_cycle_*`, `review_cycle_*` except the scale bench, `qt_review_cycle_view_*`, `application_review_cycle_*`, `github_publication_contract`, `application_github_host`, the round-trip pin) | 75 of 75 pass |
| `architecture_boundaries` | Pass on a clean export of the working tree (in the checkout it fails only on another agent's worktree under `.claude/worktrees/`) |
| `test_network_isolation_manifest` | On the clean export's fresh, unbuilt configure its only complaint is `gh_cli_git_smart_transport`'s executable, as recorded for BPR-1178; it names nothing of this card's |
| clang-format; `tidy.py --warnings-as-errors` on the changed translation units | Clean for this card's code; the remaining findings in `review_tour_destination.cpp` and `github_application.cpp` are on unchanged lines |

The promotion cases. After one clean publication, Add Notes to Review Tour
adds the cycle's five notes (on R1, on the published lines, on a line the
change did not touch, on the whole change, on a folder) through the tour's
real SQLite store, behind a port that dies at a request's boundary: before it
reaches the store, or after its stop and receipt are durable and before the
answer. The driver kills it there and checks both stores with SQLite before
anything opens them. A restart asks again under the same identities, and a
last process reopens, offers a reworded note (already in, nothing sent) and
adds all five again (all already in). Every phase checks that each note's
stop is in the tour at most once (exactly once when complete), with its words
unchanged, exact lines only for the two notes on the published bytes, nothing
publishable, and that GitHub received nothing, not even a read.

| Case | Dies at | Restart |
|---|---|---|
| promote-clean | (no death) | first, a pull request never opened has no tour and nothing is written; then 5 added; again, 5 already |
| promote-before-first | before the first request | 5 added |
| promote-after-first | after the first stop is durable | 1 already, 4 added |
| promote-after-third | after the third | 3 already, 2 added |
| promote-after-last | after the fifth | 5 already |
| promote-lost-answer | (no death: the second answer lost) | the sheet says it did not hear and how to finish; asked again, 2 already, 3 added |

Negative controls, each applied, rebuilt, run and reverted:

| Mutation | Fails at |
|---|---|
| Lines proved by the path alone, not the bytes | `core_review_cycle_promotion`: "bytes that do not hash to the published object prove nothing" |
| A historical note given a convenient current line | `core_review_cycle_promotion`: "a historical note: no line is chosen for it"; promote-clean: "the historical stop carries no current line" |
| A request identity per attempt | promote-after-first: "the stops already in are not added again (5 added, 0 already)" |
| A lost answer taken as added | promote-lost-answer: "the lost answer is said" |
| No receipt read before sending | `application_review_cycle_publish_public_path`: "added again, nothing changes: The tour refused 1 note" (through the owner route a resent request is refused, not replayed) |
| The promotion kill point never waits | promote-after-third: "the process survived its kill point" |

Acceptance:

- **RC59-09, pass for this card's share.** The actual pull request is linked
  (BPR-1177's share) and its existing tour semantics are reused: a chosen
  note joins the linked pull request's Review Tour as a stop through the
  tour's own store, receipts and owner; its source and planned destination
  are what the existing placement policy gives; historical and off-commit
  notes are never retargeted to convenient current lines; stops wait for the
  reviewer; no private history is uploaded and no GitHub review event or
  comment is written by promotion. With BPR-1177's share, RFC-BPR-0059's
  RC59-09 row has its evidence for closeout ([[BPR-1192]]).

No criterion of this card needs the owner, so no attended session was opened.
The sheet's words have not been judged by the owner.

Limits, none gating:

- A note on a file the commit did not change gets no lines, even when its
  bytes equal the base's: nothing here reads P's tree to prove them.
- A pull request is promotable only after it was opened signed in on this
  profile; a signed-out public preview starts no tour.
- With several links the most recent one with a tour is used; there is no
  chooser.
- The tour's own publication was not run again: it is unchanged apart from
  its observation moving into core, covered by the `application_review_tour_*`
  rows. GitHub is simulated or recorded throughout.
- The kill points are the tour port's request boundaries; a death inside the
  tour store's commit is left to its SQLite transaction, which
  `integrity_check` confirms after every kill.

Owed station runs (not gating):

- Linux (`bpr-main-linux`): `git pull --rebase --autostash && cmake --build --preset dev && ctest --preset dev -j4 --output-on-failure -R "^core_review_cycle_promotion$|^review_cycle_publication_matrix$|^application_review_cycle_publish_public_path$|^application_review_round_trip_composition_contract$|^qt_review_cycle_view_|^application_review_tour_publication_|^architecture_boundaries$"`
- Windows (`bpr-main-win`, Debug): `git pull --rebase --autostash; cmake --build --preset windows-dev; ctest --preset windows-dev -j4 --output-on-failure -R "^core_review_cycle_promotion$|^review_cycle_publication_matrix$|^application_review_cycle_publish_public_path$|^application_review_round_trip_composition_contract$|^qt_review_cycle_view_|^application_review_tour_publication_|^test_network_isolation_manifest$"`
  (the matrix row stays pinned `dos-volume-name`; the publish course runs
  uncontained with its measured line).

### BPR-1184: large-project responsiveness, and the steering question (2026-10-04)

Mandate of 2026-10-03 ("find and implement available unattended milestones,
keep going until you run out of candidates"), with the amendment allowing
station work and promotion of an eligible Backlog card. BPR-1184 was promoted
at `0bdec4309` (BPR-1166 and BPR-1171 Done) and claimed at `c71ce1258`. No
selection file was supplied, so the milestone helper's binding was not run;
the card holds the checkpoint.

Owned paths: the capture lane, progress and cancel in
`review_cycle_runtime`, `review_cycle_capture` and `review_cycle_workbench`
(with the projection's `Capturing`, `FilesFirst`, `FilesEarlier` and
`CapturedEntries`); the desktop's capture worker in `review_cycle_desktop`;
the Updates line, Cancel capture, the drawer's windows and the over-budget
words in `review_cycle_view`; the too-large status in

Measured on named corpora through the real desktop and `bpr cycle`, the page
had five defects at scale, and the fixes exposed a sixth; all are fixed in
`397908dec`. A capture stopped every read (Next file waited 9,836.8 ms during a
100,000-file capture) and ran on the shared workbench effect thread; a 9–14 s
capture showed no progress and could not be stopped; the drawer stopped at
4,096 files with the rest unreachable; a file over the 128 MiB budget read as
"not available" with no reason; the over-limit refusal did not name its
limit; and tab saves waited on the capture's write lock. The
[evidence](BPR-1184-large-project-2026-10-04.md) gives the method, every
before-and-after figure and the negative controls.

- RC54-08 (this card's share): at `397908dec`, macOS Release, the drawer and

  last file opened; changed-only pages exactly the edited files), a capture is
  cancellable from the window within 7.47 ms and records nothing, hashing runs

  (SHA-256 `4a649b44…947291c`). Windows and Linux reports are owed.

  Mixed and negative: a 514-item round takes about 3 s per note change and

- RC61-10 (human steering benefit): needs a person. Asked as

CLI, handoff, publish, parity, two-round, desktop, network-isolation and

### BPR-1183: the hostile-input, data-loss and mixed-cycle recovery corpus (2026-10-04)

Mandate of 2026-10-03 ("find and implement available unattended milestones,
keep going until you run out of candidates"), with the amendment allowing
station work and promotion of an eligible Backlog card. BPR-1183 was promoted
at `e310cc48a` (BPR-1152, BPR-1160, BPR-1165, BPR-1178 and BPR-1181 Done) and
claimed at `6a3047c59`. No selection file was supplied, so the milestone
helper's binding was not run; the card holds the checkpoint. Landed: `60c438c02` (rebased from `ef4947ebc` over `eeb96af6d`, which
changed only BPR-1181's card).

Owned paths:

- Course: `tests/end_to_end/review_cycle_recovery_corpus.py` and its row
  `application_review_cycle_recovery_corpus` in `tests/end_to_end/CMakeLists.txt`;
  the desktop driver's `capture-refused` gesture in
  `review_cycle_two_round_public_path_tests.cpp`.
- Store: `cycle_store_failure::store_damaged` (`review_cycle_store.hpp`) and
  its classification in `review_cycle_store.cpp`, with
  `refuses_damaged_stores_as_damaged` in
  `tests/sqlite_contract/review_cycle_storage_tests.cpp`.
- Words: `review_cycle_words.cpp` (the root and damaged-store words, shared by
  the window and the terminal) and `cycle_commands.cpp` (capture, compare and
  repeated-operation refusals).
- Documents: [the CLI guide](../development/cycle-cli.md) ("Working while
  the desktop is open") and this section.

The corpus. One course root holds a profile with two cycles: `alpha`, which
is attacked, and `beta`, which must never change. The shipped `bpr cycle` and
the reviewer's composed desktop run as real processes. After every case the
course checks the store with SQLite's `integrity_check` and with queries for a
revision, pin, round or response naming an object the store lacks, or an
object missing a chunk. It also checks that `alpha`'s F1 exports byte for
byte as first frozen, that `beta`'s cycle, round and folder are unchanged,
and that the cycle is exactly as before or exactly as after the interrupted
command. The parts print:

| Part | Marker | What it shows |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `RECOVERY_KILL_POINTS_OK` | Capture, note, goal, freeze, response import and a bundle save, each killed (SIGKILL) at its first store write and at 25, 50, 75 and 95% of its measured run: 30 kills. Each leaves the cycle before or after, never between. Repeated with its `--operation-id`, each is applied exactly once. A save leaves the round's exact bytes or nothing, and no temporary file. The store check is shown failing on a copy that lost one pinned object. |
| 2 | `RECOVERY_OWNER_KILLED_OK` | The desktop owns the profile and is killed while writing a forwarded capture. `bpr` exits 6 `uncertain`; the repeat applies it once; a new desktop shows the capture once. |
| 3 | `RECOVERY_DISK_FULL_OK` | Under a 64 KiB file-size limit on the writer (POSIX `RLIMIT_FSIZE`, `SIGXFSZ` ignored), a 2 MiB capture, a 200 KiB response, a round of four 15 KiB notes and a 60 KiB goal, and that round's bundle saved to a file are each refused as `storage_unavailable` or `destination_unavailable`. Nothing is recorded or claimed, and nothing is left at the chosen name. With space again each succeeds once. |
| 4 | `RECOVERY_DAMAGED_MATERIAL_OK` | A corrupted R1-only pin is `source_unavailable` and shows neither the damaged nor the live bytes; a sibling pin reads. A pin removed from the store is "are missing", and an unlisted path is `not_found`. A corrupted pin whose bytes are still in the folder is repaired by `capture --again`. A damaged F1 writes no markdown or bundle, prints nothing, grants nothing, and stays listed. `beta` reads throughout. |
| 5 | `RECOVERY_DAMAGED_STORE_OK` | A store whose header or pages SQLite cannot read is `storage_unavailable` and "damaged" for `list`, `show` and `open`. It is left byte for byte, and nothing is created beside it. A lost store lists nothing and `show RC-1` is `not_found`. The folder opens as a new `RC-1` whose F1 is new, and a reply to the lost F1 is refused (`wrong_digest`). The reviewer's copy, restored, reads as before. |
| 6 | `RECOVERY_STALE_AND_ROOTS_OK` | A goal and a freeze against a moved draft revision are `stale_revision` with nothing changed. A writer that never stops makes capture `capture_unstable` with nothing recorded; once quiet, it captures. A root replaced at its location is `source_unavailable` with no `Next:`, in words saying another folder answers there, and the window says the same through Capture revision. A link at the location to another folder is refused, nothing is recorded, and the folder it names is unchanged. The folder now there opens as its own cycle. The moved original is recognised as RC-1 by identity, but RC-1 reads its old location ("no longer exists"). Moved back, it captures again. |
| 7 | `RECOVERY_DUPLICATES_OK` | The same response twice is one response; another under its identifier is `conflict` (`reused_identifier`); duplicate JSON keys are `invalid_input`; a repeated `--operation-id` is `conflict` in the new words. Nothing changes. |
| 8 | `RECOVERY_CASE_COLLISIONS_OK` | On a case-sensitive volume (a sparse APFS image the course attaches on macOS; natively on Linux), `src/Cache.cpp` and `src/cache.cpp` are captured, noted, carried as context, frozen and exported as two paths. A granted harness's `round source` lists both. The bundle has no two members a case-insensitive reader merges, and the folder is unchanged. |
| 9 | `RECOVERY_MIXED_BUILDS_OK` | With `--older-cycle-application` and `--agent-free-cycle-application`: a schema-9 `bpr` writes a profile (open, note, freeze, export, response). The current desktop opens it first and shows the note and the response. The current terminal and an agent-free `bpr` read F1 byte for byte through the owner; the agent-free one adds a note the window shows. The older `bpr`, through the current owner, is served. Alone, it refuses the upgraded store as `unsupported_version` and leaves it as it is. |

Defects found by the corpus and fixed at their source:

- **A damaged store was called another version's.** A store whose header or
  pages SQLite could not read failed every schema check and was reported as
  saved by a different version of BetterPullReview (`unsupported_version`),
  which sends the reviewer looking for another build. When no schema matches,
  the store now asks SQLite's `quick_check`. A file it cannot read, or whose
  pages fail the check, is `store_damaged`: "This profile's Review Cycles
  store is damaged and cannot be read; it was left as it is. Restore it from
  a backup of the profile." (`storage_unavailable`). One it could not read
  because it was busy is unavailable. Only a sound database with another
  schema is another version's. The check runs only when no schema matched.
- **A replaced root was said to be replaced while it was read, with a retry.**
  `root_changed` covers a location that answers with another root before
  reading as well as during it. Both said "The chosen folder was replaced
  while it was read" as `capture_unstable`, with `Next: bpr cycle capture`,
  which reads the same folder and is refused again. The words now say another
  folder answers at the location, that capturing again reads the same folder,
  and what does help: move the original back, or open the folder now there
  as a cycle of its own. A changed repository and a changed kind have words
  of their own. The terminal answers `source_unavailable` with no `Next:`.
- **Missing retained bytes were "no file".** A pin whose object the store no
  longer held read as `missing`, which `compare --path` reported as
  `not_found` and the note path as "R1 holds no file". A path the compared
  revisions list is now `source_unavailable`, "The retained bytes of PATH are
  missing; nothing was substituted". `not_found` is kept for a path neither
  lists, now worded "Neither R1 nor R2 holds PATH." The window already said
  "not available".
- **A repeat of an applied command said its ID named another request.** The
  terminal reads the cycle before it writes, so a command repeated after it
  wrote reads the cycle that run moved, and its request differs. The repeat
  was refused (never applied twice), but as "That operation ID was already
  used for another request". It now says the earlier run with the ID may have
  completed, or the ID named another request, and to read the cycle. The
  owner's in-memory answers for a live owner are unchanged.

Decisions and findings that are not defects:

- **Self-run repeats are refused, not replayed.** Making a terminal repeat
  replay would mean taking what the first run read out of the store's
  request identity. That is a change to every write's identity, not this
  card's. The guide now says what a repeat after a write answers.
- **A goal or freeze repeated after it applied is `stale_revision`.** The
  terminal checks the draft revision before it reaches the store's receipt.
  The words are true ("Nothing was changed"/"frozen"), and reading the draft
  shows the first run's change.
- **A moved root cannot rejoin its cycle.** The core requires an explicit,
  confirmed rebind with a material check (BPR-1148). No command or window
  offers one, so a moved project keeps its cycle only by moving back.
  Recorded as an open gap, not a pass for S05's rebind half. The
  follow-up card is reported to the coordinator: this worktree is not the
  main integrator, so it could not allocate an ID.
- **A link at the root's own location to the same folder is read.** Identity
  decides, so a link that names the bound folder itself captures it. A link
  to another folder is another root and is refused.
- **A full disk is "The local cycle store is unavailable."** True, if not
  specific; SQLite's out-of-space is not told apart from other write
  failures. Not changed.
- **Bundle import has no shipped entry point**, as BPR-1181 found, so the
  hostile-archive half of S15 and RC56-05 remains the core's, the host's and
  the fuzz target's (BPR-1160, BPR-1162).

Criteria (this card's share; [[BPR-1187]], [[BPR-1189]] and [[BPR-1193]] judge
the rows whole):

- **RC54-03 passes on macOS, this card's share.** Through the shipped `bpr`
  and the window, a root replaced at its location, a link there to another
  folder, and a moved root are each refused, and nothing is read from them.
  Each refusal says retrying will not help. The folder now there opens only
  as a cycle of its own. Unsafe descendant paths and link escapes are
  BPR-1148's and BPR-1181's unchanged evidence. Explicit rebind has no
  product surface and stays open.
- **RC56-05 passes, this card's share, at the seams that exist.** No bundle
  import is shipped, so the hostile-archive corpus is BPR-1160's and
  BPR-1162's core, host and fuzz evidence. Their rows passed again here.
  Through `bpr`, duplicate JSON keys are refused and a bundle saved under a
  full disk or a kill is whole or absent. Case-colliding paths make no
  aliasing members.
- **RC60-05 passes on macOS, this card's share.** A duplicate, a reused
  identifier and duplicate keys are refused with nothing changed. A reply to
  a lost round is refused in the new cycle that took its name. A corrupted
  round exports, grants and prints nothing, and a corrupted pin never shows
  damaged or live bytes in its place. Execution and forged-authority
  refusals are BPR-1181's unchanged evidence.
- **RC60-06 passes on macOS, this card's share.** Kill points across capture,
  notes, goals, freezes, responses and saves, an owner killed mid-write and a
  full disk leave complete records or none, with no dangling object and no
  claimed delivery. A damaged store is left untouched and said to be damaged.
  A lost store reconstructs nothing, and the reviewer's own copy restores it.
  An older build refuses an upgraded store without writing it. Another
  cycle in the profile never changes. Owner-channel bounds and identity are
  BPR-1181's unchanged evidence.

Limits: Linux and Windows built nothing here. The full disk is POSIX only. A
case-sensitive folder needs Linux or an attachable disk image. Kill timing is
sampled, not exhaustive: each operation's kills land before and after its
commit across runs, but no single run is guaranteed to hit both. The older
and agent-free builds join only when given; the ctest row skips them. No
second OS user ran, and the installed packages were not exercised
(BPR-1182).
