What the earlier cards left for this one, and what became of it:

- **BPR-1166 and BPR-1177: Share notes folds at 900 pixels.** Measured, the
  cycle's review bar wanted 967 pixels on cocoa in its plain full-source state
  and 1,055 offscreen; at 900 the toolbar folded Share notes, then Comment,
  Suggest change and Notes into its overflow. The workbench specification
  says no control folds down to the platform's floor. The bar now takes the
  pull request bar's forms in three places: one button names the view shown
  and opens the chooser (the label and the separate View button were
  redundant), Show newer is the refresh glyph and Mark reviewed the
  private-progress tick, each named in its tooltip and accessible name. It
  also yields RFC-BPR-0052's way as the window narrows: the view's revisions,
  then the position's words, then its numbers. The widest state tested (a
  named comparison, newer material, two open notes, Capture revision's and
  Share notes' menus) needs 875 pixels on cocoa with nothing yielded and 889
  offscreen after two yields. The doubled-font question stays BPR-1255's: no
  yield was added to the pull request bar and the floor is unchanged. The
  candidate's look is the owner's question below.
- **BPR-1171: the Original line's CR.** A note on a whole CRLF line showed the
  line's CR. `first_code_line` drops the ending's CR; the view test asserts the

  owner's question below.

| Package | `verify_release_package.py` | Families | Files | Executable | Composition audit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Each SPDX `sourceInfo` is `git:7a25821b1…; clean=True; tests-disabled Release;
Darwin`, with `; agent integrations absent` and `; local automation absent` as
the families go. An earlier full package recorded `clean=False` because a
course file was being written during its build; the audit refused it
(`package-source-identity-mismatch`) and it was rebuilt.

`installed_cycle_package_course.py` then installs full, agent-free, agent-free
automation-off and full again into one place, over one clean profile, and
starts each natively on cocoa: each composed its profile, started no child
process (sampled every 50 ms for five idle seconds), held no network socket
and ran no PATH canary; each bound its activation endpoint, and the owner
command endpoint was bound by the full and agent-free packages and not by the
automation-off one (read from the process's own bound sockets, not from
files left by a terminated launch). A cycle made with `bpr` before the first
swap (R1, a note on a CRLF line, F1 frozen) read back byte for byte after
each package ran, F1's export `f9084b9f…` each time. Negative controls: told
the automation-off package has automation, the course fails "owner command
endpoint absent"; told the agent-free package has agents, it fails "MCP helper
missing".

The default data root is resolved through Foundation, which standardises
`/private/var/…` to `/var/…`, and the store refuses a root whose canonical path
differs, so a package started with `HOME` under the system temporary directory
stops at `STARTUP_FAILED`. Under a real home it starts. The course therefore
makes its root under the account's home; this is not a product defect for a
person's own home.

An installed package cannot be driven from outside on this machine: macOS
returned the application element for every window query of the Qt windows
(`AXWindows`, hit tests, focused and main window), so no accessibility course
could reach the installed GUI. The one installed-GUI loop is therefore the
owner's question below.

### BPR-1280, BPR-1281 and BPR-1282: the owner's three BPR-1182 notes (2026-10-04)

The same mandate, with the amendment that other stations' work may be taken
over. Each note became a card (allocated in `d53bb742d`, claimed in
`a3f73dfb0`, `f471b223c` and `fc5d01032`). Candidate `aa6a47104`. Commits:
`b81eed2d6`, `3aa25c574` and `aa6a47104` (BPR-1282), `9a3e8e99d` (BPR-1281),
`c1cd6b585` (BPR-1280, with RFC-BPR-0061's record of both requests).

- **[[BPR-1280]], Command-Return saves.** A `QShortcut` on the cycle page,
  live only while a note, a reply or Suggest change is open, saves the
  composer that holds keyboard focus, exactly as its Save does. Keys: Qt's
  Control with Return and with Enter, which is Command-Return on macOS and
  Control-Enter on Windows and Linux. A suggestion with nothing to save stays
  open. Save note, Save reply and Save suggestion name the key on the button
  and in the tooltip: ⌘↩ on macOS (U+2318 U+21A9, the glyph its menus use; Qt
  writes ↵), `Ctrl+Enter` elsewhere. Collisions: no other binding of
  Control- or Command-Return exists in `src/`. While editing, the code view
  inserted a line break for Return with any modifier; Command-Return now
  saves instead, and the Mac's physical Control-Return (Qt's Meta) still
  breaks the line. Return is unchanged everywhere.
- **[[BPR-1281]], the comparison's colours.** The pane never had them. It
  has been a plain `QPlainTextEdit` since `48ba9741d` ([[BPR-1163]]), and
  `3ac3d0f1f` touched none of its lines. Each side's rows are joined by LF,
  so a row's line is the same line of the text that is classified. Both
  sides go to the desktop's code syntax host (the packaged classifier the
  share sheet uses), and the rows in view are painted through
  `code_sample_painter` (`code_syntax_format_ranges_at`,
  `apply_syntax_format_ranges`), in the panes' diff fills, with the line
  numbers in the placeholder colour. A classification is adopted only for a
  side's exact text.
- **[[BPR-1282]], the Send box.** `core::harness_name` names a profile's
  harness: Claude for Claude's stream protocol, Codex for either Codex
  protocol, and a custom harness by its own display name. Each offer
  carries it with its protocol. The box says one line ("Runs Codex in
  send-project.", "Starts a new Claude session in send-project.",
  "Continues Claude session … in send-project."). Details, a flat text
  button that reads Hide details while open, holds the rest: the exact text
  as Copy gives it, the harness's own settings and sign-in, no credential
  passed, no fallback, and the full folder. A screen reader hears the details
  as the line's description. The agent list names harnesses. The Session
  row shows only for a harness that continues sessions and has one to
  continue. The statuses ("Sending F1 to Codex…", with session words only
  for Claude), the refusal and the reply label name the harness the same
  way. A new reply is stored as from "Claude (Send to Agent)"; replies
  already stored keep their words. Words shown went from 44 to 7, with 29
  behind Details.

Checks, macOS 26.6.2 arm64, Apple clang 21, Qt 6.11.2, `build/dev` (Debug,
offscreen unless named):

- `qt_review_cycle_view_1_0/_2_0`, with window-system keys: Command-Return
  saves a two-line note whole and gives the code focus back, saves by the

## Optional adapters milestone

### BPR-1179: removable agent integrations and local automation (2026-10-04)

Mandate of 2026-10-03 ("find and implement available unattended milestones,
keep going until you run out of candidates"), with the amendment allowing
station work and promotion of an eligible Backlog card. BPR-1179 was promoted
at `f62213774` (BPR-1146, BPR-1167 and BPR-1171 Done) and claimed at
`3691cc379`. No selection file was supplied, so the milestone helper's
binding was not run; the card holds the checkpoint.

The verified candidate is `c3c7d6b3c`. It landed as `192ffb56b` after a rebase
over `12b7faa1c` (whitespace in nine files, including
`review_cycle_workbench.cpp`), `7d295d444`, `6a118a565` and `8d838c696`. No
path this card owns differs, and the round-trip pins match both trees. Two
clang-format-only commits of mine, covering the same files, were dropped in
the rebase as already upstream.

Owned paths:

- Switches: root `CMakeLists.txt` (`BPR_BUILD_AGENT_INTEGRATIONS`,
  `BPR_BUILD_LOCAL_AUTOMATION`, and the refusal of `BPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI` without
  automation).
- Agent family in the build: `src/adapters/agent/CMakeLists.txt` (the
  header-only `BetterPullReview::WorkspaceGuard`, the supervisor in every
  graph, and the launch adapter only with agents),
  `src/adapters/mcp/CMakeLists.txt`, `src/adapters/CMakeLists.txt`,
  `src/adapters/git/CMakeLists.txt` (Windows Git links the guard target),
  `src/application/CMakeLists.txt`, the guarded launch and protocol code in
  `src/application/src/agent_launch.cpp`, and `src/app/CMakeLists.txt`.
- The guarded composition: `src/app/src/github_application.cpp`.
- Presentation: `workbench_window::withdraw_agent_surfaces` and
  `settings_window::withdraw_page`.
- Packaging: `packaging/Install.cmake`, `package_runtime.py`, `package.py`,
  `CMakePresets.json` (six variant presets, the agent-free `tour-cli`), and
  `tools/release.sh --variant`.
- Audits: `scripts/verify_composed_hosts.py` (agent and automation anchor
  families) and `scripts/verify_release_package.py`.
- Courses: `tests/end_to_end/review_cycle_two_round_contract.py`
  (`--without-local-automation`, `--reopen-application`, the owner command
  endpoint check), with the endpoint expectation in
  `review_cycle_two_round_public_path_tests.cpp` and the assistance
  expectation in `review_tour_composition_public_path.cpp`.
- Test-graph gating in the `tests/*/CMakeLists.txt` files, and the unit tests
  of both audits.

Decisions and compatibility:

- **Switch names.** The spec's candidate `BPR_BUILD_AGENT_INTEGRATIONS` is kept.
  Automation is `BPR_BUILD_LOCAL_AUTOMATION`, not `BPR_BUILD_CYCLE_AUTOMATION`:
  the owner command channel and `bpr` serve tours as well as cycles.
- **Local automation is the owner command endpoint plus `bpr`.** Without it, no
  endpoint is bound and no cycle owner service is compiled. The tour owner
  service is still made: the desktop's own promotion into a linked pull
  request's tour (BPR-1273) routes through it in process.
- **Supervisor.** `better_pull_review_agent_supervisor` stays in every package,
  agent-free included. [[BPR-0622]] made it the owner of guarded Git launches,
  and it holds no agent protocol. The file name is kept; see the release
  packages guide.
- **What an agent-free image still holds.** It keeps the core agent data
  types and the codecs for stored records: private findings, writing results
  and session catalogs, which old reviews need. It keeps Git's cleanup of
  disposable agent workspaces, and the Qt panel classes, which the window
  builds and then withdraws. Nothing in the image can launch an agent, frame
  its input, decode a provider protocol or serve MCP: the audit forbids those
  anchors.
- **Package identity.** Every variant has the same identity, and its artifacts
  are named for the variant. Installing one variant over another replaces
  program files only. Windows and Linux replacement is not qualified natively.

Environment: macOS 26.6.2 arm64, Apple clang 21, Qt 6.11.2, Release
tests-enabled graphs from the `dev` preset. No live GitHub was contacted and no
model was invoked; agent-named executables placed first on PATH were never run.

Graphs and suites, each the whole registered suite except the 720 s
large-project row:

| Graph | Families | Result | Failures |
|---|---|---|---|
| `build/bpr1179-off-dev`, agents and automation OFF | neither | 543 of 546 | the round-trip pin and the composition contract before the reseal, which pass after it; BPR-1236 |
| the same tree with automation ON and `bpr` built | automation only | 555 of 558, then 5 of 5 after the reseal | the same three |
| `build/bpr1179-on-release`, both ON, `bpr` built | both | 601 of 605, then 2 of 2 after the reseal | `coding_format_check` (other cards' files; cleared upstream by `12b7faa1c`), the pin, the composition contract, BPR-1236 |

`application_profile_window_review_restoration_public_path` fails the same way
in every graph, and in the Debug `build/dev` tree at the pre-change revision.
It is [[BPR-1236]] (failed reading restoration, `unavailable-anchor`), not this
card. The first agent-free run also failed `qt_agent_launch_panel`: the panel
read two `canonical_hex(...).view()` temporaries after they were freed, which
only a Release build exposed. The fix keeps the values alive, and one fixture
lambda had the same pattern. Two other rows failed only under `-j6` load and
pass alone: `release_update_native_https` and
`application_queue_composition_contract`.

Image and package audits (the packages at `c3c7d6b3c`):

- **Tests-disabled development image.** The agent-free, automation-off image
  (`app-release` with both switches OFF) holds none of the eight anchors:
  `verify_composed_hosts.py --profile production --without-agent-integrations
  --without-local-automation` passes. The full audit of the same image fails,
  naming exactly the four agent hosts, the launcher, the protocol decoder, the
  MCP supervisor and the cycle owner service. A `-Wl,-why_load` relink traced what
  was still pulled in. Splitting `agent_launch.cpp` took
  `agent_protocol_decoder`, `build_agent_launch`, `frame_agent_input`,
  `finalize_agent_run` and `materialize_agent_environment` out of the image.
  Its `.app` holds the application, the supervisor and `bpr_update_helper`, and
  no MCP helper.
- **Agent-free macOS package.** `tools/release.sh --version 0.1.3
  --prepare-only --skip-tests --variant agent-free` (universal, ad-hoc sealed,
  relocated launch smoke) produced
  `BetterPullReview-0.1.3-macos-universal-agent-free-local.zip`, with its SPDX
  and the corresponding-source ZIP.
  - `package-inputs.json` names `{"agent_integrations":false,"local_automation":true}`
    and the supervisor as the only helper.
  - The SPDX `sourceInfo` is `git:c3c7d6b3c…; clean=True; tests-disabled
    Release; Darwin; agent integrations absent`, and its 41 files include no
    MCP helper.
  - `verify_release_package.py` prints `RELEASE_PACKAGE_OK` with those
    families: executable `72e34fcd…747d72`, SPDX `8fc87753…cc439`, and twelve
    audited binaries.
  - Negative controls: a copy of the package with the full build's MCP helper
    added fails `omitted-family-file-present`, and the full composition audit
    of its executable fails on the seven agent anchors.
  - Started relocated with a fresh HOME and agent-named canaries first on PATH,
    it reached `AUTOMATED_PROFILE_PRIVATE_OK` and `WORKBENCH_HOST_COMPOSED_OK`
    with `NATIVE_PLATFORM_VAULT_SELECTED:false`. It started no child process,
    and no canary ran.
- **Agent-free CLI.** The `tour-cli` preset now builds without agent
  integrations: no agent adapter or MCP helper target exists in that graph.
  The installed `TourCLI` component is 12 files: `bin/bpr`, the supervisor,
  `qt.conf`, QtCore, QtNetwork and the TLS plugin.
  - Its `bpr` holds no agent anchor and keeps the 15 cycle-command symbols.

- **Agent-free, automation-off macOS package.** The same command with
  `--variant agent-free-automation-off` produced
  `…-macos-universal-agent-free-automation-off-local.zip`.
  - Its `sourceInfo` ends `; agent integrations absent; local automation
    absent`.
  - The audit prints `RELEASE_PACKAGE_OK` with both families false: 41 files,
    executable `af410581…10424a`, SPDX `8aa78693…472c15`.
  - The composition audit with both `--without` flags passes (28 required, 11

- **Full macOS package, for comparison.** The default preset produced

  - It has 42 files, the MCP helper included, and its `sourceInfo` is
    unchanged in form (`…; tests-disabled Release; Darwin`).
  - The audit prints `RELEASE_PACKAGE_OK` with both families true and every

  - So the record changes only for a package that omits a family.

Courses (composition level, the same composition library the images link):

- **RC60-03, automation off.** `application_review_cycle_automation_off_two_round`

  - Each family audit, run on an image of the wrong family, fails naming the
    anchors.
  - A planted MCP helper fails the package audit.
  - The unit tests refuse a full record presented as an agent-free one, an
    unknown family name and a non-boolean family.
  - The first agent-free run of the tour composition row failed with `Problem
    Tab reaches the visible assistance control`. The real window had hidden
    the control, so the withdrawal is observed, not assumed. That row now
    expects the control to be hidden in an agent-free graph.

Criteria (this card's share; [[BPR-1182]] runs the native installed courses
and [[BPR-1193]] judges the rows whole):

- RC60-01 passes on macOS. The agent-free and automation-off packages and the
  agent-free CLI omit the agent adapter, the protocol decoder, the hosts and
  the MCP helper, by file list, symbol audit and record. They start with no
  child process, no agent canary run and no vault selection. The supervisor
  stays by the recorded decision.
- RC60-02 passes on macOS at composition level: the complete two-round course
  with `bpr`, publication preparation, and a full desktop's cycle reopened by
  an agent-free build. The installed-package GUI course is BPR-1182's.
- RC60-03 passes on macOS. Local automation is its own switch and package
  variant, independent of agents. With it off, the window's whole manual loop
  runs with no command endpoint bound and no cycle lost. `bpr` is removed by
  its install manifest, and the desktop does not depend on it.

Limits: Windows and Linux built nothing here. Their agent-free builds,
packages (MSI, DEB/RPM) and variant-over-variant replacement are owed to the
stations, as are MSVC and GCC compiles of the `OFF` code. The packages are
unsigned local candidates, not release artifacts. The installed packages'
`--self-test` does not bind the owner endpoints, so the endpoint's absence is
observed at composition level, and in the package by the forbidden cycle owner
service anchor. clang-tidy over the seven changed translation units found 25
issues, none on a changed line.

Station requests (each from a clean checkout at the pushed revision):

- `bpr-main-linux`: `cmake --preset dev -B build/bpr1179-off
  -DBPR_BUILD_AGENT_INTEGRATIONS=OFF -DBPR_BUILD_LOCAL_AUTOMATION=OFF`, build,
  then `ctest --test-dir build/bpr1179-off -R
  "^(application_review_cycle_automation_off_two_round|application_review_cycle_publish_public_path|application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path|composed_application_hosts|architecture_boundaries|review_cycle_dependency_closure)$"`.
  Then a `dev` build and `ctest --preset dev` for the full graph's regression.
  On the Ubuntu 24.04 baseline only, also `cmake --preset
  linux-package-agent-free`, `python3 packaging/package.py --build
  build/linux-package-agent-free --generator DEB --output
  build/releases/bpr1179-linux-agent-free`, and `scripts/verify_release_package.py`
  on the expanded runtime.
- `bpr-main-win`: the same two `ctest` passes in the native x64 tools
  environment (`windows-dev`, and `windows-dev` with both switches OFF). Then
  `cmake --preset windows-app-release-agent-free` and `python
  packaging/package.py --build build/windows-app-release-agent-free
  --generator WIX --output build/releases/bpr1179-windows-agent-free`. Install
  that MSI over a full one and back, checking that the profile's cycles
  reopen.

### BPR-1180: Send to Agent, a thin client of the frozen round (2026-10-04)

Mandate of 2026-10-03 ("find and implement available unattended milestones,
keep going until you run out of candidates"), with the amendment allowing
station work and promotion of an eligible Backlog card. BPR-1180 was promoted
at `6acb5b25a` (BPR-1179, BPR-1169 and BPR-1164 Done) and claimed at
`b1d95b24c`. No selection file was supplied, so the milestone helper's binding
was not run; the card holds the checkpoint.

The verified candidate is `2dad1dbbc`, over `3d414df18` (it was rebased once
over BPR-1278's change to `review_cycle_workbench.cpp`, with no conflict; the
focused selection, the agent-free selection and the cross-build reopen were
run again after it). `afe26732a` adds only the launch test
(`application_review_cycle_round_launch`). Both landed as `e72b7f972` and
`70606b8c3` after a rebase over two card-only commits (`b874334c2`,
`2330413ee`); no path this card owns differs.

Owned paths:

- Core: `src/core/include/bpr/core/review_cycle_send.hpp` and
  `src/core/src/review_cycle_send.cpp` (new: the decision, sessions, the host
  policy a cycle answers to, the observations a send records and reading a
  response out of a reply); `admit_delivery` in `review_cycle_share.cpp`
  (an adapter's acknowledgment may name its session); the `agent_adapter`
  response channel in `review_cycle_response.{hpp,cpp}`.
- Host: `src/application/include/bpr/application/review_cycle_send.hpp` and
  `src/application/src/review_cycle_send.cpp` (new: the sender port, the
  environment and `record_send`, in every graph);
  `review_cycle_agent_sender.{hpp,cpp}` (new: `agent_round_sender`, compiled
  only with the agent integrations); `build_round_launch` in
  `agent_launch.{hpp,cpp}`, inside the existing agent-only block; the
  lasting `record_send` call in `review_cycle_runtime.{hpp,cpp}`; Send's
  document budget in `review_cycle_share.cpp`; the send in
  `review_cycle_workbench.{hpp,cpp}`; `review_cycle_send_view` in
  `review_cycle_projection.hpp`; `src/application/CMakeLists.txt`.
- Storage: one decode bound in `src/adapters/sqlite/src/review_cycle_store.cpp`.
- Presentation: the Send box and reply box in `review_cycle_share_sheet.{hpp,cpp}`;
  the response channel's words in `review_cycle_view.cpp`.
- Composition: `src/app/src/review_cycle_desktop.{hpp,cpp}`, under
  `BPR_AGENT_INTEGRATIONS`. `github_application.cpp` is unchanged, so the
  round-trip pins are unchanged and no source joined the `src/app` inventory.
- Audit: the round sender is an agent-family anchor in
  `scripts/verify_composed_hosts.py`.
- Tests: `tests/core/review_cycle_send_tests.cpp`,
  `tests/workspace_contract/review_cycle_send_tests.cpp`,
  `tests/end_to_end/review_cycle_send_public_path.cpp`, the stand-in harness
  `tests/fixtures/agent/cycle_send_agent_main.cpp`, their registrations, and
  the desktop course's Send route expectation.
- Documentation: `docs/architecture/domain-and-state-model.md`,
  `docs/architecture/system-architecture.md`, `docs/development/cycle-cli.md`
  and `docs/development/release-packages.md`.

Decisions and compatibility:

- **Thin.** Send reuses the existing agent profiles, the agent host policy
  (`core::decide_agent_invocation`), the supervised process launcher with its
  byte, frame and time bounds and its protocol decoders, the round's channel
  decision, the freeze and the response model. The new code decides which
  profile and session, hands the round over and records what was seen. It
  has no coordinator, queue, retry or protocol of its own.
- **What is sent.** The round's readable document, the bytes Copy and Save
  give, within `core::maximum_round_send_bytes` (4 MiB, half the agent input
  bound so a protocol frame that escapes it still fits). Claude stream JSON
  and Codex App Server carry it as the user message; Codex JSONL and the
  external terminal read it as the prompt. Nothing else is added.
- **Where it runs.** In the folder the cycle is bound to (a file's own
  folder), said in the box before sending. The harness runs with its own
  settings and sign-in; the launch passes only each profile's allowed
  environment names and no credential.
- **Which host policy.** A local cycle has no Git host, so
  `core::cycle_send_policy` uses the corporate (explicit GHES) built-in
  policy when the source is known enterprise material or the account this
  profile acts as is on a host other than github.com, and GitHub.com's
  otherwise. A local folder of unknown origin is gated first by the
  `agent_adapter` channel, which is refused until configured.
- **Sessions.** New is the default and is said as new. A session can be
  continued only by a harness that can (Claude stream JSON, `--resume`) and
  only by a name that harness reported to an earlier send of this cycle,
  recorded in the acknowledgment's `Destination` as `protocol/session`. A
  harness that reports a different session than the one asked for is said
  to have done so.
- **States.** `failed` when nothing was started; else `accepted_by_adapter`
  with the bytes, `recipient_acknowledged` when the harness reported its
  session, and `uncertain` when its turn did not finish (stopped, timed out
  or stopped by the reviewer). A finished turn is said in the status and
  adds no observation: the delivery-state vocabulary is the domain's, and
  "completed" would claim reading. None of them gates capture.
- **Responses.** A finished reply that is a structured response (the whole
  reply, or its one fenced `json` block) is checked against the round that
  was sent, as every response is, and appended with the new `agent_adapter`
  response channel; one about another round or with other content is refused
  and nothing is appended. Prose is shown, kept only if the reviewer adds it
  as a reply.
- **Late.** The send's observations and response are recorded against the
  round it sent through `review_cycle_runtime::record_send`, a call no change
  of selection cancels; only shutdown ends it.
- **Storage compatibility.** No schema change. The response channel gained a
  value (3) and an adapter acknowledgment may carry a `Destination`; a build
  older than this one reads a cycle that used Send as corrupt rather than
  misreading it. An agent-free build of this revision reads all of it.
- **Visible route.** In a full build the share sheet now lists Send to
  Agent…, refused for a local folder until `bpr cycle policy
  --classification unclassified --channel agent_adapter --permission
  allowed`, as BPR-1161 lists every refused route. An agent-free build does
  not list it.

Environment: macOS 26.6.2 arm64, Apple clang 21, Qt 6.11.2, Debug `dev`
preset graphs. No model was invoked and no network or GitHub was contacted:
every harness was the stand-in copied into the course's own root.

Evidence:

| Graph | Selection | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `build/dev`, both families ON (Debug) | whole registered suite but the 720 s large-project row, before the rebase | 592 of 595 |
| the same, after the rebase | `core_review_cycle_send`, `_share`, `_response`, `review_cycle_send`, `review_cycle_workbench`, `application_review_cycle_round_launch` (added then), `application_review_cycle_send_public_path`, `application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path`, `sqlite_review_cycle_storage`, `qt_review_cycle_view_1_0`, `composed_application_hosts`, `composed_host_verifier_contract`, `architecture_boundaries`, the round-trip composition contract, `coding_format_check` | 15 of 15 |
| `build/bpr1180-off`, agents OFF, automation ON (Debug) | `core_review_cycle_send`, `review_cycle_send`, `application_review_cycle_send_public_path`, `application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path`, `composed_application_hosts` | 5 of 5, before and after the rebase |

The whole-suite failures: `composed_host_verifier_contract`, whose fixture
table lacked the new anchor (added; it passes); `live_mutation_isolation`,
which timed out at 300 s under `-j6` and passes alone in 281 s; and
`application_profile_window_review_restoration_public_path`, [[BPR-1236]],
as in every graph. The two-round and CLI courses need a `BPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI`
graph, which was not built here; the CLI's own export call was compared
instead (below).

- **Real processes (`application_review_cycle_send_public_path`, full graph).**
  The real window, the composed desktop and the real `agent_process_launcher`
  under its supervisor start copies of the stand-in harness in the course's
  own root.
  - Send to Agent… is listed refused ("Not allowed until it is configured")
    until the stored policy allows `agent_adapter`; then the box lists four
    agents and only a new session, and says "Starts a new Approved Claude
    stream JSON session in <folder> … exactly as Copy gives it … If the send
    fails, nothing else is tried" before Send Round 1.
  - One harness process with `-p --input-format stream-json --output-format
    stream-json --verbose`; the text of its one user message equals the
    preview, the platform clipboard after Copy and the file Save wrote.
  - The sheet lists "F1 · Accepted by the agent adapter" and "F1 · The
    recipient acknowledged receiving it"; the harness's structured response
    is appended with the `agent_adapter` channel; nothing is captured.
  - F2 continues `fixture-session-1`: the second process's arguments end
    `--resume fixture-session-1`. Its prose reply is shown, not appended, and
    Add as Reply appends it to F2 as a manual entry.
  - With an account on `ghe.example.com`, Codex JSONL is listed "not allowed
    here" and not choosable; asking the host to send to it anyway says
    "does not allow Codex JSONL (profile_forbidden_on_host)", freezes
    nothing and starts no process anywhere.
  - The external terminal harness reads the document as its plain stdin,
    byte for byte, with no arguments, and its response is F3's.
  - `SEND_STOP_CANARY`: the harness acknowledges and exits 9. F4 reads
    accepted, acknowledged, "Delivery not confirmed"; half a second later
    there is still one process for it and no other observation.
  - A missing harness executable: "F5 · Delivery failed", "could not be
    started".
  - With no agent installed (`set_send_profiles({ })`) the route and box are
    gone, every observation is still listed and a hand-entered reply
    continues F4.
- **Host (`review_cycle_send`, both graphs).** The workbench, the real store
  and a scripted adapter on a lane of its own:
  - no adapter, no route; refused until configured with nothing frozen or
    started; a denied channel refused for every agent; a corporate account
    refusing Codex with nothing frozen, sent, copied or recorded in its place;
  - the adapter receives the previewed document, the chosen profile, a new
    session and the cycle's folder; Copy and the terminal's export call
    (`export_round_selected` with `terminal_output`, what `bpr cycle export`
    prints) give the same bytes; one send at a time;
  - a session never reported, or a harness that cannot continue, is refused
    with nothing frozen;
  - F1's reply arriving after F2 was frozen and shown is appended to F1; a
    reply to F2's send naming F1 is refused ("The response is for another
    round") and nothing is appended;
  - stopped, timed out, cancelled by Stop and not started record exactly
    accepted/acknowledged/uncertain or failed, with no second send and no
    copy, save or other agent in its place;
  - a send whose cycle was closed while it ran is still recorded against its
    round, and the host is quiescent after;
  - a host composed with no adapter over the same profile lists every
    observation and response, offers no route, and Copy and a hand-entered
    reply continue the cycle.
- **Launch (`application_review_cycle_round_launch`, full graph).** The
  launch for each built-in protocol, without a process: Claude's arguments
  and its one user message carrying the document escaped; `--resume s-1`
  only when asked; Codex JSONL and the external terminal reading the
  document as their prompt; Codex App Server's initialize with the document
  held for the turn; only allowed environment names; a continuation refused
  for every other harness and for a name that reads as an option; over the
  input bound, nothing. The adapter's reading of each run outcome (finished,
  not started, stopped, timed out, cancelled), the session from a Claude
  session or a Codex thread, the reply from assistant items or plain lines,
  and no launch for an assignment that cannot be built.
- **Core (`core_review_cycle_send`).** Session names (never read as an
  option), records and parsing; the policy a cycle answers to; offers;
  every refusal in order; the observations for each way a send ends, each
  admitted by `admit_delivery`; reading a response out of a reply.
- **Removal across builds.** The full graph's course run with
  `--profile-root DIR` left its profile; the agent-free graph's course run
  with `--reopen DIR` lists no route over that cycle, reads back
  "F1 · Accepted by the agent adapter" and the acknowledgment, reads the
  responses (the adapter's first), copies the round and appends a reply by
  hand.
- **Images.** `nm -C` finds 95 `agent_round_sender`/`build_round_launch`
  symbols in the full application image and none in the agent-free one.
  `verify_composed_hosts.py` passes each image with its own families.
- **Negative controls.**
  - One byte added to the document Send renders fails both courses: "the
    previewed document …" (host) and "the harness got the round's readable
    document" (real processes).
  - `cycle_send_policy` ignoring the corporate host fails all three
    (`core`: "an account on a corporate host"; host and real processes:
    Codex not refused).
  - `record_send` checking a reply against the next round fails the host
    course ("the reply's response, checked and appended …").
  - The full audit of the agent-free image fails on eight anchors, the round
    sender among them; the agent-free audit of the full image fails naming
    "Send to Agent round sender constructor" as present.
  - `--reopen` on an empty profile fails "the profile holds the sent cycle".
- **Static checks.** clang-format 22 and 23 agree on every changed file;
  clang-tidy over the three new and ten changed translation units reports
  eight findings, all in `agent_launch.cpp` and none on a changed line;
  `DOCUMENTATION_OK`.

Station requests (each from a clean checkout at the pushed revision; no
remote mutation):

- `bpr-main-linux`: `cmake --preset dev`, build, then `ctest --test-dir
  build/dev -R
  "^(core_review_cycle_send|core_review_cycle_share|core_review_cycle_response|review_cycle_send|review_cycle_workbench|application_review_cycle_round_launch|application_review_cycle_send_public_path|application_review_cycle_desktop_public_path|sqlite_review_cycle_storage|composed_application_hosts|composed_host_verifier_contract|architecture_boundaries|application_review_round_trip_composition_contract)$"`.

Criteria (this card's share; [[BPR-1182]] runs native installed courses and
[[BPR-1193]] judges the rows whole):

- **RC60-07 passes on macOS.** Send hands one profile exactly the round's
  readable document, the bytes Copy, Save and the terminal's export give

- **RC60-08 passes on macOS.** The profile is the existing one under its
  existing agent host policy: a corporate account's host refuses Codex by
  name with nothing frozen or started, and the round's channel decision

  said as new, and an existing one is continued only by a harness that can

- **RC60-09 passes on macOS.** Late output is recorded against the round it

  and cancelled sends stay uncertain and a missing harness fails, each with
  no second send and no other agent or route; nothing captures or verifies
  by itself; with the adapter removed, at run time or by an agent-free
  build, the cycle reads every send and continues by hand.

Limits: Windows and Linux built nothing here; their runs are the station
requests above. The share sheet's Send box has no owner verdict: its layout
and words were not put to the owner, and native usability is [[BPR-1182]]'s.
No real harness or model ran; the stand-in speaks the Claude stream JSON
shapes the decoder already admits, and Codex App Server and JSONL sends are
covered by their launch framing and the adapter's reading of a run, not by a
process. The
two-round and CLI courses were not rebuilt in a `tour-cli` graph.

### BPR-1181: security boundaries attacked through real processes (2026-10-04)

Mandate of 2026-10-03 ("find and implement available unattended milestones,
keep going until you run out of candidates"), with the amendment allowing
station work and promotion of an eligible Backlog card. BPR-1181 was promoted
at `6d9e71619` (BPR-1179, BPR-1180 and BPR-1162 Done) and claimed at
`37fd91558`. No selection file was supplied, so the milestone helper's binding
was not run; the card holds the checkpoint.

Landed: `278956f0a` (the two fixes), `3a21cafb6` (the course) and `20aaaf206`
(the threat model and CLI guide), over `32d052a5a`, which changed no owned
path.

Owned paths:

- Core: `cycle_round_problem::unportable_path` and its check in the round
  assembler (`review_cycle_round.{hpp,cpp}`), with
  `tests/core/review_cycle_round_tests.cpp`.
- Words: the refusal in `cycle_commands.cpp`, `review_cycle_workbench.cpp`
  and `review_cycle_share_sheet.cpp`; the response file's refusal in
  `review_cycle_words.cpp` and its exit code in `cycle_commands.cpp`.
- Course: `tests/end_to_end/review_cycle_security_qualification.py` and its
  registration in `tests/end_to_end/CMakeLists.txt`.
- Documents: the Review Cycles section of the
  [threat model](../architecture/security-threat-model.md#qualified-boundaries-and-residual-risks-bpr-1181)
  and [the CLI guide](../development/cycle-cli.md).

Defects found by the attack corpus and fixed at their source:

- **A hostile file name froze an unreadable round.** A checkout may hold a
  file whose name has a control character (the course uses
  `src/esc\x1b[31mred.cpp`). A note on it was admitted, and the round
  assembler encoded its anchor path. The payload reader then refused that
  path, so the frozen round could never be exported, copied, granted or read
  again ("Retained material is damaged"). The assembler now applies the
  reader's own path rule to every anchor and suggestion. It refuses with
  `unportable_path`, naming the note, before anything is frozen. The
  terminal, the workbench and the share sheet say so. `core_review_cycle_round`
  shows ESC, tab and DEL names refused, and a name with a colon, a space and
  a non-ASCII letter carried and read back.
- **A response file's bound and link were reported as unreadable.** A file
  over 16 MiB or a link was "could not be read" (`source_unavailable`). It
  is now `over_limit` naming the bound, or said to be a link that is never
  followed. The reader already refused both; only the words were wrong.

Decisions and findings that are not defects:

- **Known origin.** No surface observes a classification for a local folder
  or checkout, so every cycle decides as `unclassified`. Nothing washes away
  a known enterprise restriction, because none can be known for a local
  cycle today. Publication and the external link admit only github.com, no
  provider cache is copied into a cycle, and Send to Agent already answers to
  the one known enterprise fact (BPR-1180). The CLI guide now says so.
- **The window lists note text as plain text,** control characters included,
  in a list item that interprets nothing. Only a terminal interprets
  escapes, and `bpr` neutralizes them there.
- **Bundle import has no shipped entry point.**
  `review_cycle_runtime::import_bundle` has no caller outside the host
  contracts, so the hostile-archive corpus stays the core's, the host's and
  the fuzz target's (BPR-1160, BPR-1162).
- **A same-user process can drive the owner.** The course captures a genuine
  request from `bpr` and replays it. After a wrong or foreign greeting it is
  dropped; after the greeting the owner gave, it is served and applied once.
  The greeting binds a connection to this root's owner, and the peer check
  admits this user. This is the documented same-user limit, not a defect.
- **The profile's session log** (`data/logs/session-*.jsonl`) is written
  during the desktop's run. Its two records hold only the fixed diagnostic
  fields, and none of the course's content or credential canaries.

Environment: macOS 26.6.2 arm64, Apple clang 21.0.0, Qt 6.11.2, Apple Git
2.50.1, the Debug `dev` preset with `-DBPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI=ON`
(`build/bpr1181`), so both families and local automation are built. No
GitHub, network or model was contacted. Agent-named canaries were first on
PATH and none ran.

Commands and results:

- `ctest --test-dir build/bpr1181 -R '^application_review_cycle_security_qualification$'`
  passes in 31 s under the macOS sandbox runner. The course prints
  `SECURITY_HOSTILE_CHECKOUT_OK`, `SECURITY_FORGED_RESPONSES_OK`,
  `SECURITY_ONE_POLICY_OK`, `SECURITY_OWNER_CHANNEL_OK
  silent_peer_dropped_after=5.0s`, `SECURITY_WRONG_OWNER_OK`,
  `SECURITY_GIT_CANARIES_LIVE 12` and `REVIEW_CYCLE_SECURITY_QUALIFICATION_OK`.
- The focused selection after the last change passes 20 of 20:
  `core_review_cycle_round`, `review_cycle_{workbench,response,export,round,send}`,
  both `qt_review_cycle_view` rows, `cycle_cli_public_path`,
  `skill_bpr_cycle_package`, `application_profile_cycle_handoff_public_path`,
  `application_review_cycle_{two_round,parity,desktop,send}_public_path`, the
  new course, `architecture_boundaries`,
  `application_review_round_trip_composition_contract` (pins unchanged),
  `test_network_isolation_manifest` and `coding_format_check`. An earlier run
  of 28 also passed `core_review_cycle_{policy,command,retrieval,response,share,send}`,
  `sqlite_review_cycle_storage` and `sqlite_tour_owner_channel`. Its two
  failures were the format check, since fixed, and the manifest, which needed
  `gh_cli_git_smart_transport`'s executable built, because this tree builds
  only the selection.
- clang-format 22.1.8 and 23.1.1 agree on every changed C++ file. clang-tidy
  23.1.1 over the six changed translation units reports no finding on a
  changed line. `DOCUMENTATION_OK`; `verify_documentation_tests.py` OK.

Negative controls, each run and then reverted:

- The assembler's new refusal removed: `core_review_cycle_round` fails "a
  name the reader refuses", and the course fails "the escaped name is
  refused before freezing".
- The owner's greeting check removed (`tour_native_server::receive` accepts
  any 64 bytes): the course fails "a genuine request after a wrong greeting:
  the owner answered".
- `neutral_terminal_text` returning its input: the course fails "the
  terminal shows no control byte".
- The detectors are shown live in every run. Git itself fires the checkout's
  canaries 12 times after the course, and the FIFO watch sees the course's
  own reader.

Criteria (this card's share; [[BPR-1182]] runs the installed native courses
and [[BPR-1193]] judges the rows whole):

- **RC60-04 passes on macOS.** One stored policy decides for the terminal, a
  file, a bundle, a harness's granted read and the window's Copy, Save and
  Save as Bundle routes. A denial holds in each, and a later denial closes an
  existing grant, both with the desktop owning the profile and without it.
  The agent adapter channel is BPR-1180's, decided first. A role variable
  that is neither `consumer` nor `reviewer` is refused. Provider variables
  and a copy of `bpr` named `claude` get the same refusals. Another root
  reads nothing, and an unknown profile is `profile_unknown`. No local source
  is classified, and the guide says so rather than implying an enterprise
  rule applies.
- **RC60-05 passes on macOS.** A hostile checkout runs nothing and is
  unchanged byte for byte, `.git` included, through open, note, compose,
  freeze and capture. A link is carried as its own text. A path outside the
  capture names nothing, and a name a round cannot carry is refused before
  freezing. Note and response text is stored and shown as typed and opens
  nothing. A forged response (verified, approved, grant, trust, duplicate
  keys, deep nesting, not UTF-8, over the bound, a link, another round or
  cycle) is refused with the store unchanged. A well-formed one claiming
  certification is a claim the window marks as one. It verifies no note,
  changes no policy, grant or round, and leaves F1 exporting byte for byte as
  frozen.
- **RC60-06 passes on macOS.** Every file and directory under the data root
  is the user's and owner-private, and a loosened root or store is refused.
  The owner's socket is the user's, in an owner-private directory. Wrong and
  foreign greetings, oversized, overlong, short, zero-length and garbage
  frames and a silent peer are each dropped, and the owner serves `bpr`
  after each. Impostor owners of another root or profile receive no byte. No
  credential canary reaches any file, the stores and log included, or any
  output. No content canary reaches anything but the store and the reviewer's
  exports, and no refusal repeats a response's text. `bpr` claims no
  encryption or sandbox, and the threat model states the same-user,
  encryption, clipboard, scrollback, harness and dump limits. Pins: F1
  exports unchanged after a capture and the imports; reclamation and removal
  are BPR-1145's unchanged evidence.
- **RC60-10 passes on macOS, this card's share.** Each result above names
  what produced it. The shipped `bpr` and the composed profile desktop ran as
  real processes, natively, in a tests-enabled Debug graph. That is neither
  the installed package (BPR-1182) nor another platform, and nothing was
  cross-compiled. Second-user refusal is read from the code, not observed.
  Removal and the omitted families are BPR-1179's and BPR-1180's unchanged
  audits: no agent-family path changed here.

Critical review: the card's implementer audited the code paths above at
`3a21cafb6` (owner channel framing and peer check in `profile_activation.cpp`,
the round codec's path rule, the response reader, the policy combination in
`review_cycle_share.cpp` and `review_cycle_retrieval.cpp`, terminal text) and
recorded the two defects and the findings above. No independent reviewer ran:
the coordinator-implementer recipe dispatches none.

Limits: Linux and Windows built nothing here. The course's Git canaries,
FIFO, raw socket attacks and mode checks are POSIX; on Windows it runs the
rest and prints `SECURITY_QUALIFICATION_SKIPPED` for each skipped part. No
second OS user ran. The installed packages were not exercised.

Station requests (each from a clean checkout at the pushed revision; no remote
mutation):

- `bpr-main-linux`: `cmake --preset dev -B build/bpr1181
  -DBPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI=ON`, `cmake --build build/bpr1181`, then `ctest
  --test-dir build/bpr1181 -j4 --output-on-failure -R
  "^(application_review_cycle_security_qualification|core_review_cycle_round|cycle_cli_public_path|review_cycle_workbench|review_cycle_response|qt_review_cycle_view_1_0|application_profile_cycle_handoff_public_path|test_network_isolation_manifest|architecture_boundaries)$"`.
  Every POSIX part runs there.
- `bpr-main-win`: the same in the native x64 tools environment: `cmake
  --preset windows-dev -B build/windows-bpr1181 -DBPR_BUILD_TOUR_CLI=ON`,
  `cmake --build build/windows-bpr1181 --config Debug`, and the same `ctest`
  with `-C Debug`. The course is registered contained. If it stops inside the
  container, pin it `WINDOWS_UNCONTAINED` with the measured line in
  `scripts/verify_test_network_isolation.py`, rerun it uncontained and
  record both. Expect `test_network_isolation_manifest` to name three earlier
  rows that carry `WINDOWS_UNCONTAINED` with no pin:
  `application_review_cycle_automation_off_two_round` (BPR-1179),
  `application_review_cycle_send_public_path` and `review_cycle_send`
  (BPR-1180). Measure and pin them the same way.

## Qualification milestone, concluded

### BPR-1185: documentation, examples and the installed packages (2026-10-04)

The same mandate ("find and implement available unattended milestones, keep
going until you run out of candidates"), with the amendment that other
stations' work may be taken over. BPR-1185 was promoted at `6d99d589e` and
claimed at `2f2eef6f5`. No selection file was supplied, so the milestone
helper's binding was not run; the card holds the checkpoint.

The candidate is `88bc1a82f` (written as `498cd9942`, rebased over
`d7ed57b8f`, which changed two card notes). The packages were built at
`2f2eef6f5` from a clean clone of it. Between the two the only product source
that changed is `src/app/src/tour_main.cpp`, which is linked into `bpr` alone
(`app_bpr`), never into the desktop packages, so the packages' evidence stands
for the candidate. `bpr` was rebuilt with the change and every `bpr` result
below is that build's.

Owned paths:

- Documentation: `README.md` (a Review Cycles section);
  `docs/architecture/product-contract.md` (the scope as built, the definition,
  an authority row); `system-architecture.md` (status, a state row, a feature
  row and a Review Cycles section); `domain-and-state-model.md` (the heading no
  longer says in progress); `git-and-epoch-model.md` (Review Cycles beside
  epochs); `review-ledger.md`; `review-round-trip.md`; `ai-agent-boundary.md`
  (Review Cycles and agents); `security-threat-model.md` (the section is no
  longer a registration, a stale sentence about publication, Send to Agent and
  the removable families); `docs/development/build-and-test.md` (the three
  switches); `cycle-cli.md` (common options, `bpr` alone, an honest "Not in this
  build"); `release-packages.md` (the installed course);
  `storage-and-diagnostics.md` (the cycle store and its limits);
  `docs/security/corporate-security-overview.md` (a network row and a Review
  Cycles section); and the new reviewer's guide
  `docs/ux/review-cycles-workflow.md`.
- `src/app/src/tour_main.cpp`: `bpr` alone, `bpr help` and `bpr --help` name
  both command families. Before, `bpr` alone printed only the tour family's
  help, so `bpr cycle` could not be found from the terminal.
- `tests/scripts/cycle_cli_documentation_tests.py` and its row
  `cycle_cli_documentation` in `tests/CMakeLists.txt`, registered with
  `WINDOWS_UNCONTAINED` and its pending reason in
  `scripts/verify_test_network_isolation.py`.
- This ledger: the BPR-1185 section, and five links repaired to the renamed
  domain and threat-model headings.

Every user-facing word in the guide was read from the Qt source at the
candidate (`review_cycle_view.cpp`, `review_cycle_share_sheet.cpp`,
`review_cycle_publish_sheet.cpp`, `review_cycle_promote_sheet.cpp`,
`workbench_window.cpp`), and every statement of a limit from the code that
enforces it (`maximum_captured_file_bytes`, `default_cycle_retained_budget_bytes`,
`core::cycle_command_options`, `admit_profile`).

`cycle_cli_documentation` reads `cycle-cli.md` against the built `bpr`, so the
page can no longer drift from what ships:

- The command table must name exactly the commands `capabilities` lists, in
  its order.
- Each row's options must equal the core registry's
  (`core::cycle_command_options`, parsed from `review_cycle_command.cpp`).
- Each documented option must be accepted by its command. Every other option
  the page or the registry names must be refused by it (`unsupported_option`):
  648 probes, each with a profile that does not exist, so nothing runs past
  parsing.
- The exit table must use the binary's exit numbers, and the thirteen codes
  the course provokes must exit as the table says. They are
  `unsupported_option`, `unsupported_command`, `unsupported_version`,
  `invalid_input`, `not_found`, `profile_unknown`, `interaction_required`,
  `stale_revision`, `conflict`, `policy_denied`, `requires_partition`,
  `source_unavailable` and `storage_unavailable`.
- The limits, schema name, owner protocol, consumer commands, channels and
  kinds of source, and the default refusals the prose states must be the
  binary's.
- The four examples run as written, in the order the prose requires. The grant
  is refused until the policy example allows its channel, and the read returns
  every field the page names.
- `bpr` and `bpr cycle` alone print what the page says.

Six altered copies of the page must each fail: a command left out, an option the
binary refuses, an option the page drops, a code under the wrong exit, a wrong
limit, and a channel left out. Against the `bpr` built before the
`tour_main.cpp` change the row failed at "bpr alone prints 'bpr tour — local
review-tour terminal…', not its two families". That failure is the claim
observed false, and it is why the change was made.

A finding, not fixed here: profile purge does not list `publication-git-v1/`,
the BPR-owned Git store that Publish as Pull Request makes in the profile root
on first use (`review_cycle_desktop.cpp`, `set_publishing`).
`release_maintenance_files.cpp` classifies every top-level entry of a profile it
purges and refuses an unclassified one as `incomplete_inventory`, and its
allow-list has no such entry. A profile that has planned a publication therefore
cannot be purged. This is read from the code, not observed in a run. The storage
guide says so and tells the reader not to delete the folder by hand. A follow-up
card is needed. It was not allocated here: allocation is the main integrator's,
and this worktree is not on `main`. (Later filed as [[BPR-1283]], reproduced
and fixed there.)

Environment: macOS 26.6.2 arm64, Apple clang 21, Qt 6.11.2, Python 3.14.7.
Someone was using another application throughout. No GitHub, network or model
was contacted.

Checks:

- `bpr` from the `tour-cli` preset (Release, tests disabled, agent-free),
  installed with the guide's own commands (`cmake --preset tour-cli`, `cmake
  --build --preset tour-cli`, `cmake --install build/tour-cli --component
  TourCLI --prefix /absolute/root`, then `bin/bpr cycle capabilities`); `bpr`
  `ef8e0d27…`:

  - `cycle_cli_tests.py`, `bpr_cycle_skill_tests.py` (package
    `8b5946f5…`, 11 taught lines, 14 broken copies refused) and
    `tour_cli_tests.py` pass against the same binary.
  - The guide's other command,
    `bpr cycle policy --classification unclassified --channel agent_adapter --permission allowed`,
    and its `--permission unconfigured` counterpart answer `ok`.
- The `tour-cli` preset with `BUILD_TESTING=ON`, only `app_bpr` built: `ctest

- `clang-format` is clean on `tour_main.cpp`. `python3

The installed packages: tests-disabled universal candidates from the guide's
`tools/release.sh --version 0.1.3 --prepare-only --skip-tests [--variant …]`

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| full | `RELEASE_PACKAGE_OK` | agents, automation | 42 | `d8cff708…` | `f5e45407…` |

Each SPDX `sourceInfo` is `git:2f2eef6f5…; clean=True; tests-disabled Release;
Darwin`, with `; agent integrations absent` and `; local automation absent` as
the families go. Every package carries the same corresponding-source asset
(`fdf51558…`). The agent-free images have no MCP helper.

`installed_cycle_package_course.py`, run as the release guide now writes it,
prints `INSTALLED_CYCLE_PACKAGE_COURSE_OK`. It installs the full, agent-free,
agent-free automation-off and full packages in turn over one profile, and starts
each on Cocoa. Each launch:

- composed the profile and started no child process, sampled every 50 ms for
  five idle seconds;
- held no network socket, and ran none of the nine agent canaries placed first
  on PATH;
- bound `activate`, and bound `tourcmd` in the full and agent-free packages and
  not in the automation-off one.

The cycle made before the first swap (R1, a note on a CRLF line, F1 frozen) read
back byte for byte after each package, F1's export `1b20aa65…` each time.
Negative controls:

- The full package placed as the agent-free one fails `agent-free: MCP helper
  present`.
- The agent-free package placed as the automation-off one fails
  `3:agent-free-automation-off: owner command endpoint bound with local
  automation absent`.

hostile and recovery, packaging and human-steering evidence are recorded across
BPR-1182 to BPR-1185, with the gates above left open by name. Every owned row
has direct evidence or a named gate, and nothing is claimed beyond what ran.
BPR-1185 is Done. The `qualification` milestone is complete, and the next
eligible card is the first closeout, [[BPR-1186]] (RFC-BPR-0053), which waits
only on this card. BPR-1187 to BPR-1194 follow it one at a time.

## RFC closeout

### BPR-1186: RFC-BPR-0053 closed (2026-10-04)

The same mandate ("find and implement available unattended milestones, keep
going until you run out of candidates"), with the amendment that other
stations' work may be taken over and an eligible, dependency-complete Backlog
card promoted. BPR-1186 was promoted at `05bcb3762` and claimed at
`d5f9fdeb6`. Its only dependency, BPR-1185, is Done. RFC-BPR-0053's listed
dependencies, RFC-BPR-0001, RFC-BPR-0003 and RFC-BPR-0007, are Auto-Verified.
No selection file was supplied, so the milestone helper's binding was not run;
the card holds the checkpoint. This is a documentation and board change only:
no product source, test or build file changed.

Each criterion is judged whole below, against the sections above that hold
its evidence, at the revisions they recorded. Each row ran on macOS arm64 and
on both stations unless a limit says otherwise. No RC53 row asks for the
owner's judgment, and no owner verdict bears on one, so no attended session
was opened.

| Criterion | Evidence (cards; platforms) | Result |
|---|---|---|
| RC53-01 | A cycle with no pull request, remote, commit, account or agent and an empty complete R0 (BPR-1144), reopened through a new store object (BPR-1145) and through the runtime with only a profile root (BPR-1146). Through the product: `cycle_cli_public_path` (BPR-1167) and the two-round course (BPR-1166), corpus scenario S01 (BPR-1183). Core and store rows on all three; the CLI and handoff courses on all three (BPR-1168's Windows and Linux runs) | Pass |
| RC53-02 | Distinct identity types and per-cycle counters that only advance and refuse overflow (BPR-1144); all seven counters persist and reload, a removed cycle's identity is not reissued, and no provider identity column exists (BPR-1145). All three | Pass |
| RC53-03 | `review_subject` holds `pull_request_authority` whole; no pull-request or epoch type was edited; bounded observations (BPR-1144). The material bridge passes pull-request requests through unchanged and refuses cross-catalog, unrecorded and pull-request-without-port requests (BPR-1146). All three | Pass |
| RC53-04 | Real SIGKILL before and after `COMMIT` for object install, capture and freeze (BPR-1145), and for content install, manifest install and revision record (BPR-1152): whole record or none, never a partial round; a retry completes once or replays. Missing and corrupt objects reported, never substituted; full disk refused with the cycle unchanged (BPR-1152). A desktop owner killed mid-write (BPR-1183 part 2) | Pass. The kill and full-disk courses ran on macOS and Linux; on Windows they are compiled out, as in every SQLite contract, and the corruption, quota and explanation rows pass there |
| RC53-05 | Store: stale expected revisions refused with the current state, identical retries replayed, a reused operation ID refused, refused requests leave no receipt (BPR-1145). Across interfaces: through the owner a repeated `--operation-id` returns the first answer and adds one note, the same ID for another request is `conflict`, a stale draft write is `stale_revision` (BPR-1168, BPR-1171); after the owner is killed mid-write the repeat applies once (BPR-1183). Handoff and parity courses on all three | Pass. Limit: run in the terminal with no owner, a command repeated after it applied is refused rather than replayed (BPR-1183's recorded decision). It is never applied twice |
| RC53-06 | `review_cycle_runtime` opens, creates, captures and reopens with only a profile root and executors; its link closure has no agent, MCP, AI, GitHub, Git, Qt or composition target, and a closure with `adapter_agent` fails (BPR-1146). The agent-free, automation-off tree passes its cycle courses on all three (BPR-1179). Installed agent-free packages ran no agent canary placed first on PATH (BPR-1182, BPR-1185; macOS) | Pass |
| RC53-07 | On a profile holding a pull-request tour draft, creating a cycle leaves `review-tours.sqlite3` byte-identical and the draft exact (BPR-1146); a neighbouring store unchanged and an unexpected schema refused (BPR-1145). Read at this closeout: since the execution base `22d184166`, no Review Cycles commit changed a pull-request store (`review_ledger_*`, `private_review_*`, `review_tour_*`, `cached_review_overview_store`, `epoch_catalog`, `review_mutation_recovery_store`); the only non-cycle SQLite files they touched are the owner channel (`profile_activation.cpp`, `tour_owner_codec.cpp`, protocol 3, an older peer refused before a payload) and the purge inventory's new cycle entry. All three for the runtime row | Pass |
| RC53-08 | Quota: an over-budget install refused with the inventory, `reclaim` removing only unreferenced objects, pinned evidence readable (BPR-1145, BPR-1152). Publication: after a squashed publication, a reclaim and a restart, R1 to R3, every captured byte, the round, reply, decision, note and delivery remain until explicit removal (BPR-1177, `review_cycle_publish_desk`). Removal needs a preview whose token still matches, naming revisions, drafts, rounds, feedback and released bytes (BPR-1145). All three | Pass. Limit: no window or `bpr` command offers removing a cycle; removal is the store's operation, and the storage guide states the irrecoverable loss. Profile purge, the user's only removal, is refused today for a profile with `publication-git-v1/` ([[BPR-1283]]); it loses nothing |
| RC53-09 | Scope changes are new versions; partial, out-of-scope and unavailable paths classify unknown and never compare as deleted (BPR-1144); through capture, corpus S03 (BPR-1149, BPR-1172). All three | Pass |
| RC53-10 | The intake registration (BPR-1143), the policy section (BPR-1147), and the documents as built (BPR-1185): the local scope, the disclosure channels, one new GitHub write (a new branch and pull request on github.com after a confirmed plan) and unchanged GitHub authority | Pass |

Fresh at this closeout, macOS 26.6.2 arm64, `build/dev` (Debug, Homebrew
LLVM), source at `d5f9fdeb6`, which is `88bc1a82f`'s product tree: the six
targets were up to date, and `ctest --test-dir build/dev --output-on-failure
-R
'^(core_review_cycle|core_review_cycle_policy|sqlite_review_cycle_storage|sqlite_review_cycle_runtime|review_cycle_dependency_closure|review_cycle_recovery|sqlite_release_maintenance_files|architecture_boundaries)$'`
passed 8 of 8 under the network-isolated runner. The SIGKILL courses ran.

Owed elsewhere in the pack, and not RFC-BPR-0053's: the desktop two-round
course under `xcb` on Linux, the agent-free DEB on Ubuntu 24.04, the
agent-free WiX MSI and variant replacement on Windows, `cycle_cli_documentation`
on the stations, and GHES publication. They are RC59, RC60 and RC61 rows and
are judged by BPR-1192 to BPR-1194.

RFC-BPR-0053 is Auto-Verified. The next closeout is [[BPR-1187]]
(RFC-BPR-0054), which waits only on this card.

### BPR-1187: RFC-BPR-0054 reconciled, not closed (2026-10-04)

Same mandate and amendment. BPR-1187's dependency BPR-1186 is Done and
RFC-BPR-0054's listed dependencies (RFC-BPR-0053, 0003, 0004) are
Auto-Verified, so the reconciliation ran before promotion. It stops at one row.
The card was not promoted: it now depends on [[BPR-1284]] and stays in
Backlog. Documentation and board only.

| Criterion | Evidence (cards; platforms) | Result |
|---|---|---|
| RC54-01 | `workspace_source`: plain folder, single file, a Git checkout with no commit, a dirty checkout and a linked worktree, network-isolated, no account (BPR-1148). macOS, Linux, Windows | Pass |
| RC54-02 | `review_cycle_capture`: hooks, fsmonitor, filter and textconv drivers and a promisor remote all point at a canary that never runs; the tree, `.git` included, hashes identically before and after; working bytes captured, staged identity kept apart (BPR-1149). All three. BPR-1181's hostile checkout through the product, macOS and Linux | Pass |
| RC54-03 | Unsafe descendant paths refused before any open, on all three. Root replacement: detected with reads held to the opened root on macOS and Linux; on Windows the held root cannot be renamed, and a same-named checkout with the same bytes is `replaced` on all three (BPR-1148). Through `bpr` and the window: a replaced root, a link to another folder and a moved root refused (BPR-1183; macOS, Linux). Symbolic-link escapes (listing, stat and read through a directory link, a file link, a directory swapped for a link): macOS and Linux, and Windows on `bpr-main-win`, which makes symbolic links (BPR-1244's run of `workspace_source`, 2026-10-01; the row skips itself only where links cannot be made) | **Open**: no run has made a junction. `workspace_source` creates only symbolic links. The Win32 adapter refuses descent through any reparse point by `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT` and lists a non-symlink one as `other`, so a junction should be refused by the same branch. That is a reading of the code, not an observed pass. Owed: [[BPR-1284]] on Windows |
| RC54-04 | One change between passes retried once; a writer that never stops exhausts three retries as `unstable`, recording nothing; only `observed_stable` is produced; cancellation records no revision (BPR-1149). A capture settled after retries is flagged with its count (BPR-1152). All three | Pass |
| RC54-05 | Narrowed and widened scopes (version 2, first-seen paths unknown), tracked deletion versus untracked absence, sparse entries visible and unread, exclusions with reasons, `missing_scope` (BPR-1149). All three | Pass |
| RC54-06 | The byte corpus read back exactly, raw hash equal to SHA-256 of the bytes, CRLF, BOM and no final newline kept, offsets at scalar boundaries; 100755/100644 on POSIX, no execute bit invented on Windows (BPR-1150). All three | Pass |
| RC54-07 | Binary, UTF-16 and invalid UTF-8 file-level only, LFS pointer and filter-managed bytes marked, a gitlink a boundary, a FIFO unsupported, a link kept as its target bytes (BPR-1150; on Windows since BPR-1244); case and normalization pairs written and flagged on a case-sensitive Linux volume (BPR-1148) | Pass |
| RC54-08 | Comparison pages of 256 paths, resumable and cancellable, reads on the effect lane (BPR-1151). 100,001-file Release reports on macOS, Linux and Windows, `failures` empty: 25 windows paged, Cancel capture within 11 ms recording nothing, the GUI's longest turn during a capture at most 1.23 ms (BPR-1184) | Pass |
| RC54-09 | Full source reads the captured bytes after an edit, deletion and restart; corrupt and missing pins report no bytes and never the live file (BPR-1151); a verified recapture repairs a corrupt pin (BPR-1152). All three | Pass |
| RC54-10 | Two same-named checkouts with identical bytes have different identities and the other is `replaced`; the core refuses an unconfirmed rebind and requires a material check; imported projects resolve only through an explicit mapping (BPR-1148, BPR-1149, BPR-1162). The product refuses another folder at the location, which opens only as a cycle of its own (BPR-1183) | Pass. Limit: no window or command offers the rebind, so a moved project rejoins its cycle only by moving back ([[BPR-1279]], Backlog). Nothing rebinds silently |

No RC54 row asks for the owner's judgment. When [[BPR-1284]] is Done,
promote BPR-1187, record its RC54-03 result, rerun the documentation gate and
close the RFC. The nine passing rows need no rerun unless their inputs change.
BPR-1188 to BPR-1194 wait on BPR-1187 one at a time, so the closeout chain
stops here.
